# Detailed changes since 5debfb7

This is the engineering record for the Pool of Radiance port, a Turbo Pascal 6 reconstruction of SSI's version 1.3. For a shorter, player-facing overview, start with the [changelog](changelog.md). This document is the place to look when you need the exact behaviour, the affected routine, or the issue and commit behind a change.

The main range begins after commit 5debfb7 (2026-10-10) and runs through 3b52671, the head of `fix/issues-670-672` on 2026-10-17. It contains 238 non-merge commits from 54 merged pull requests and one then-open pull request, #673, which holds the final three commits for issues #670, #671, and #672. Later work such as #681 and #685 appears in the relevant sections without commit hashes. The commit messages remain the source of truth; the notes here explain them in context.

Rather than walking through commits in date order, the document groups related work by area. Each entry starts with the practical result, then explains the original problem and the implementation where that detail is useful. Issue numbers, SSI quirk numbers, and short commit hashes appear at the end. When the port intentionally behaves differently from SSI's version 1.3, the entry calls that out as "Differs from SSI." Several commits may share one entry when they are all part of the same change.

The areas are:

1. New features
2. Gameplay and rules fixes
3. SSI quirks fixed (and the ones labelled)
4. Combat and the combat AI
5. User interface: menus, screens, keys and messages
6. Saving, loading, quick save and the vault
7. Data fixes to the shipped scripts and items (DataPat)
8. Video (the driver refactor and VGA), memory, overlays and EMS
9. Robustness guards from the source review
10. Refactor phases 0 to 6 (structure, units, tests, sizes)
11. Tools, CI, tests and regression scenarios
12. Documentation

---

## 1. New features

These are the additions players will notice most. The entries also cover the supporting file formats, menu work, and compatibility details behind them.

**Paladins and rangers can be created.** The earlier #209 work gave both classes their tables, hit points, warrior rules and special abilities, but character creation could not make them. Humans can now be paladins and rangers, and half-elves rangers and cleric/rangers, in Curse of the Azure Bonds' order; the half-elf row of `classesByRace` grows to 13 classes, and the class menu guards its count and pick. Adding a character follows Curse's party rules: a paladin does not join a party with an evil member, an evil character does not join a party with a paladin, and a party has at most three rangers. A refused character gets Curse's message on the bottom line, which waits for a key, and stays on the roster; as in Curse, the first character joins unchecked and scripted NPC joins are not checked. The race screen shows the new classes with the rule book's maximums (U for a human paladin or ranger, 8 for a half-elf ranger, CL/RA among the half-elf multiclasses), and its multiclass column may run on beside the note at the foot of the box. Differs from SSI: SSI's Pool offered neither class at creation. #209. 11a5399. A related guard: paladin and ranger share usability bit 6, and the one place that builds `itemLimits` summed class bits with `Inc`, which would carry into bit 7 for a class holding both; the bits are now or-ed (no class combines them, so nothing changes). #445. 1683b84.

**Settings screen, saved globally in POOLOPT.CFG.** One screen for the game's settings, in the list style of the Journal and race screens, as designed in #549. It opens from the start menu (Game Settings) and from Alter in game (Settings), replacing Alter's Prefs and Speed. Up and Down pick a setting; Enter, Left or Right turn it over; the speed line offers Slower and Faster, chosen with Left and Right and applied with Enter. The settings are game speed, monster pictures, character portraits, music, sound, Auto End Quick Mode (#338's behaviour, now a setting) and Quick Mode Magic (the Alt-M state each fight starts with; off by default, as in SSI's game). They are global: POOLOPT.CFG in the save folder keeps them as KEY=VALUE lines, read in GameInit before the title music and written back on every change (also by Ctrl-S and the combat speed menu). Unknown keys are ignored and kept, missing or bad ones take the defaults, and if the file cannot be written a change holds for the session. Saves still write SSI's copies of speed and the settings word, but loading a save no longer changes the settings. Below the settings the screen shows, for display only, what the game runs with from POOL.CFG (video, sound device, data and save paths), the build version and commit, the data archive version and how many families read their merged archive, and free memory and the largest free block; NORAND and STING show at the right of the last rows when given. `build.sh` writes `obj/BUILDID.INC` from git for the version and commit. Differs from SSI: settings were per saved game. #549. efccdbf.

**The game version string and the start menu title.** The owner's batch "Updates from BC." added `GDConsts.GAME_VERSION`, the build version from `BUILDID.INC` followed by " (BC)", shown centred on the credits screen (in place of the fixed "version 1.3") and as the Settings screen's Version line. The start menu's prompt, "Choose a function", is now "pool of radiance" and the version, in magenta. The same batch added the Brazen Crows picture (RAVFULL.DAX) at the start of the introduction (see section 5 for the later change that skips it when the file is missing). 038f32e.

**View: Heal and Cure on the main menu, More Info under the portrait.** A paladin's Heal and Cure were reachable only from the More page. As in Curse, they are now on the View menu itself while each can be used (Items Spells Trade Drop Heal Cure Exit). The menu line drops its "View:" prompt, as Curse's does, since a paladin with items and money would not otherwise fit in 40 columns. More moves off the menu line to a "More Info" label under the portrait frame (column 29, row 13, the M in white and the rest in green), drawn whether or not a portrait is shown; M opens the More Info page, now the saving throws alone with an Exit menu. `MenuCore` gains `extraKeys`, keys a caller takes although the menu line does not show them, returned upper-cased without changing the current option and cleared on every return. #592. 2a9448f.

**Full-screen Load/Save menu.** See section 6 (#604, #652, #656, #657).

**Quick save (F5) and quick load (F8).** See section 6 (#605).

**The vault: The Hoarder's Respite.** See section 6 and section 7 (#606).

**Stojanow Gate after it is taken; Dirten's temple.** Data fixes with a temple mode in Pascal; see section 7 (#683, #682).

**Statistics per character and per game.** A new resident unit, `Stats`, keeps for each party member and for the game: kills, in all and by kind (the creature's name; past 47 kinds the rest go under OTHER), damage done and taken, healing given and taken, times raised, times poisoned, items and coin looted (coin by its worth in copper, shown in gold), spells cast, times knocked down and times died. Counters are LongInts that stop at 2,147,483,647 rather than wrap. The unit is not overlaid, so counting a blow in combat loads no overlay. Credit goes to the creature acting (`curChar`): in combat a party member's blow on anyone outside the party is damage done, and its fall is a kill; healing given counts while a spell or an item's spell is resolved and for a paladin's laying on of hands; healing taken counts every hit point a party member gets back; coin and items count when taken from the loot after a fight, not from the vault. A party member's block is on the heap, found by record and name as the usual spells are, and freed with it; it is written to `<character>.sts` beside the character's file and read back with it, and the game's totals go to `savgam<x>.sts`. Older files start from nothing. The 1 KB file record is static, not on the stack. #611. 2f26748. Coin looted was then made net: the post-combat loot menu notes each member's coin before every action (Take, Pool, Share, Drop in View) and adds the difference after, so pooling the party's own money and taking it back no longer counts as loot; a character's figure can go below zero. Shops, temples and the vault do not count. #611. 7edfa4f.

**The Stats screen: a Game page and a Kills page.** The first version (2f26748) put Stats on More Info, showing the character's counters with its ten most-killed kinds, and a Game page with the party's totals. The screen is now remodelled: Stats opens on Game, the character's twelve counters in this game, and Kills lists the kinds it has killed in alphabetical order, the count right-aligned before each name, in two columns; Up and Down scroll a row and, past a page, Next and Prev turn it, with "(more)" above or below. The bottom line reads Stats: Game Kills Exit. The party-wide totals page is gone from the screen; the game's totals are still kept and saved. #672 (open PR #673). 3b52671.

**More Info: Saves, Stats, Kills and Thief.** More Info, to the owner's design, now opens on its Saves page with "More Info: Saves sTats Kills tHief" on the bottom line in place of Stats and Exit. The character's name is centred in light cyan, "Saving Throws" is white, each saving throw's name light green and its number white. sTats and Kills open the statistics' Game and Kills pages (`StatsPage` takes the page to open on), and their Exit returns to the More Info page left; Escape leaves More Info for the character sheet, since the design has no Exit. Thief, offered only to a character with thief levels (single or multi-class), lists the eight thief skills in light green with four white columns, Race, Dex, Level and Total: the racial and Dexterity adjustments (Dexterity only for the first five), the base for the thief level (level 9's above 9) and the skill the game uses, signed, as in "+ 0". The total is the stored `thiefSkills` value, so a skill stopped at 0 shows 0 although its parts sum lower. The figures are percentages but carry no % sign, which the game's font does not have, and the 36-column window takes the design's columns one space apart. `ClassCal.ThiefSkillParts` gives a skill's three parts and `ClassCal.ThiefSkill` the skill they make; `RecalcThiefSkills` and the page share them, and `TClasCal` checks both. The saving throws' and skills' names are string literals in the overlay rather than a typed constant, which frees 126 bytes of the data segment. Differs from SSI: SSI shows thief skills nowhere. #685. Then restructured to the owner's later design: More Info's menu is "More Info: Saves sTats Kills tHief Exit" (E now Exit), and Stats and Kills are its own pages instead of the statistics screen with its Game Kills Exit submenu, which is gone (`StatsPage` and `StatsHeading` are removed; `MorePage` turns all five pages). Each page centres the name in light cyan on row 2, then a centred white title on row 4 (Saving Throws, Game Stats, Kill Count; the saving throws' names lose their indent) and starts on row 6, so Kill Count shows 30 kinds a page (15 rows of two) where it showed 32. On Kill Count, Up and Down scroll a row and Page Up and Page Down a page; there is no room for Next and Prev on the menu line. The statistics block is still read only when Stats or Kills is first chosen (`ReadStats`), since reading it makes one, which a save then writes. Game Stats' names are light green and its counts white, as Saving Throws' are, and Kill Count's counts are white before the light green names. #685.

**More Info: the Cleric page.** `ViewChar.MorePage` offers Cler (C) to a character with a turn level (`ClassCal.TurnLevel` above 0), and `DrawCleric` draws it to the owner's design: the name and the white title Turn Undead as on the other pages, Cleric Level in light green with the level in white, the white headings Undead and Result, then the ten kinds of undead in light green with each result in white, right-aligned: D where any roll destroys (a table value of 0 or -1), T where any roll turns (1), the least d20 roll otherwise, and a dash where the level cannot turn (99). A key to D, T and # is in light green on rows 20 to 22; the rows run one lower than the design's, as every More Info page's do, under the name on row 2. The prompt becomes "Info:", as the designs have it, since "More Info: Saves sTats Kills tHief Cler Exit" is 44 columns. The turning table and the turn level move from `CbtActs` to `ClassCal` (`TurnValue`, `TurnLevel`; `TURN_UNDEAD_KINDS`, `TURN_IMMUNE`), so combat and the page read one table: `CombatTurnUndead` calls `TurnValue`, and `Combat` and `CombatAi` call `ClassCal.TurnLevel`. The turn level is the usable cleric level (`CharHlp.GetClassLevel`, so a dual-classed human's resting cleric levels give none, #838) or the paladin level less 2, whichever is higher, so a paladin of level 3 and up has the page. TClasCal checks the table's columns (1..8, 9..13, 14 and up), the rows above 10 and the turn level of a fighter, cleric, paladins and a dual-classed human. The design is mocks/info-cleric.txt. #904.

**The Bestiary (F2).** What the party has learned of each kind of creature it has met, keyed by the name the player sees, as `Stats` keys its kills. Nothing is read from MONCHA.DAX: each fact goes in when the rule behind it acts with a party member on the other side. The resident unit `Bestiary` keeps a 32-byte entry per kind (name, 64 fact bits in `C_BEAST`, the armor class bounds, hit dice, the most damage in a blow, seen and killed) in a heap table grown 16 entries at a time while `MaxAvail` allows. `Met` counts a meeting once an encounter (EclVm's LOAD MONSTER and `CombatSt.CombatStart`, so a meeting that does not fight counts; `NewEncounter` at CLEAR MONSTERS, `ForgetMonsters` and `PostCombat`); `Killed`, from `Stats.Damaged`, counts the party's kills and records the hit dice (`levelHighest`); `LearnBlow`, from `CbtActs.ProcessAttackResult`, keeps the most damage a monster's hit did (slaying the helpless aside); `LearnSize` notes Large or not at `DoAttack`'s large-dice swap; `LearnArmor` narrows the armor class from each frontal roll (not a backstab or from behind, a natural 1 or 20, or an attack displaced): the roll plus `FxEngine.HitBonus` (split out of `AttackRoll`) less the range's penalty, a contradiction starting that side over. In `EffHndlr`, `HalveDamage` notes half damage in the half-damage handlers, `NoteNullified` and `NoteImmunity` note an immunity only when `NullifyAttack` cleared something, `MagicResistance` takes the creature and notes a resisted effect, and the touches, gazes, drains, rot, regeneration and `EffNeedMagic` note theirs. The knowledge is the player's: the overlaid `BeastFil` keeps it in BESTIARY.DAT in the save folder (a header of signature, version, entry size and count, then the entries), read in `GameInit2` and written after each fight, after a save and on the way out to DOS (`GameIo.beforeExit`); a missing file is empty, a damaged one reads as empty and becomes BESTIARY.BAD at the next write, and a newer format's is read and never written. `BeastMnu` draws the list (two columns by name, View Next Prev Exit, the arrows moving the highlight) and the entry page (?? in yellow for what is unknown; the armor class as a range until one value is left); F2 (`C_KEYS.F2`) opens it from `Explore.MapMainMenu` and `Camp.CampMainMenu`, whatever the Companion Tools setting. `FileUtil.RenameChecked` is now public. TBeast tests the table, the narrowing and the file. A sage who fills in entries for a fee is #868. #842.

**The sage in New Phlan, the Slums' fortune teller.** DataPat fixes to three blocks. New Phlan's map (GEO3 block 0) walls (15,12), the dead end of the east tavern entered at (15,14), off from the tavern on the south and gives it a door on the west from the lane at (14,12), open at every hour (wall type 10, the tavern's own door; barrier 1), each wall set on both sides. In the Slums' script (ECL2 block 20) the fortune teller's murder at (3,5), which SSI marked only in the Slums' own [$4A0B] (cleared when the next area's script loads), goes through new code at the end of the script ($B6FD) that sets `C_GBVM.GYPSY_KILLED` ($4A53, a cell no script used) and goes on to the continue prompt. New Phlan's script (ECL3 block 0) sends event 20's event-table entry to new code at its end ($B66F, after #864's), which passes every other event-20 square to #606's vault code as before; at (15,12) it greets the party once a visit as the shops do (`C_GBVM.PHLAN_GREETED`); until the Slums are cleared (`C_GBVM.REWARD_SLUMS` at `QUEST_DONE` or past it) with `GYPSY_KILLED` 0 it prints "A BARE, RICKETY SHOP.", with the shattered crystal ball strewn across the floor when she was murdered; then it refuses it after a fight with the watch (`C_GBVM.WATCH_FOUGHT`), shows her as the Slums do (head $19 on body $0D) and runs PROGRAM 10, `EclVm.PROG_SAGE`; `C_DATA.VERSION` is 39. The new overlaid unit `Sage` runs it: Ask picks a creature from the Bestiary's list page (`BeastMnu.PickBeast`; `BestiaryMenu` and it share `ListPages`), `FindRecord` finds its record by name in the merged MONCHA.DAX (traced back to a disk and id with `DaxIo.RemapId`, now public) or else in MON1CHA to MON8CHA, scanning each archive once (`DaxIo.ReadBlock`, split out of `LoadDax`), and `EclMenu.InitCreature` loads it. `MoneyCal.SageFee` asks 50 gp a hit die, at least 100, paid with `Money.PayGold`; `BeastFil.Tell` then sets size, hit dice, the exact armor class and the facts the record's effects stand for (`C_BEAST.EFFECT_FACTS`, Pool's effect ids), leaving seen, killed and the worst blow alone, and marks the entry `C_BEAST.TOLD` (bit 20), so `BeastMnu.DrawGroup` leaves the ?? off its defenses and attacks; `BeastMnu.ShowEntry` shows the entry. An entry Tell would not change costs nothing and is marked told; a name with no record gets "I have heard of no such thing". TBeast and TMoneyCa test Tell, the told mark, the table and the fee; TDataPat checks the three jumps (its #864 check is now `FindJump`, shared). #868.

**The Bestiary's missing facts, and the rest of the game's creatures.** An effect with no fact in `C_BEAST` never showed, in play or from the sage. Every creature record (MON*CHA with its MON*SPC effects, the effects its readied items grant, and the displacer beast's #1136 displacement, which #1183 took away again) was checked against what the Bestiary can show, and seventeen facts were added in bits 40..56, with bit 38, Pools of Darkness's "Burning touch", for Tyranthraxus's fire touch (the same creature in both games). The defenses: `SILVER_OR_MAGIC` (effs 96 and 123, learned in `NoHarmWithoutSilver` when a party member's blow that would have hurt does nothing), `HALF_SILVER` (123, through `HalveDamage`), `BLINKS` (37), `DISPLACED` (89), `FIGHTS_ON` (99, 95), `GAS_ESCAPE` (103), `DODGES_MISSILES` (104) and `DODGES_BOULDERS` (120, both through `AvoidRanged`, which now takes the fact), `RESISTS_FIRE` (61, 113, `NoteFireResisted`), `SEES_INVISIBLE` (24, learned in `EffInvisible` when the attacker sees the invisible party member) and `TURNS_INVISIBLE` (72); the attacks: `ATK_FEAR` (82, 81), `ATK_BLOOD` (76, 75), `ATK_ACID` (80, 121), `ATK_BREATH` (88, in `Magic.DragonBreathHandler`), `ATK_FIRE` (79), `ATK_RAKE` (73, in `CbtActs.AttackClawRake`) and `ATK_HOLD` (77, 74). `EFFECT_FACTS` maps each for `BeastFil.Tell`. Holy water, the mummy's and the troll's weakness to fire, and the mirror against a gaze are weaknesses, not defenses, and are left out. With defense bits in both words, the entry page's lists are ordered lists, as Pools does it: `BeastFil.FactAt`, `FactName` (moved from `BeastMnu`) and `KnownCount`. `C_BEAST` drops the bit ranges and names Pools' own bits (21..25, 39) so none is given twice. #1110, #1122, #1142.

**None under an empty list.** A told entry's empty list shows None, and `BeastFil.ListRows` counts that row when `BeastMnu.DrawEntry` chooses one column or two: the told juju zombie (ten defenses, no attack) filled the thirteen rows exactly, so Attacks was the last row with nothing under it. It now takes two columns. In two columns, a told list with an odd number of facts no longer has the next heading right under its last fact: the blank line between them is kept, as Pools does. TBeast tests the row count. #1114.

**STING debug tools from Curse.** From Curse of the Azure Bonds' debug mode, under Pool's STING parameter (Curse asks for a second word; Pool keeps the one): Ctrl-L turns the script trace log on and off (debug.txt gets each opcode's mnemonic as it runs, from the new `EclTrace` unit with Curse's `OpName` for opcodes $00..$3D, SUBTRAT included, and the status line shows '*'; Curse's key was Ctrl-D, which here stays the hit-point pop-up); Ctrl-Z, while the log is open, writes each creature's effects to it; V in the Items menu (not shown on the menu line) shows the selected item's raw record (Curse's ViewItemDetail); A on the 3D menu maps anywhere, ignoring the area's no-automap flag; J turns free training on and off, as in Curse, where Pool had turned it on for good. Ctrl-Y shows the overlay load count, buffer and probation sizes, and the heap's free memory and largest block on the bottom line, then redraws the menu line (`GDVars.bottomLineTaken`). `MenuVertical` now passes its caller's `extraKeys` to every key it waits for, not only the first. #612. 5226426. Later additions to Ctrl-Y (picture memory, EMS) are in section 8.

**Camp's Fix rest explains why it does nothing.** In the owner's batch, `CampFixMenu` now tells a party with no cleric "No clerics available to heal.", one whose clerics are all unable to cast "No cleric is in a condition to heal.", and, when nothing can be treated, "Further healing would do no good." (nothing to treat) or "Further healing isn't possible." (conditions remain but no slot can treat them), instead of returning silently. 038f32e.

**Journal pages show "(more)".** In the owner's batch, the in-game Journal's entry pane draws "(more)", centred, on its top row when a page comes before and on its bottom row when one follows; pages are counted accordingly (a page holds the full rows alone, one fewer first or last, two fewer between). 038f32e.

**The Journal lists what the party has found.** The new resident unit `JrnlSeen` keeps a mark for each of the Journal's 99 items, cleared for a new game (`GameInit.ResetGameState`) and saved beside each save as `savgam<x>.jnl` (`SaveLoad.SaveToSlot`/`LoadGame`, `SaveMenu.SlotSet`; `FileUtil.SET_FILES` 55). SSI's journal was a printed book, so the scripts only print which entry to read; `EclVm.EclPrint` passes every printed line to `JrnlSeen.Scan`, which marks "ENTRY n", "ENTRIES n AND m" and "(ENTRY n)" (a word followed by digits only, so "AT YOUR ENTRY," and "ENTRY TO" mark nothing), takes the number from the next line after a line ending in "ENTRY " (the Kobold Caves' subroutine at block 13 $B512, whose callers print 42., 28., 16., 20.) or "TAVERN TALE " (New Phlan's tavern, block 0 $A413, which prints the rolled 1 to 23), and marks every proclamation posted by then (`JrnlQst.IsPosted`) on City Hall's "PROCLAMATIONS ARE POSTED ON THE WALLS" line. Reading the text follows DataPat's changed scripts with no address table. A scan of every packed string in ECL1 to ECL8, DataPat applied, finds 36 entries named (3, 7, 8, 10, 11, 14, 16, 19-21, 23, 25-31, 33, 35, 37, 38, 40-43, 46-51, 53, 55-57) and none of the other 22 (1, 2, 4-6, 9, 12, 13, 15, 17, 18, 22, 24, 32, 34, 36, 39, 44, 45, 52, 54, 58), which the game never hands out (by their text, the book's decoys); nor are CXX, CLVI, CLXX and CXC ever posted. `JrnlView` lists only the found items (`JrnlSeen.Listed`), its highlight and paging walking that list; an empty list shows "Your journal is empty." and offers Quests Exit. The setting Journal (key JOURNAL, FOUND or ALL, default FOUND; `GameVars.journalFoundOn`) on the Game page, after Auto Join, lists every item when ALL. A save without a `.jnl` file (or a damaged one) loads with every item found. The Area view's three tests of the zone's noAutomap (Explore's A, `MapView`'s redraw, `CharDisp.PrintMiscInfo`'s coordinates) are now `JrnlSeen.AreaMapAllowed`, also true where the party has found the Journal's map of the area: entry 10 for the Textile House (script 2, where the script turns the view off after the party flees the hobgoblins), 26 for Sorcerer's Island's maze (22), 29 for Valjevo Castle NE (4), 41 for Valjevo Castle NW (script 3, map 3) and 42 for the Kobold Caves (13). Entry 37's atlas draws Phlan's blocks and the lands around it, not a block's streets, and 28 is a wilderness trail, so neither opens a block. Tests in TJrnlSn and TCfgFile. #1090.

**The Journal's Quests page.** The Journal's Quests page (#852) lists the missions the party has been given, each with DONE or ---- (`JrnlView.ShowQuests`), a page at a time with Next and Prev, under the bottom line JOURNAL: ENTRIES QUESTS EXIT: Entries (E) turns back to the Journal's list, Quests stays, and Exit, which has no key, or Escape leaves the Journal. The list is only read; the first version's Select, which turned back to the Journal on a quest's proclamation, confused what was happening and is gone (#874). The new overlaid unit `JrnlQst` holds 21 quests, worked out from the merged ECL.DAX (gbt ecl disasm), City Hall's script (block 8) and the Clue Book. The quests are the six blocks LIX pays for (the Slums, Kuto's Well, Podol Plaza, the Textile House, Mendor's Library, the Wealthy Area), the quests of CI, CXIV, CXXVI, CXXIX, CXXXIV, CLIV, CCI, CCIV and CCXIV, and the auction, Bishop Braccio's temple, Cadorna's Zhentil Keep message, the order to kill Cadorna, Stojanow Gate and Valjevo Castle. `IsDone` reads each mission's cell: the script that ends a mission writes $FE (C_GBVM.QUEST_DONE), and the clerk writes $FF as she pays. Two quests need more than that. The library needs both its fights won (`LIBRARY_FIGHTS`). A Bivant heir left behind is told apart from a rescued one by the Buccaneer Base's own cell (`BUCCANEER_BASE`). `IsGiven` is true once the proclamation is posted outside City Hall, which follows how many major rewards the clerk has paid (`IsPosted`, `COUNCIL_REWARDS`). It is also true once the scripts record the offer, the acceptance or the summons, or once the mission is done. Left out: LXIV, LXXVIII and CIX, which nothing records; CXX, CLVI, CLXX and CXC, which are never posted; and Cadorna's family treasure, where a treasure kept from him ends in the same state as one returned. SSI's ninth posted proclamation says CXIV where it means CCXIV (POR-0209, #864); `IsPosted` follows what was meant. C_GBVM names every cell read; TJrnlQst tests them. #851.

**Exit to DOS without saving.** The main menu's and the training hall's Exit to DOS ran the save menu until a save was made, so Escape could not leave it. A party not saved since it changed is now asked "Save first": Yes opens the save menu (leaving it unsaved stays in the menu), No quits without saving. #660. 09aa25f.

**A destroyed party returns to the main menu.** After "PARTY HAS BEEN DESTROYED" the program exited to DOS. It now frees the party (`SaveLoad.FreeParty`, shared with QuickLoad), forgets the session vault, resets the game (`GameInit.ResetGameState`, split out of `GameInit2`, as in Curse) and returns to the main menu. Differs from SSI. #658. 5d4e0cc.

**Out of memory, the game offers a save.** `HeapErr`'s save ran only while `heapFailureSaveEnabled` was nonzero, and nothing ever set it (in SSI's v1.3 either), so a failed allocation always quit to DOS unsaved; had it been set, a save that could not finish would have looped. The handler now frees a 4 KB reserve that `GameInit2` takes once the overlay buffer is set up (enough for the vault's copy buffer, a new statistics block and menu nodes), then asks on the bottom line: Enter opens the save menu, Escape quits. A save backed out of or failed asks again; a failure inside the handler returns 0, the runtime error the game had before, rather than re-entering. The flag is gone. Not exercised by any scenario. POR-0168, #643. e9e0acb.

**Humans change class (dual-classing).** As the 1st-edition PHB allows (p.33) and Curse does at its training hall, a human of one class may leave it for another: Dual Class (U) on the party menu opens `ChgClass.ChangeClassMenu` (mocks/change-class.txt). Leaving needs 15 in each of the class's prime requisites and taking one 17 in each of the new class's (`ClassCal.primeRequisites`, the Curse port's), with the new class's minimums and an alignment it allows; humans may leave and take paladins and rangers, as Curse's race table lets them. No level is needed (Curse, Secret and Pools ask none). The change costs 2500 gp and needs a hall that trains the new class (`C_GBVM.TRAIN_CLASS_MASK`). `ClassCal.ChangeClass` keeps the old level, starts the new class at 1 with no experience, forfeits drained levels (as Pools does), empties the spell slots and memorized spells and sets up a new caster as creation does (`StartCasterClass`, now shared with creation). The classes left are a mask in `TCharRecord.portExt.formerClasses`; `CharHlp.GetClassLevel(creature, mask)` gives the level a character can use in a class mask (`C_LEVEL.*_BIT`), and a class left counts only once the class followed passes it (`ClassResting`). THAC0, saves, slots, thief skills, attacks, item limits (`ClassCal.ItemLimitsFor`), turning, backstab, the ranger's bonus, the paladin's powers, caster levels, scroll reading, scribing and the door checks ask it; `classLevels` keeps the real levels for training, the screens and the scripts (a ranger in the party counts by its levels), and `CHAR_LEVEL` reads the class followed (`CharHlp.CurrentClassLevel`). `ClassCal.HitDice` gives each class its dice in the order taken, which `HpMinimum`, `HpMaximum` and `ConHpTotal` use, undivided; `GainLevels` adds hit points only past the old class, `TrainingMenu`, `CanTrain` and the Companion's level editor skip the classes left, `DrainLevels` drains the class followed alone (Pools' rule), `RestorationHandler` restores it alone (Curse's), and `ChgClass.AnnounceWoken` and `SyncClassPowers` wake the old class with its paladin ward and put away readied items a resting class lent. The character screen shows the class left in brackets, dark gray while it rests. Modify Character refuses a dual-classed character, as Curse does. `portExt` follows the record's 285 saved bytes (`DiskRec` stops there and clears it on reading), so SSI's files and Curse's import are unchanged; the new `CharExt` unit keeps it in a `.chx` file beside the character's files (`FileUtil.SET_FILES` 54). #838.

**Spell information on the spell lists.** Every list `Magic.SpellSelection` runs but the show-only one (Cast in camp and combat, a scroll's spells under Use, Memorize, Scribe, Learn on gaining a level, the spells in memory) shows the highlighted spell's summary line: the list ends at row 20 and the line is on row 22, or for Memorize, whose list ends at row 15 above `Camp.DisplaySpellSlots`, on the message window's top row (17). `MenuCore.movesToCaller` returns each move of the highlight, and the new `MenuCore.moved` tells a move from a key, since Page Up's scan code is 'I'. Info (I, added to the bottom line's menu) opens the spell page, which covers the list window under its heading; Next and Prev step through the list's spells and Exit returns to the list on the spell shown last (`Magic.SpellPage`), Memorize's slots drawn again through `Magic.belowListHook`. The new overlaid unit `SpellInf` works out the figures from the routines combat uses: `CharHlp.CalcSpellRange` and `GetCasterLevel` (at `C_SPELL.ITEM_CASTER_LEVEL` for a scroll line, `GameVars.usingItem` set while it works), the new `CharHlp.SpellArea`, which now also gives the aim preview its shapes (`CbtActs.CombatCastTargetMenu`: Lightning Bolt's path, Stinking Cloud's 2x2 block, a fireball's radius 2 outdoors), the new `CharHlp.SpellDuration`, which holds the rolled durations `Magic.CalcSpellDuration` rolls and the page shows as dice (Cause Disease's 1D6 TU), and `FxEngine.AuraRange`, split out of `EffectSource` (Prayer's 6, Silence and the 10' protections' 1). Whether a save negates, halves or lessens comes from `SpellInf`'s own table, checked against the handlers. The 56 descriptions are string literals in the overlaid unit `SpellTxt`, as the journal's are, so they cost no data segment. `TSpellRec.area`'s comment, which said the high nibble was a radius, now says what the code reads. Tests in TSpelInf. #839.

**The spell page for items.** `ViewChar.UseItemMenu` offers Info (I) on an identified item whose Use casts a spell the page describes (`SpellInf.HasItemPage`), and `ViewChar.ItemInfo` opens `SpellInf.ShowItemPage`, which steps through the character's items that have one and leaves the list on the item shown last. The new `CharHlp.ItemSpell` gives the spell an item's Use casts (an effect past 56 less `C_SPELL.ITEM_EFFECT_SHIFT`, none past the spell table); `Magic.UseItem`, `Magic.UseItemSpell` and `CombatAi.SelectItemToUse` now take it from there instead of each mapping the effect. The page works an item's figures out as Use casts it (`C_SPELL.FROM_ITEM`, so at `C_SPELL.ITEM_CASTER_LEVEL`). An item spell (57 to 67) has no name in the spell table, so the item's name heads its page, over MAGIC ITEM and its caster level (`SpellInf.CasterLevelText`: 12, or the Necklace of Missiles' rolled 3, 5 or 7, `C_SPELL.ITEM_FIREBALL_DIE`); CAST BY names the item. `SpellInf`'s save table runs to 67, `SpellInf.AreaText` gives the Javelin of Lightning its 3-square bolt (`C_SPELL.JAVELIN_BOLT_CELLS`, which `Magic.JavelinOfLightningHandler` now uses too), and `SpellTxt` holds the ten item spells' descriptions, written from their handlers. `SpellInf.CastTimeText` gives AT ONCE for a spell cast from a scroll or an item, which `Magic.UseItemSpell` casts on the spot; the page had shown a scroll line the casting time of the spell from memory. The items menu's bottom line (`ViewChar.ItemMenuLine`) is rebuilt as the highlight moves (`MenuCore.movesToCaller`): Halve only on a stack of 2 or more, Join only when another stack can join it (`ViewChar.CanJoin`, sharing `ViewChar.SameStack` with `JoinItem`), Id only on an unidentified item in a shop, so it and Info never both want I, and Halve gives way when the line would pass 40 columns. `C_ITEM.CARRY_MAX` names the 16 items a character carries. On an item page a spell cast only on its user reads AREA USER, not THE CASTER (`SpellInf.ItemAreaText`), and save column 0 (paralyzation, poison and death magic) is named PARALYSIS on every page, so the Wand of Paralyzation's save reads NEGATES (VS PARALYSIS). `CbtActs.CombatCastTargetMenu` still aims the javelin at one creature, but previews and counts its 3-cell bolt as Lightning Bolt's: `Magic.BoltShape` gives a bolt spell's length and near-rebound charge to both handlers and to the preview's tracer, `Magic.TraceBolt` (was `TraceLightningBolt`), which `CbtMap.StartSpellPreview` now hands the spell. Tests in TSpelInf. #861, #891, #892.

**The combat log (F7).** A new resident unit, `CbtLog`, keeps the fight's messages, each with its colour changes inline (#1 then the colour), in a ring of 256-byte slots: under DOS one EMS page, allocated by `CbtLog.Startup` from `GameBoot` after `OvrInitEMS` (so the overlays have first claim) and read and written with EMS function 57h, Move Memory Region, which leaves the page frame's mapping to the overlay manager; an exit procedure gives the page back. Without an EMS 4.0 driver or a free page `CbtLog.Usable` is false and nothing is kept. The native build has no EMS: its ring is on the heap and always usable. The messages are caught where they are built rather than in `TextRend`, since the combat window's columns also carry the creature panel and the View screen: `CharDisp.PrintNameString` (from `COMBAT_MESSAGE_TOP` down) starts an entry with the name in `CharDisp.NameColor`, split out of `DisplayCreatureName`; `MessageText`, `PrintTwoLines` and, in combat, `PrintBottomLine` log too; `CbtActs.ProcessAttackResult` adds the defender, the result and "and is Dying"; `Magic.PrintCastSpellMsg` adds the spell's name. `CharDisp.PrintActionLine`, `PrintBottomLine` for something a creature does, logs its name first in `NameColor`: guarding (`Combat.DoneMenu`, `CombatAi.FinishRound`) logs "MORRIGAN is Guarding" where the log had only "Guarding" (#875), a blocked flight "is blocked from escaping" and an aborted spell "aborts the spell", while the bottom line keeps SSI's words. `Combat.CombatMain` clears it with the settings' size and heads each round with its number. F7 on the combat menu (`CombatMenu` answers it as `SHOW_LOG`, as its scan code is 'A''s) opens `LogView.ShowLog`, overlaid, which wraps the entries to 37 columns with `LogWrap.WrapEntry`, also overlaid, and opens at the end. Settings gains Combat Log and Log Lines (10 to 60 by fives, 50 by default; `COMBATLOG` and `LOGLINES` in POOLOPT.CFG); the speed line's Slower and Faster became a stepping line both share (`Options.StepNames`), and without a store both lines are drawn dark grey, Combat Log reads Needs EMS, and `Options.NextSetting` skips them. The log is not saved, and is not shown after the fight. Quick-mode turns are logged as the player's are; Pool's scripts do not run during a fight. Tests: `tcbtlog`. #841.
---

**The Clue Book's map keys on the Companion Tools' map.** The new overlaid unit `ClueBook` holds the 1988 Clue Book's numbered keys for every 3D area it maps, keyed by GEO map id (`zone0^.mapId`), as string literals in `Entry` so the table lives in the overlay: each key's squares as `x,y`, then its text in the book's words, cut to 30 characters, without the book's key number ("Holes", not "6. Holes", #878). The keys an area holds of another's map keep the book's name for it ("Maze: Teleporters", "Tower: False Tyranthraxus"), and the Pool of Radiance's teleporter keys keep their labels (A0, B1), by which they name one another. A key printed at several places is on each square, a number printed across a grid line on each square it straddles. `Note` gives a square's key; `Marked` answers from a 16-word bit map built once per map. The coordinates were read off the book's maps and checked against each map's GEO walls and event squares and its script; where the book draws a room a square off (the Buccaneers' Base side towers, the Cadorna pillar by 1a) the key is on the game's square, and Sorcerer's Island's C2 and C3 rooms, which the book swaps against the dial, are left out. `CompTool.DrawSquare` gives keyed squares light cyan walls under a note's yellow, the legend gains "Cyan: Clue Book" and reads "Dim: Not Visited" and "Yellow: Player Note" (#878), and the Map tool shows the key on the line below the map and the player's note on the line under it, so the two never share a field. The keys show at once, as the walls and event kinds of unvisited squares already do; the Clue Book Notes setting under Companion Tools (`GameVars.clueNotesOn`, `CLUENOTES` in POOLOPT.CFG, on by default) hides them. Nothing is saved, so saves are unchanged. `TClueBk` checks New Phlan's keys and that every entry parses, fits, shares no square and starts with no number. #856.

**POOLCFG, the set-up program; one unit for the configuration files.** The new overlaid unit `CfgFile` reads and writes POOL.CFG and POOLOPT.CFG for the game and for the new program `POOLCFG.EXE`, with its constants in the new `C_OPT`. `Options`' key table, labels, parsing, steps and safe write moved there (`KeyName`, `SettingName`, `SettingLabel`, `StepNames`, `NextSetting`, `ReadValue`, `ReadOptions`, `WriteOptions`, `ChangeSetting`), the settings live in a `TSettings` record, and `Options.Collect` and `Apply` are the one place it meets `GameVars`; `GameInit` sets the defaults with `CfgFile.Defaults`, and `CbtLog`'s ring sizes are `C_OPT`'s. `GameBoot` reads POOL.CFG with `CfgFile.ReadPoolCfg`, which takes a letter in either case (POR-0211, #895) and reads S as silent and B as the Sound Blaster, as SSI's data card and the Curse port have them (#880); `NormalizePath` moved to `CfgFile`, its Halt staying in `GameBoot`. Reading POOL.CFG now loads an overlay before the buffer is resized, so `OvrClearBuf` comes before `OvrSetBuf`. With no POOL.CFG, or one `ReadPoolCfg` finds damaged (it would not open, or a line holds a value START does not know), `GameBoot.RunSetup` cuts START's DOS memory block back to the empty heap (INT 21h AH=4Ah), runs POOLCFG.EXE with `Exec` and `C_OPT.SETUP_FROM_START` and gives the block back, and START carries on with the file it wrote, or halts to DOS when POOLCFG's exit code says it left unsaved; with that switch POOLCFG's notice for a damaged file opens with "POOL.CFG was damaged, unreadable, or unparseable. Please reconfigure the game."; without the program the prompt reads "Run POOLCFG to set up the game, or insert the startup disk." POOLCFG is an 80x25 text program on the new `TuiKit` (direct text-screen writes, boxes, a message, a Yes/No/Cancel question, a pick list and a line editor whose keys are the screenless `LineEdit`): a Main Menu with a Files box, Hardware (video, sound, introduction), Game Settings (the Settings screen's list with an About box) and Paths (the four games' folders). `CfgFile.CheckPath` checks each path as it is entered and creates nothing ("Please verify the directory exists."); untouched paths are kept even when missing, and F5 restores a line's default. Saving writes POOLOPT.CFG into the save folder being written, then POOL.CFG (CR LF always), each through `FileUtil`'s temporary copy; a POOL.CFG that could not be read is never written over; the exit code is 1 when it left changes unsaved or a write failed. `build.bat` builds it after the game when the source has `poolcfg.pas` (SSI's tags have none), `build.sh` checks, reports and installs it, and `package.sh` ships it with a README.TXT paragraph. Tests: `TCfgFile`, `TLinEdit`. The native tool is #894. Resident code +416 bytes, data -228; POOLCFG.EXE 37,136 bytes. #876.

**POOLCFG for the native ports.** `build-native.sh` builds `poolcfg` (`poolcfg.exe`, a console program without `-WG`, on Windows) beside `poolrad` for every target, first, since it needs no SDL; on a Mac it stays beside the app. It is the same `poolcfg.pas`, `CfgFile`, `LineEdit` and `C_OPT`: `TuiKit` writes its cells through one pointer, the text screen's memory under DOS and natively a buffer of its own that `Flush` copies into Free Pascal's `Video` unit (centred in a bigger terminal, cut in a smaller one) before each key; its native `Init`, `Done`, cursor and `GetKey` stand behind `{$IFDEF FPC}`, with no `Crt` (src/native/crt.pas is the game's SDL one). On Windows the keys come from the `Keyboard` unit; on Linux and macOS `GetKey` puts the terminal in raw mode and decodes its escape sequences itself (`ReadByte`, `SeqKey`), as the `Keyboard` unit takes a lone Escape for an Alt prefix and needs it pressed twice. Natively POOLCFG enters the game's folder with `NatFile.EnterGameFolder`, whose `HoldsGame` now also takes a folder holding GEO1.DAX, so a game with no POOL.CFG is found too; the data path is that folder, shown and not edited, the save path must lie inside it (`InGameFolder`), both taken as the native game takes them with the new `CfgFile.SaveFolderIn`, which `GameBoot.NativeFolders` now calls; the Hardware page says the video is always VGA and Tandy, AdLib and Sound Blaster are all Digital Audio, and its notices name POOLRAD for START. `CfgFile.NormalizePath` natively takes '/' as a separator and keeps a path from the root whole on Linux and macOS, and `CheckPath` keeps a native path's case. POOL.CFG's defaults are the new `CfgFile.PoolDefaults` (VGA, the full introduction, SAVE\ under the folder; silent under DOS and Digital Audio natively), used by POOLCFG and, natively, by `GameBoot.ReadConfig`, which with no POOL.CFG or an unreadable one now plays on them and writes one line to the error output, where before `DaxIo.WaitForFile` asked for the startup disk; the native game never runs POOLCFG. Tests: `TCfgFile` (`PoolDefaults`, the native separators, `SaveFolderIn`). Resident code and data unchanged, overlays +9 bytes; POOLCFG.EXE 37,248 bytes. #894.

**The native game finds a GOG or Steam copy itself.** The new native unit `GameDirs` replaces `NatFile.EnterGameFolder`. `Settle` finds the data folder without asking: `--data-folder PATH` (remembered), then beside the program as before (POOLRAD_DIR, the current folder, the program's, the folder its macOS app is in; `HoldsGame`, moved from `NatFile`), then the folder remembered in the player's own folder (`Remembered`, `Remember`: datafolder.txt), then the stores' folders when exactly one holds GEO1.DAX (`HoldsData`). `Candidates` builds the store folders from a `TSearchRoots` (`DefaultRoots` per system: Steam's own folders, with every library `ParseLibraryFolders` reads from steamapps/libraryfolders.vdf, current and older forms, VDF escapes undone; on Windows Steam's `SteamPath` and GOG's `HKLM\SOFTWARE\WOW6432Node\GOG.com\Games\1432640961` `path` from the registry, and subfolders of GOG's default folders that hold goggame-1432640961.info; ~/GOG Games/* and /opt/GOG Games/*; Pool of Radiance.app in /Applications and ~/Applications; Heroic's ~/Games/Heroic), and `Found` keeps the ones holding the data, each folder once (`SameFolder`, by inode on Linux and macOS). Each store's layout is a `TStoreGame` (`POOL`), so the later games need their own entry only. The new native unit `DataPick` asks when `Settle` cannot settle, in the game's window in the BIOS's 40-column text before anything of the game is loaded: the copies found with Another Folder, or `SDL_ShowOpenFolderDialog` (bound in `Sdl3Min`, given the new `SdlHost.GameWindow`) and, where it reports an error, a typed path; Escape, or cancelling with nothing found, quits with a message; `--choose-data-folder` asks even with a copy found. `GameDirs.Place` makes the writable folder the data folder only when the data is beside the program and `MustNotWrite` allows (not inside a macOS app bundle, and a file can be made there); otherwise it is `UserFolder` (macOS ~/Library/Application Support/Pool of Radiance/, Windows %APPDATA%\Pool of Radiance\, Linux $XDG_DATA_HOME or ~/.local/share/pool-of-radiance/). Natively the new `DaxIo.FolderOf` puts DataBld's own files (a merged archive, its .$$$ copy, DATABLD.LOG) in the writable folder, and `DataBld.GamePath`, `DaxIo.FamilyUsable` and `LoadDax`, `Sage`, `EclVm.EclNewEcl` and `BldShow` use it, so later starts find the merged archives there; `GameBoot.ReadConfig` reads POOL.CFG from the writable folder before the data folder (`GameDirs.ReadName`); `CfgFile.NativeSaveFolder`, replacing `SaveFolderIn`, places the saves (and so POOLOPT.CFG, BESTIARY.DAT and the vault) in the writable folder, or in any existing folder POOL.CFG names by a whole path; `Intro` and `SfxFile` read RAVFULL.DAX and SBSFX.DAT from beside the program first (`GameDirs.OwnFolder`). Native POOLCFG finds the folders as the game does without asking (the first copy when there are several); its data path is editable, takes only a folder holding GEO1.DAX and is remembered on saving; POOL.CFG is read and written in the writable folder; the save path may be any existing folder. The macOS release zip carries tools/macos-readme.txt as README.TXT. Every change is under `{$IFDEF FPC}`: DOS sizes unchanged. Tests: `TGameDir` (the VDF parser, the candidates from folders built as each store lays them out, `Found`, the per-user folders and the bundle and write checks), `TCfgFile` (`NativeSaveFolder`). #1064.

**Auto Join, and Settings in Game and Sound pages.** Each bundle of arrows or darts bought was an item of its own, and a character carries 16 (`C_ITEM.CARRY_MAX`), so buying ammunition meant joining by hand every few bundles. The new Auto Join setting (`C_OPT.OPT_AUTO_JOIN`, POOLOPT.CFG `AUTOJOIN`, `GameVars.autoJoinOn`, on by default) has a stackable item join the first of the character's stacks it matches as it is bought, taken after a fight, given in character editing (all through `Shop.GiveItemCopyToCreature`) or traded: `CharHlp.AddToInventory` appends the copy and joins it, so the stack already there, readied or not, is the one that grows. The stack rule and the join moved from ViewChar to CharHlp (`SameStack`, `JoinStacks`, which now takes the creature; the item menu's Join calls it) unchanged: a stack fills to 255 and the rest stays a stack of its own. `ViewChar.CannotCarryItem` lets a character at 16 items take one that joins a stack whole (`CharHlp.JoinsWhole`); with the setting off, every item needs its slot as before. The settings no longer fit one page, so the Settings screen splits them over Game and Sound (`Game Sound Paths sYstem Exit`, the music and the sound effects on Sound, `CfgFile.SoundSetting` and `SettingLine` placing each line for both screens), and POOLCFG's Main Menu gains Sound Settings (its key O, Save and Exit keeping S). The settings are renumbered, Auto Join after the portraits; POOLOPT.CFG goes by key name, so old files read as before. `TAutoJn` covers the joining, the cap, the readied mark and the room check, and `TCfgFile` the pages. #1088.

**Auto Memorize: Rest queues each caster's usual spells.** Pools of Darkness never clears a cast spell, so its next rest memorizes again what was cast; Pool clears the slot, and its usual spells (`UsualSpl`, the set last confirmed in Memorize) came back only by opening Memorize for each caster. `Camp.RestForSpellPrep`, which camp's Rest and Magic > Rest both run, now first queues each member's usual spells (`UsualSpl.QueueUsualSpells`) when the new Auto Memorize setting is on (`C_OPT.OPT_AUTO_MEMORIZE`, POOLOPT.CFG `AUTOMEMORIZE`, `GameVars.autoMemorizeOn`, on by default), passing by an animated or disabled member and one with a spell already pending, so a set picked in Memorize wins; the prep time then covers the queued spells as for any others. Fix's rest is unchanged. `QueueUsualSpells` and the slot helpers it needs (`SlotsUsed`, `HasSlotKind`, `SpellSlotsRemaining`, `PutPending`, `SortMemorizedSpells`, and the new `HasPending`) moved from Camp to `UsualSpl` and take the creature instead of reading `GameVars.curChar`; Memorize and Scribe call them there. Like Pools, this also brings back spells lost to a hit in combat. Tests: `TUsualSp` (queueing into the free slots, a held spell kept, a drained caster, a spell no longer known), `TCfgFile` (the key, on by default). #1119.

**Fix and level drain, mummy rot, and Fix's items and paladin's powers.** Fix already planned from a drained cleric's slots (a drain rebuilds `clericSpells`), but a party whose cleric was drained below Cure Disease rested the whole time for nothing: the wounds of a member with mummy rot were counted, Cure Light Wounds were planned for them, and `FxEngine.RestoreHitPoints` then refused every heal, with no message, since "Further healing isn't possible." came only when nothing at all was planned. The planning moved out of `Camp.CampFixMenu` into the new unit `FixPlan`, so it can be tested: `FixPlan.Plan` plans the cures first, from a table (`CureSpell`: a drained level Restoration, a disease Cure Disease, blindness Cure Blindness, Bestow Curse Remove Curse), each to the least busy okay cleric with a free slot of the spell's level that knows it, one cast per drained level; then as many Cure Light Wounds as the wounds need at 4.5 hit points each, counting a member with mummy rot only when its Cure Disease was planned. Only spell levels the clerics have slots for (`Types.CASTER_LEVEL_LAST`) are planned, so Restoration (level 7) is never planned here, and a game whose clerics reach it plans it like the others with no new guard. The rest time is the memorize rule generalized over spell levels (`CasterTime`, `RestMinutes`); the cures are carried out through `GameVars.spellFns` by the table. After Fix, each level-drained cleric who lacks every slot of a cure's level that its undrained level would give (`FixPlan.DrainedOutOf`, reading the drained levels as the cleric's) while a treatable member still has that condition gets a line, "<name> is level drained and can't cast <spell>.". Four new settings under a Fix heading on the Camp page (`OPT_FIX_SCROLLS`, `OPT_FIX_POTIONS`, `OPT_FIX_CURE`, `OPT_FIX_HEAL`; POOLOPT.CFG `FIXSCROLLS`, `FIXPOTIONS`, `FIXPALADINCURE`, `FIXPALADINHEAL`; all off by default) let Fix use more than spells, through `FixPlan.NextUse`, called until it finds nothing (each use spends what it uses): with Use Scrolls on, the party first reads its scrolls of Restoration on the drained before planning, so a cleric given back a level brings its slots to the same Fix (asked for on the issue); after a completed rest, or when the clerics have nothing to cast, for each treatable member in party order a disease goes to a paladin's weekly cure, a scroll of Cure Disease or Keoghtom's Ointment (not on a poisoned member, whom it would cure of poison instead), blindness to a scroll of Cure Blindness, Bestow Curse to a scroll of Remove Curse, a drained level to a scroll of Restoration; then a paladin lays on hands on the most wounded member, and scrolls of Cure Light Wounds, Potions of Healing and Keoghtom's Ointment treat wounds of at least their average roll (4, 7, 10 hit points), never on a member with mummy rot. A paladin's power comes before an item, as it comes back with time; a scroll is read by the first okay member who can read and use it (`CharHlp.CastableScroll`), a potion given by the first okay member carrying one. The new `Magic.CastItemSpellOn` casts an item's spell at a chosen target with no menu, and `Magic.SpendItemSpell`, split from the tail of `UseItemSpell`, spends it for both; the selection-free halves of View's Heal and Cure became `ViewChar.PaladinHeal` and `PaladinCure`, and the paladin checks moved to `CharHlp.PaladinReady`, `PaladinCanHeal` and `PaladinCanCure` for `FixPlan`. Fix then lists what it used ("Fix: <name> used <item>.", "... on <name> ..." for someone else, or what the paladin did) and the drain lines in the message window, waiting for a key when a line would start below it; with nothing done, the old messages stand, the drain lines under "Further healing isn't possible.". A drained member with nothing else to treat now gets that message rather than "Further healing would do no good.". The Game page had no room left after #1145, so the camp settings have a page of their own: `CfgFile.SettingPage` (replacing `SoundSetting`) puts each setting on the Game, Sound or Camp page (`C_OPT.OPT_PAGE_*`, the camp settings `OPT_FIRST_CAMP` to `OPT_LAST_CAMP`), and `SettingLine` counts a page's lines with its headings, now from the new `CfgFile.Heading` (Quick Mode, Fix). The game's Settings screen offers Game Sound Camp Paths sYstem Exit on its bottom line, without its "Settings:" prompt, which no longer fits; POOLCFG's Main Menu gains Camp Settings (C), and its Files box names the Camp Settings among the pages changed. Tests: `TFixPlan` (the drained cleric and the rot, the level 5 cleric's plan and rest, each setting's use and the order among them), `TCfgFile` (the keys, defaults off, the lines and headings). #1129.

**The temple sells Pools of Darkness's services, and Repair.** `Temple` is now goldbox-tp7's PoR temple over its shared `TownCore` (src/towncore.pas, carried over with the canonical races declared in it, since this repo has no CharType, and with no const parameters, which Turbo Pascal 6 lacks): the Heal list is `TownCore.ServiceName`'s twelve services, Heal, Resurrection and Restoration added, at `TownCore.StandardPrices`' 1st-edition gold (Heal 5000, Resurrection 11000, Restoration 7000; the rest as before), less the tenth off where the script asks (`MoneyCal.TemplePrice`). `Temple.TempleRules` keeps this temple's rules: a failed survival roll gone for good (#752), the elf refused (#61), and, unlike TownCore's default, a race-0 corpse raised, as SSI's temple raised any corpse but an elf's (a creature that joined through Animate Dead, #1089, is one); `Temple.CanonRace` maps C_RACE onto TownCore's races. The poisoned-corpse refusal (#751), Stone to Flesh's system shock (#1004), the question before a cure that cannot help (#1000) and Slow Poison's rule (#423) are TownCore's. The list starts a row higher (rows 3 to 15) so the twelve services and Exit fit one page. Repair (R on the Heal screen's bottom line, `Heal Repair Exit`; the counter's line, 39 columns with pooled money, has no room for it) is Pools' whole-party cure at this temple's prices: `TownCore.RepairPlan` lists what each member needs (Neutralize Poison; Raise Dead for the dead, or Stone to Flesh; a Restoration per drained level, `TTempleChar.drained`; then Heal when wounded or rotting, else Cure Blindness and Cure Disease), `RepairCost` prices it, `Temple.RepairParty` names the members refused (a dead elf, the gone) and quotes the party's total once (`RepairQuote`), takes it from the current character or the pool, and `TownCore.TempleRepair` carries each plan out without questions, Heal giving back every missing hit point. The survival roll and system shock are rolled and said as in the Heal list; a failure ends that member's plan, and the price of what was not done goes into the pool. The animated dead are left alone (raising one is the player's choice). Remove Curse is not part of Repair, as it is not of Pools'. Differs from SSI: SSI's temple sold nine services and no Repair; Pools' temple is free and its Repair rolls nothing. Tests: TTemple (goldbox-tp7's, with this game's rules, the tenth off and Repair). #1125.

**Animate Dead's dead join the party.** The manual says a creature animated by the party may join it; SSI never did it, and `PostCbt.PostCombatCreatureCleanup` removed every monster, so an animated enemy corpse vanished when the fight ended. After a won fight (not fled, not lost, not a duel), the cleanup now keeps each monster Animate Dead raised on the party's side and still animated (`RisenForParty`) while the party has room: out of the list, its combat record freed, its Animate Dead effect's stored side (bit 4 of the effect's strength) made the party's, so that Dispel Magic ending the spell (`EffHndlr.EffAnimateDead`, which gives back that side) no longer turns a party member hostile, and marked `CharExt.EXT_ANIMATE_JOINED`. `PostCbt.JoinRisen` then joins each through `EclMenu.JoinParty`, the portrait, add-to-party and icon steps taken out of `AddCreatureToParty`, so it joins as a script-joined NPC does, and says "<NAME> joins the party.", or "<NAME> collapses; the party is full." for one with no room; this runs before Retrieve Thrown (#1118), so a weapon the creature threw comes back to it, and before the loot menu. It stays an animated NPC (C_NPC.ANIMATED_NPC) under the computer's control, gets no experience and trades as an animated NPC does. The temple may raise one (Brian's call); a creature that joined this way and is alive again (`CharExt.SlipsAway`) leaves the party with all it carries at the next rest that passes any time, "<NAME> slipped away while the party slept." (`Camp.SlipAway`, after Rest, and after Fix when its clerics rested; `GameTime.rested`). The mark is a port field, `TCharExt.flags`, kept in the .chx file: SSI's record has no spare byte, its reserved bytes hold whatever the monster files put there, and the npc byte's bits are the morale. The .chx format is version 2; version 1 files still give the class left. Differs from SSI. Tests: TAnimJn, TDualCls. #1089.

**A magic shop in New Phlan.** Kirben asked for somewhere to spend the money a party piles up (#1091), and Brian chose the arms and armor shop at (11,12) (#1158); its stock adapts Curse's Zhentil Keep magic shop. New Phlan's script (ECL3 block 0) sends event 22, which (13,8), (8,11), (11,12) and (8,13)-(9,13) share, through new code that leaves the other arms and armor shops as they were; see section 7. At (11,12) the script runs PROGRAM 11, `EclVm.PROG_MAGIC_SHOP`, and then enters the shop as every shop does. The new overlaid unit `MagicShp` fills the stock (`StockShop`, `GameVars.treasureItems`, afresh each visit, so nothing sells out) from two tables: `wares`, seven items each built from RandItem's template (`RandItem.ApplyTemplate`) or as ammunition +1, and `scrolls`, twelve magic-user scrolls of three spells. Prices are Curse's where Curse sells the item (Potion of Extra Healing 1,600, Potion of Speed 900, Wand of Magic Missiles 40,000, 10 Arrows +1 240) and otherwise twice this game's value (Javelin of Lightning 6,000, Bag of Holding 50,000, 10 Darts +1 150, ammunition +1 being worth 150 a bundle of 20); every price fits `TItemRec.value`, a Word. Curse's Potion of Invisibility is left out: PoR has no such item in its data or its templates. A scroll costs `SCROLL_PRICE_PER_LEVEL`, 1,200 gold pieces (Curse's 300, four times over), for each level of each spell (`ScrollPrice`, from `GameDefs.spellTable`): 3,600, 7,200 and 10,800. The scrolls cover all 31 magic-user spells of levels 1 to 3 (13, 7 and 11: five, three and four scrolls), each level's last scroll repeating its most useful spells (Magic Missile and Sleep, Stinking Cloud and Mirror Image, Fireball); levels 4 and 5 are rows to add once those spells exist (#1121). Every stock item is identified (`unidMask` 0), so `Shop.WantsItem` can list a scroll's spells in the message window at Buy and ask "Buy it?" before the money is taken; the shop's line, "Magic User Scroll With 3", cannot tell the scrolls apart. Differs from SSI. Tests: TMagShop (each ware's name, plus, weight, quantity, properties and price; the scrolls' spells, levels and prices, their cover of every magic-user spell up to their highest level; `StockShop`), TDataPat, and the `magicshop` scenario. #1091, #1158.
**The startup advisory: where to send questions, and a speed check.** Players of this build were asking GOG and Steam for help, and DOSBox's default speed (`cycles=auto`, 3,000 cycles in real mode) is far too slow for it (#1173). `FixMain` now calls `Advisory.Measure` and `Advisory.Show` after the introduction, once `SoundDrv.StopMusic` has taken the sound timer off the PIT, before `CharGen.MainMenu`. The new overlaid unit `Advisory` shows, centred on a cleared screen, the game's name and `BUILD_VERSION` and the parts due, waits for a key, and clears the screen: the support part (a modified version; GOG, Steam and SNEG can't help; questions and bug reports to talonius@brazencrows.com) on the first start and after each new version, and under DOS the speed part (raise DOSBox's cycles: `cycles=max`, or for example `cycles=fixed 20000`) on every start the speed check finds slow. With neither due there is no screen. The decisions are in the new unit `AdvPlan` (`SupportDue`, `SpeedSlow`), apart from the screen for the tests. POOLOPT.CFG keeps the version last advised in a new key, `ADVISED` (`C_OPT.KEY_ADVISED`, after the later games' paths; `TSettings.advised`, read tidied, written as kept; `Options.AdvisedVersion` and `Options.SetAdvised`, which saves the file once the support part has been shown). The speed check (DOS only; natively it would measure only the host) times 16 runs of a 1,700-pass loop, each against PIT channel 0's counter (latched; in mode 3 it runs down by two a 1.19 MHz clock, wrapping every 27.5 ms, so each run is timed on its own, with the BIOS tick count as the time when a run outlasts that), about a tenth of a second at 3,000 cycles; it does not run while `SoundDrv.SoundTimerHooked` (now in SoundDrv's interface), the PIT then being at the sound timer's rate. The limit, `AdvPlan.SLOW_CLOCKS`, 19,900 clocks (16.7 ms, about 18,000 cycles), was measured in goldbox-player's DOSBox-X: the loop takes 300,000 / cycles ms; start to main menu takes 52.2 s at 3,000 cycles, 21.8 at 9,000, 15.7 at 15,000, 13.5 at 20,000, 11.7 at 30,000 and 10.5 at max, and a Quick fight 148.8, 91.1, 74.7, 62.4, 50.7 and 28.6 s; 20,000 passes and 17,500 does not (the figures are by the constant). STING skips the advisory, so the harness's scenarios, whose saves have no POOLOPT.CFG, still boot to the main menu; ADVISE after STING (`GameVars.adviseArg`, set in GameBoot) brings it back. The game font's 6-bit table has no `@` (`UpCase('@') mod 64` is glyph 0), and glyph 64 of font block 201, SSI's nearest, reads as a C on screen, so `Advisory.DisplayText` lends glyph 64's rows an 8x8 at sign of its own (`AT_BITMAP`, in the font's two-pixel strokes) while it draws one and puts them back after; every video driver draws a glyph from `gameFont` as it stands, so it shows in each mode and natively. The Settings screen's System page shows the speed check (Speed Check, in ms, SLOW past the limit, N/A natively), ADVISE among the switches, and the support lines wrapped to its box; the README and the distributed readmes say the same, and the README the speed advice. Tests: TAdvPlan (the support part with none advised, the same version in any case, a new or older one; the speed at, past and far past the limit, and unmeasured), TCfgFile (`ADVISED` read, tidied, blank, written last), and the `advisory` scenario. #1173.

**The magic shop lists a scroll's spells as the scribe list does.** `Shop.WantsItem` listed the scroll's spells flush left in white under "The scroll holds:". It now lists them as the scribe list's lines (#1196): a light magenta level heading (`Magic.LevelHeading`) in the first column, given again only when the level changes, and each spell indented under it with '+' in the marker column on a spell the highlighted character (`curChar`) could learn but doesn't know (`Magic.SpellLine` with `MARK_NOT_KNOWN`, the mark a scroll's Use list gives, #207), so a fighter, a cleric buying a magic-user scroll, or a magic-user who knows them all sees no marks. Brian chose the + alone, without the scribe list's - on known spells. The magic shop's scrolls hold three spells of one level, so the title, the heading and the spells fill the window's five rows; a scroll whose headings wouldn't fit is listed without them. Each line is drawn at its row (`TextRend.DisplayString`), not through `MessageText`, whose word wrap would take away the indent. To share them, `AddSpellToList`'s line (`SpellLine`) and its level names (`LevelHeading`) became functions of their own, and `SpellMark` and the `MARK_*` constants moved to Magic's interface.

**The Paths page goes; POOL.CFG's paths move to POOLCFG's Hardware page.** The later games' six folders in POOLOPT.CFG (CURSEDATA to POOLSSAVE) were kept for copying graphics from the other games, which waits until the goldbox-tp7 work is done (#1177). `C_OPT.KEY_PATHS` and `LATER_PATHS` go, `KEY_ADVISED` follows the settings directly, and `CfgFile` drops `TSettings.paths`, the six keys and their branches in `KeyName`, `Defaults`, `StoredValue` and `ReadValue`. An old POOLOPT.CFG still reads: the six lines are keys this version does not know, which `ReadOptions` passes over without counting them bad and `WriteOptions` writes back after the known keys, unchanged. The game's Settings screen loses its Paths page (`Options.DrawPathsPage`, `PAGE_PATHS`, the P key), its bottom line reading `Game Sound Camp sYstem Exit`. POOLCFG loses its Paths page and Main Menu item; POOL.CFG's own Data Path and Save Path, which START (natively POOLRAD) reads and only POOLCFG edits, become the Hardware page's fourth and fifth lines (`HW_DATA`, `HW_SAVE`), with the same in-place editor (`EditPath`, now at the values' column, 50 wide), checks, warnings, F5 defaults and too-long marking; the page's divider moves down two rows and its help names each path's POOL.CFG line or says what a missing folder or a path too long means. `HardwareChanged` now counts the paths, so the Files box names Hardware for them, and the Main Menu describes Hardware as "Video, sound, intro, folders". Tests: `TCfgFile` (ADVISED right after the settings, CURSEDATA no key, an old file's path read without a bad value and written back after the unknown lines). #1177.

**The System page shows the cycles the speed check matches.** The speed check (#1173) measured the computer, but the System page gave only the loop's time, while DOSBox asks for cycles. Its calibration in goldbox-player's DOSBox-X has the loop take 300,000 / cycles ms, so cycles are 357,954,600 (`AdvPlan.CYCLE_CLOCKS`, 300,000 ms at 1,193.182 PIT clocks a millisecond) over the clocks measured. The new pure `AdvPlan.CyclesOf` gives that to the nearest 100 (0 for a check that did not run), and `Advisory.CyclesText` shows it as "about 30,000", "under 100" for a computer slower than that, or N/A natively, where `speedClocks` is 0. The System page's new Cycles line sits under Speed Check, the switches and the support lines moving down a row; its 20 rows are now full. It is DOSBox-X's fixed cycles from one measurement at startup: GOG's DOSBox 0.74 or DOSBox Staging may map cycles somewhat differently (not measured), and on a real PC it is only a comparison, hence "about". The `advisory` scenario ignores the line, whose figure varies. Tests: `TAdvPlan` (`CyclesOf` at 0, GOG's 30,000, 20,000, DOSBox's default 3,000, the slow limit's 18,000, a computer far slower, and one clock without overflow). #1178.

**Consolidate Coin.** A new Game-page setting, Consolidate Coin (`C_OPT.OPT_CONSOLIDATE`, POOLOPT.CFG `CONSOLIDATECOIN`, `GameVars.consolidateOn`, off by default, between Auto Join and the Journal), changes a character's purse into the fewest coins of the same value whenever it picks coins up (#1179). Every coin weighs 1 (`CharHlp.RawWeight`), so it never adds weight and usually takes a lot off. The game already did this to a payer's purse: `MoneyCal.MakeChange` remakes a value as the largest coins, platinum first, gems and jewelry left alone, and each coin is worth a whole number of the next smaller (1, 10, 100, 200, 1000 copper), so the greedy change is the fewest coins and loses nothing. The new pure `MoneyCal.Consolidate` is `MakeChange` of the purse's own copper value, each type at most `C_MONEY.MAX_COINS`; `Money.Consolidate` applies it to a creature, with its stats recalculated, when the setting is on. It runs after a successful `TakeMoney` (Take in the loot, the shop and temple pool and the vault), after each sharer's shares in `ShareMoney`'s first pass (so the lighter purse has room for leftovers) and after each leftover in its second, whose four-way branch became one clamp with no change in what it gives, and at the end of `GiveCoins` (selling an item, or an appraised gem or jewel). Not in `TradeMoney`: handing coins over is a deliberate move of a chosen type, and consolidating the receiver would stop it handing the same coins back. The treasure pool itself is not consolidated; it is not carried. Script money reaches a purse through the pool, and the only scripts touching a character's coins total them by value (ECL2 block 9) or charge one platinum (ECL3 block 0), so no script depends on a coin mix. A take is still refused by the coins' raw weight before consolidating, as before. The settings are renumbered, Consolidate Coin taking 6; POOLOPT.CFG goes by key name. POOLCFG's About text: "When a character picks up coins, its purse is changed into the fewest coins of the same value: 5 gold make a platinum. Coins weigh the same, so the load drops." Tests: `TMoneyCa` (1,835 copper's worth of mixed coins becomes 1 pp, 4 gp, 3 sp and 5 cp, gems and jewelry kept; a consolidated purse unchanged; 1,000 cp a platinum; platinum at its cap leaving the rest in gold), `TCfgFile` (the key, its default off, its line). #1179.

**Non-Human Level Cap.** A new Game-page setting, Non-Human Level Cap (`C_OPT.OPT_NONHUMAN_CAP`, POOLOPT.CFG `NONHUMANCAP`, `GameVars.nonHumanCapOn`, on by default, after the Journal), lifts the demihumans' racial level limits when off (#1176). They are all in `CharHlp.AtLevelCap` (fighters and half-elf rangers by permanent Strength, half-elf clerics at 5, half-orc clerics by Wisdom and thieves by Dexterity), which training, its experience clamp and Restoration ask; it now answers False before its race tests while the setting is off. Which classes a race may take is not its business, so off lifts limits without opening classes. The other limit, the experience table's last level for each class (`GameDefs.classExperience`: clerics and magic-users 6, paladins and rangers 7, fighters 8, thieves 9), applies to every race and is #1121's, gated where the table is read, so the two settings stay independent: with only this one off a demihuman goes as far as a human of its class; turned back on, a character already past its cap keeps its levels and stops training. With it off the race screen at creation (`CharGen.ShowRaceInfo`) shows U for every class the race can take. `GameVars` starts zeroed, which reads as off until `GameInit` applies the defaults, so `TCharHlp` sets it before testing the caps. The settings are renumbered, Non-Human Level Cap taking 8; POOLOPT.CFG goes by key name. POOLCFG's About text: "Dwarves, elves, gnomes, half-elves, halflings and half-orcs stop at their race's level limits. Off: they train as far as the experience table goes, as humans do." Tests: `TCharHlp` (with the setting off, no cap for a half-elf cleric at 5, an elf fighter at 7 or a half-orc cleric of Wisdom 14 at 4; on, as before), `TCfgFile` (the key, its default on, its line). #1176.

**Game Level Cap, and the spells to level 9.** A new Game-page setting, Game Level Cap (`C_OPT.OPT_LEVEL_CAP` = 9, after Non-Human Level Cap, the later settings moved up one; POOLOPT.CFG `LEVELCAP`; `GameVars.levelCapOn`, on by default), lets every class train to 9 when off (#1121). The cap was the experience table's: `GameDefs.classExperience` marked levels a class could not reach with -1. It now holds the 1st-edition PHB's experience to 9 for every class and its slot rows to level 9 and spell level 5; `ClassCal.LevelXp` reads it, `ClassCal.MaxLevel` is SSI's caps (cleric and magic-user 6, paladin and ranger 7, fighter 8, thief 9) with the setting on and 9 with it off, and `ClassCal.LevelOpen` is what training (`CharHlp.TrainClasses`), its experience clamp and the Levels tool ask; Restoration asks only that the level exists. CharEdit's own copy of LevelXp went. The racial caps stay `CharHlp.AtLevelCap`'s (#1176), which gains the half-elf magic-user's 8. The level-6 clamp in `RecalcBaseSkills` is gone, so slots follow the real level; its slot work moved to `ClassCal.CasterSlots`, which also adds a paladin's cleric slot from 9 and a ranger's druid slots from 8 and magic-user slot from 9 (`GameDefs.paladinSlots`, `rangerDruidSlots`, `rangerMageSlots`), gives the cleric's 5th-level slot only at Wisdom 17, and marks every cleric and druid spell of a level with slots known. `CharHlp.GetCasterLevel` casts a paladin's spells at his level less 8 and a ranger's druid and magic-user spells at his level less 7 and 8.

SSI's record has three spell levels of slots, 56 known spells and 21 memorized slots, so the rest live in `Types.TCharExt` and the .chx file (version 4; versions 1-3 still read): `moreSlots` (levels 4 and 5), `druidSlots` (`C_SPELL.CLASS_DRUID`, a new slot kind; `CLASS_SPECIAL` is now 3), `moreKnown` (a bit for each id 57-100) and `moreMemorized` (slots 21-47, `Types.MEMORIZED_LAST`). Code reaches them through `CharHlp.SpellSlots`/`SetSpellSlots`, `KnowsSpell`/`SetSpellKnown` and `MemorizedAt`/`SetMemorizedAt`/`GetMemorized`; the record's `slots` view went. The usual spells' .mem file is version 2, 48 slots (version 1 still reads). The memorize screen's slot window shows five levels and a druid row.

The spells use Curse's ids (`C_SPELL.LAST` = 100). Curse has Cure Serious Wounds at 58 and Neutralize Poison at 67, PoR's Keoghtom's Ointment and Manual of Bodily Health, which move to 127 and 128 (`C_SPELL.ITEM_OINTMENT`, `ITEM_BODILY_HEALTH`), past every game's table (Pools' runs to 126); their item effects become 150 and 151 (an item effect above `ITEM_EFFECT_DIRECT_LAST`, 56, is the spell plus 23). RandItem's ointment template changes, a DataPat part fixes the shipped Manual, and `DiskRec.ItemFromDisk` moves an older Ointment or Manual by its name parts. Spell-page lines are Words (`C_SPELL.FROM_ITEM` = $100), as 128 has bit 7 set. Ported from Curse, with handlers, effects 142-156 (`C_EFFECT`), save results and descriptions: cleric 4th 58, 66-70, cleric 5th 71-76, druid 77-80, magic-user 4th 81-89 and 100, magic-user 5th 90 and 92-94; cones (Fear, Cone of Cold) and pooled targets (Faerie Fire, Charm Monsters) are new shapes in `CharHlp.SpellArea` and the aim preview. Where the port departs from Curse for the rule book: Invisibility to Animals is a touch spell on the caster's side; Animate Dead is the magic-user's 5th-level spell; Slay Living saves vs death magic and spares the undead; a failed Raise Dead survival roll is final; Feeblemind stops casting from memory until a temple heals it; Dimension Door to a cell the caster can't stand in leaves it where it was. Cloudkill (91) followed in #1193, below. tests/tspell45.pas covers the rows, shapes and handlers.

**Train Anywhere.** Joonas's idea (#1212): the Gold Box Companion trains anywhere without the rules, and this trains anywhere with them. A new Game-page setting, Train Anywhere (`C_OPT.OPT_TRAIN_ANYWHERE` = 10, after Game Level Cap, the later settings moved up one; POOLOPT.CFG `TRAINANYWHERE`; `GameVars.trainAnywhereOn`, off by default). The plan put a Train command on camp's Alter; Brian made it a key instead, F4 (`C_KEYS.F4`), so it works wherever the map and camp menus hand their extended keys to `Explore.SideScreen` (the 3D map, the wilderness and camp, as F1, F2, F6 and Alt-n do), and the label names it: "Train Anywhere (F4)". With the setting off F4 does nothing. `CharGen.TrainAnywhere` saves `GameVars.diskIndex` (the BODY portrait archive the hall's menu sets to 3), `ifcState`, `horizChoice` and `CharDisp.hallMenu`, sets `GameVars.trainingAnywhere`, runs `CharGen.MainMenu(True, True)` and puts them back; SideScreen then draws the map or camp again. `MainMenu`'s new `anywhere` parameter has `PartyMnu.OfferedCmds` offer Train, View and Dual Class (with a character) and Exit; `PartyMnu.ExitLeaves` (Exit offered without Settings, which every other form offers) has `DrawCmd` draw it "Exit" rather than "Exit to DOS" and `ChooseCmd` take Escape as Exit, and `MainMenu` returns on it. The hall's classes are now `CharHlp.HallMask`: the script's `C_GBVM.TRAIN_CLASS_MASK` word, or every class's bit while `trainingAnywhere` is set. `TrainingMenu`, `MainMenu`'s hall test and `ChgClass.Obstacle` ask it, so the word itself is never written and a game saved later carries no hall into the scripts. Everything else is `TrainingMenu`'s as at a hall: Okay status, 1,000 gp, the experience, one level per class, the experience clamp, the racial caps (`CharHlp.AtLevelCap`, with Non-Human Level Cap, #1176) and the class's last level (`ClassCal.LevelOpen`, with Game Level Cap, #1121); Dual Class keeps its 2,500 gp and its rules. No time passes (the plan's weeks of training were left out). KeyHelp lists "F4 Train Anywhere" while the setting is on; with every line shown (STING's Ctrl-Y among them) the window had no room for the blank rows between its groups, so `KeyHelp.GroupGaps` drops them then. Tests: `TCfgFile` (the key, its default, its line), `TPartyMn` (the menu's commands, ExitLeaves), `TCharHlp` (HallMask, the GBVM word untouched), `TKeyHelp` (F4 with the setting, GroupGaps); scenario `trainanywhere`. #1212.
