**Settings name their keys.** The labels of the settings a key acts on outside the Settings screen name the key (#1215): Train Anywhere (F4), Sound (Ctrl-S), Companion Tools (F6) and Combat Log (F7) (`CfgFile.SettingName`, so the game's Settings screen, POOLCFG and POOLCFG's About box all show it). The longest, "Companion Tools (F6)", is 20 characters; the Settings screen has 21 before its values (`LABEL_COL` 2 to `SETTING_COL` 24) and POOLCFG the same (4 to 26), and `TCfgFile` checks every label fits. The Minimap's label already named F3 (#1210). #1215.

**The spell lists run level by level.** The lists `Magic.GenerateSpellList` builds went in spell-id order, and `AddSpellToList` adds a level heading whenever a spell's level differs from the one before it. With Curse's ids the magic-user's Bestow Curse is 100, past the 5th-level spells at 90..94, so a 9th-level magic-user's spell book, Learn list and memorized spells (kept in id order by `UsualSpl.SortMemorizedSpells`) ended with a second "4th Level" heading holding Bestow Curse alone. Every list but Cast, which already went level by level (`AddAvailableSpells`), now walks the levels 1 to 7 and adds each level's spells in id order (`AddScrollSpells` and `AddAllScrollSpells` take the level, and the memorized lists read a slot's level with `SlotLevel`), so each heading comes once; a scroll's or the scribe list's spells of mixed levels come out grouped too. `Magic.SpellListLine` reads a line of the list for the unit tests (`tscribe`) (#1199).

**Cloudkill, and clouds of either size.** #1121 left spell 91 empty, saying PoR had no cloud system; it had Stinking Cloud's, which #1193 widens rather than duplicates. The list (`GameVars.cloudHead`, was `stinkHead`) holds both kinds: each `Types.TCloudRec` (was `TStinkRec`) has its `terrain` (`C_TERR.STINKING_CLOUD` or the new `C_TERR.CLOUDKILL`) and `side` (`C_SPELL.STINKING_CLOUD_SIDE` 2, `CLOUDKILL_SIDE` 3), and nine cells. `CbtMap.CloudCell` gives a cloud's cells, Stinking Cloud's 2x2 from the aim cell (`GameDefs.cloudDir`, as before) and Cloudkill's 3x3 round it (`GameDefs.cloudkillDir`, Curse's order). `Magic.LayCloud` is the one caster: it appends the node (`CbtMap.AppendCloud`), adds the caster's effect (40, or the new `C_EFFECT.CAST_CLOUDKILL` 157, both at `FxEngine.CloudStrength`), spreads the cloud over the arena (`CbtMap.SpreadCloud`: a cell under another cloud of either kind keeps the ground that cloud saved, a corpse's cell the corpse's ground) and tries each creature inside once. `EffHndlr.EffCastCloud` (was `EffCastStink`) serves both effects, lifting the cloud with `CbtMap.LiftCloud`, which repaints the remaining clouds with their own terrain. `FxEngine.FreeCloudNumber` numbers a caster's clouds of both kinds together, so the effect's number names one node. `CharHlp.SpellArea` gives both the cloud shape with their sides, and the spell page reads AREA 3x3 for Cloudkill.

Cloudkill's rules are the 1st-edition PHB's, as `FxEngine.CloudkillFate` gives them: a creature of up to 4 levels or hit dice (`levelHighest`) dies; one of 5 saves vs. poison at -4 and one of 6 at 0, or dies; one above 6 is untouched, as in Curse, so `FxEngine.CloudkillProof` counts it proof and the AI neither avoids the cloud for it nor counts it as a target. `Magic.ConsiderCloudkill` applies them, with the Constitution's poison bonus, killing as a poison touch does (poisoned, then `InflictStatusMsg` "is killed"), where Stinking Cloud's check runs: on the creatures inside when the cloud is laid, at each round's end (`Combat.EndOfRound`), and after each step (`CbtActs.StepCreature`, where a death ends the turn). `FxEngine.CloudkillProof` spares the animated dead and creatures immune to poison (effects 111 and 125). Magic resistance is not rolled, as in Curse. A large creature on both kinds of cloud is found in the Cloudkill by `CbtMap.ProbeTerrain`; `ProbeDirection` still names the stinking cloud first, then the Cloudkill. The cloud does not drift away from its caster as the PHB's does, so the range is Curse's 2 squares, not the PHB's 1".

The terrain is 66, past the arena's own, not Curse's 28: PoR's RANDCOM.DAX has a decoration there (a banana peel, which `CombatSt.SprinkleRareFeatures` scatters on grass), where Curse's has its blue cloud. PoR's tiles hold no Cloudkill cloud, so `MapTiles.LoadCloudkillArt` keeps a copy of RANDCOM.DAX's stinking cloud frame for each fight (one tile, about 300 bytes of picture memory in EGA) with its light green turned yellow, the PHB's "yellowish green" vapours; `MapTiles.DrawTerrain` draws every combat terrain, Cloudkill's from that picture. `terrainInfo[66]` names the stinking cloud's tile, which stands in should the picture be missing.

The AI: `CombatAi.AvoidsCloudkill` keeps out every creature the cloud can touch, unless it stands in one already and may walk out through it (Curse's kept out only those below level 7); `Pathing.RouteStep` takes a set of terrains to avoid (`Pathing.TTerrSet`), so a walker can keep out of both kinds. `CbtActs.SpellCanAffect` passes over a creature the Cloudkill cannot touch or one standing in a Cloudkill, and `CbtActs.CloudCatch` counts a cloud spell's own block. The aim preview lights the cloud's cells, its side the preview's radius. Tests: TClouds (the cells, laying a Cloudkill over a stinking cloud and a corpse, lifting each, `ProbeTerrain` under a large creature, `CloudkillFate`, `CloudkillProof`), TSpcAff (Cloudkill's catch and targets), TPathing (two terrains avoided), TSpelInf, TSpell45. #1193.

**A Combat page in Settings.** With Paths gone (#1177) the bottom line had room for another page, and the Game page held sixteen rows, half of them about fights (#1180). The settings are renumbered so that each page is one run, in page order (`C_OPT`): the Game page 1-10 (`OPT_LAST_GAME`: the speed, the three portrait settings, Auto Join, Consolidate Coin, the Journal, Non-Human Level Cap, the Companion Tools and their Clue Book notes), Sound 11-20, Camp 21-25, and the new Combat page 26-32 (`OPT_FIRST_COMBAT` to `OPT_LAST_COMBAT`: Auto End Quick Mode, Use Magic and Allow Gear Changes under the Quick Mode heading, then Aim Cycles, Retrieve Thrown, Combat Log and Log Lines). The speed stays first and the log lines last, so `OPT_FIRST_FLAG` to `OPT_LAST_FLAG` keep their meaning, and POOLOPT.CFG is read by key name, so old files load as they were and are written in the new order. `CfgFile.SettingPage` returns the new `OPT_PAGE_COMBAT` (`OPT_PAGES` is 4) for that run, and `SettingLine` puts the Quick Mode heading on the Combat page's first row. The game's Settings screen has a Combat Settings page (`Options.PAGE_COMBAT`), its bottom line reading `Game Sound Camp coMbat sYstem Exit` (34 columns), with M for Combat as C is Camp's; without EMS its combat log lines are grey as before. POOLCFG's Main Menu gains Combat Settings (M, "Quick Mode, aim, combat log") in the slot Paths had, its Game Settings described as "Speed, portraits, Journal", and its Files box names Combat Settings among the pages changed. The pages now hold Game 10 rows of the box's 20, Sound 10, Camp 6, Combat 8 and System 20. Tests: `TCfgFile` (the pages' runs in order, Combat the last, the Game page's lines to the Clue Book notes, the Combat page's from the heading to the log lines, and Down from the Clue Book notes passing a greyed Sound page to Camp), and the `boot` scenario records the Combat page. #1180.

**A Move mode in the wilderness, with diagonal steps.** Kirben's request (#1159): Home and End, the keypad's 7 and 1, picked the character, so the wilderness offered four directions only. SSI's three wilderness scripts (ECL 25, 26 and 27) already handle all eight. Every `ON GOTO` and `ON GOSUB` on the raw facing (`C_GBVM.FACING_RAW`, `[$033D]`) in their move entry has eight targets: the edge checks, the hand-overs between regions and the next-cell sum. Only the menu never offered the diagonals. `Explore.MapMainMenu`'s wilderness line is now `Move Cast View Encamp Search Look Jrnl`, as in Pools of Darkness. Move sets `moveMode`, and the line becomes `Exit`. There `MoveKey` maps Up, Page Up, Right, Page Down, Down, End, Left and Home to facings 0 to 7 and ends the menu for the step, until Exit or Escape. F1, F2, F5, F6, F8 and Alt-1..8 keep working. The mode ends when the party leaves the wilderness, and in `ResetGame`. `Explore.MovePartyWilderness` now goes through the new `Wilds.StepTo`, which holds the step to the region as before and gives what it meets (`Wilds.TileStep`, the impassable and river glyphs unchanged). A diagonal step whose two orthogonal cells are both impassable (the river counting while it is shut) is `STEP_CORNER` and refused silently, as impassable terrain is. One blocked side doesn't stop it: a square is half a day's travel, and refusing it would close diagonals along every coast. The shipped map has no such two-sided corner in any region, so only the one-sided case comes up in play. A diagonal step costs `Wilds.DIAGONAL_HOURS`, 18 hours, and a straight one `STEP_HOURS`, 12. `CombatSt.PlaceAllCreatures` places a fight that starts after a diagonal step as the cardinal facing before it (`gFacing and 6`, as a loaded save keeps it), since its placement tables have four directions. Widening the map over the party list in Move mode, as the later games do, is #1186. Tests: TView3D (`TileStep`, `StepTo`: straight, diagonal, a corner blocked on two sides and on one, the river shut and open, the region's edges), and the `wildmove` scenario (Move, north-east and north-west at 18 hours each, east at 12, Escape, Home picking the character again); `wilderness`, `wildfight`, `outpost` and `altitems` wait for the menu with or without Move. #1159.

**Move mode's wide view.** Kirben's second idea in #1159 (#1186): in Move mode the wilderness map takes the whole top of the screen, as the later games' overland does, instead of the 5x5 view beside the party list. Brian chose 13 tiles across, the 13th clipped to two of its three cells so the map fills the box (cells 1 to 38), and the status line on the bottom line before Exit. `C_AREA` gains `WILD_WIDE_LAST` (12), `WILD_WIDE_CELLS` (38) and `WILD_WIDE_ROWS` (120), and `VIEW_ROWS`/`VIEW_CELLS` for the 168x21 view buffer `GameInit` allocates. `Wilds.ViewCorner` and `BuildWildernessMap` take the view's last column (`Ending` passes `WILD_VIEW_LAST`), the centring moved from `MapView` to `Wilds.CenteredCorner`, which centres the wide view on its seventh tile (the party at view cells 18 to 20) and moves it inside the map at an edge as before; `Wilds.ViewLast` gives the shown view's width. The tiles go through the new `MapTiles.Copy2DTileClipped` (`Blit.BlitTileClipped`), which with `Copy2DTile` shares one `DrawTile`. The drivers need nothing: every `FlushSpans` places a span by its screen offset from the view's origin, and the blitter steps by the buffer's width.

The buffer: `Wilds.ShowWide` frees the view buffer and allocates it again at the size of the view to show, 120 scanlines by 38 cells for the wide view, so it costs only the difference (8,256 bytes on VGA, 4,128 on EGA and Tandy, 2,064 on CGA) and only while it is on screen. When `MaxAvail` can't hold the wide one the 5x5 buffer comes back (its own freed block holds it) and `wideWanted` is cleared, Move mode going on in the 5x5 view. It loads the second tile set first (`MapTiles.LoadSecondSheet`, split out of `SecondSheet`), as that set's blocks of up to 64K would not fit later beside the wide buffer. The plan weighed this against growing the buffer to 38 cells for good, +22,848 bytes on VGA everywhere; measured from save G on a 1 MB machine (System page, then STING Ctrl-Y): main menu free memory and largest block unchanged but for 32 bytes of code and data (VGA 194,960/145,920 before, 194,928/145,888 after); in the wilderness outside Move mode unchanged (VGA 98,656/70,904); in Move mode VGA 90,368 free, largest block 34,392 (from 70,904: the wide buffer is cut from the largest block, the narrow one's hole left apart), EGA 197,824/173,816 (from 201,984/192,120), CGA 247,744/238,528 (from 249,840/247,712); after leaving Move mode the heap is as before. The permanent buffer would have taken 22,848 bytes from every screen of the game on VGA, the fights included, to save 8,256 in Move mode, so the swap stays.

The screen: `Wilds.DrawWildWindows` draws the message window, then `DrawTopWindows`: one window (1,1)-(38,15) while `wideShown`, after clearing the top to its bottom edge so `Border` doesn't put the map window's corner back at (16,16) (#1172), or the two windows as before. `CharDisp.DisplayParty` and `PrintMiscInfo` draw nothing over the wide view (`ifcState` OVERLAND), so the paths that redraw them during a step leave the map alone. `PrintMiscInfo`'s text is `CharDisp.MiscInfoText`, which `Explore.MapMainMenu` passes as Move mode's query, in light green: `27,27 E 23:25 search Exit`. `DisplayWildernessInterface` shows the view `Wilds.wideWanted` asks for; the new `CharDisp.SetWildView` switches views and draws the top of the screen without touching the message window, clearing the bottom line when it narrows. `DisplayNoncombatInterface`'s OVERLAND case draws the wilderness screen while the wide view is up.

Turning it on and off: Move sets `wideWanted`, and each pass of the map menu shows the wide view while it is set, so a view a script narrowed during a step widens again at the next menu. Exit and Escape narrow it and end the mode (`Explore.EndMoveMode`, also on leaving the wilderness, in `ResetGame`, and when F8 is about to load the quick save, so the loaded game's scripts draw into the 5x5 buffer). `Explore.SideScreen` (F1, F2, F6, Alt-1..8) shows the 5x5 buffer before its screen (the items screen draws a portrait into it) and redraws the wide one after. Scripts: before each command, `EclVm.CallEclScript` narrows the view for any command outside `Wilds.KeepsWide`. The plan's list of silent commands would have narrowed it at every step: a trace (STING Ctrl-L) of 24 plain steps in the central wilderness shows each running `PRINTCLEAR " "`, `SPRITE OFF`, `PICTURE 255` and two `CALL`s. So `KeepsWide` also holds PRINT, PRINTCLEAR and PRINTRETURN (the message window is the same under both views), CLEAR MONSTERS, SPRITE OFF and PICTURE (which redraw the map only through `MapView.DisplayMapView`), and CALL; `EclPicture` narrows before it shows a picture, and `EclCall` before a duel or a fight alone. Everything else narrows: the menus, WHO, LOAD MONSTER, APPROACH, COMBAT, NEWECL, CLEAR BOX and so on, so an encounter, a site's question, a fight or a new area get the usual screen with the party list, and the 168-line buffer is back before combat or the 3D view draws. Tests: TView3D (`ViewCorner` and `CenteredCorner` for the wide view at both edges, the party's column at map columns 0, 6, 25 and 43, and `KeepsWide` against every opcode), and `wildmove` extended: the wide view on Move, a step west to Phlan's eastern edge narrowing it for the city's picture and menu, LEAVE widening it again, three steps, F1 and Alt-2 coming back wide, Escape narrowing it. Checked by eye in EGA, CGA and VGA captures. #1186.

**A drain log: Restoration gives back the experience a drain took.** `EffHndlr.DrainLevels` set experience to the start of the level a character fell to and recorded only the count of levels and the hit points (`drainedLevels`, `drainedHps`, SSI's bytes); Restoration then raised the class with the lowest next level, which need not be the one drained, gave back the average `drainedHps div drainedLevels`, and raised experience only to that level's start, so everything above it was lost for good. Brian asked for the drains to be kept like the Strength effects, last in, first out. Each level a drain takes (and the creature survives) now goes on a drain log, `Types.TCharExt.drainCount` and `drains[1..DRAIN_LOG_LEN]` (8), each entry the experience lost (before less after, a LongInt: a capped character's can pass 65,535), the hit points taken and the class lowered (`CharExt.PushDrain`; a ninth drops the oldest). `Magic.RestoreLevel`, split from `SpellRestoration` so it can be tested, pops the most recent entry (`CharExt.PopDrain`), gives back exactly its hit points, raises its class when Restoration may (`Magic.RestorableClass`, the old eligibility test) or else the class the old rule picks (`Magic.RestoreClass`, the old scan), and adds its experience to the experience the character has now, at least the restored level's start. SHIP, drained from cleric 4 (6750) to 3 and, after earning 500, to 2, then earning 15, is restored to 3 with 3516 and to 4 with 7265, the 6750 he had and the 515 he earned. A drained level the log does not hold (from a save before this, or past its eight entries) is restored by the old rule, and the temple, Fix and scrolls all reach `RestoreLevel` through `spellFns[56]`. Training while drained (`CharGen.GainLevels`) drops an entry (`CharExt.TrainDrain`: the most recent of the trained class, else the most recent unless an unlogged level is there to take) and forfeits its experience, as Brian decided: the level was earned again, not restored. `ClassCal.ChangeClass` clears the log with the drained levels. The log lives in the `.chx` file, now version 3 (`CharExt.ReadExt` still reads versions 1 and 2 with an empty log, and cuts a log longer than `drainedLevels`); `drainedLevels` and `drainedHps` stay as they were for SSI's format, the temple's Repair and Fix's counts, and Curse's import. `DrainLevels` is now in `EffHndlr`'s interface for the tests. Tests: TDrain (SHIP's sequence, a two-level drain, an old save, a logged drain over an unlogged one, the ninth drain, training, a class change, the `.chx` at version 3 and 2). #1174.

**The demo (#1109), from the Commodore 64's script.** The C64 and Apple II releases played a demo when the title was left alone; the PC's had none. Kirben found it in the C64's `ECL1E` (disk 1) and decoded it; its bytecode is the PC's (the same opcodes, the five-entry header, and nearly every address one our VM maps), so the port runs it as it runs an area's script. Brian supplied the C64 disks, from which the script and the four characters it adds were extracted (the archive on #1109). They are committed under `src/demo` as source data: the script as the C64 has it (`ecl1e.bin`) and the four characters converted to PC monster records (`.cha`, 285 bytes, and `.itm`, the 63-byte items). RESTAL and TARRAN are the PC's 4TH LVL FIGHTER (MON1CHA 41) renamed, DIANE is MON7CHA 79 and WILLIAM D'OR is DIRTEN (MON3CHA 107) renamed. `scripts/demodax.py` builds `src/binaries/DEMO.DAX` from them reproducibly (`--check` says whether the committed one is current), and the release ships it beside RAVFULL.DAX and SBSFX.DAT (package.sh, package-native.sh and the readmes). Its blocks (`C_DEMO`): the script is block 1, and the characters are blocks 240-243 with their items 8 above. The ids are new: on the PC, the C64's 22, 23 and 107 are the giant skeleton, the vampire and Dirten, and 79 is Diane only on disk 7.

The script is the C64's but for patches the generator makes, each checked against the bytes it replaces, with the routines they call added past its end so that no address moves. ADD NPC takes the new ids. Each caption's pause holds it for about six seconds, not the C64's two DELAYs (0.9 s at the demo's speed). After the battle, a lost or fled fight ends the demo (COMBAT_RESULT not 0). And the title portrait is taken down (PICTURE 255) once Podol Plaza is loaded: on the PC a picture keeps the view's flags (`EclMenu.monsterFlags`), and SETUP MONSTER would otherwise never draw the ogre's sprite.

The C64 things the VM lacked: `C_GBVM.KEY_COUNT` (`$C6`, the KERNAL's keyboard count, which the script tests after every pause and ends on) reads 1 once a key has ended the demo (`GameIo.DemoEnding`); `CALL_ANIMATE` (`$8403`) shows the view's next picture frame, held for the frame's time but at least a quarter second (the ogre's later frames have none); `CALL_VIEW_FRAME` (`$0806`, which 13 PC scripts call to no effect) draws the picture's frame in the demo; a `GAME_SPEED` write sets the text delay in the demo, so the step's pause between footfalls is instant; and `PROGRAM 3` (`EclVm.PROG_DEMO_END`, which no PC script uses) ends the script in the demo.

Starting and ending it. The start-up menu (`PartyMnu.ChooseListed`, with no party and DEMO.DAX there and whole, `Demo.Available`: `Demo.Complete` reads its directory with `DataArc.BlockRawSize` and wants the script and every record and item block, each entry inside the file and within `C_DATA.PACKED_LIMIT`, so a missing, unreadable or short archive never reaches DaxIo's disk prompt or its errors that end the game; the start-up menu then simply waits on, and `DEMO` starts nothing) gives `MenuCore.HorizontalMenu` a timeout (`menuTimeout`, `menuDefaultKey`, as Curse's title menu has), 30 seconds, and `CharGen.MainMenu` returns with `GameVars.demoMode` set; FixMain then runs `Demo.Run` (a new overlaid unit) and goes back to the main menu, so it plays again after another 30 seconds. The `DEMO` switch makes the first wait three seconds. `Demo.Run` sets the demo's speed and a fixed random seed (so the battle plays the same each time), runs the script's load entry (`EclVm.RunDemo`), then frees the party and the picture it showed, resets the game state as after a destroyed party (`GameInit.ResetGameState`) and puts back the player's text delay and random seed, so the next character rolls as it would have. Any key ends it: `TextRend.PauseOnly` waits in ticks and stops on a key there (`TextRend.PauseTicks`), `EclVm.Reloading` answers True once it is ended, so the script stops at its next command, `Combat.CombatMain` ends the battle after the creature acting, and `CombatAi.CheckKeystroke` takes the key for the end of the demo. The key is left pending and drained at the end. No menu waits in the demo: `GameIo.ReadGameKey` returns Escape at once (ending it), `MenuCore.HorizontalMenu` waits for nothing, `YesNoMenu` answers No, and the battle asks no "Continue Battle".

The battle: the NPCs fight under Quick (`EclVm.EclAddNpc`); `PostCbt.PostCombat` only takes the monsters away and keeps the result for the script, with no experience, treasure, summary or loot, and without taking a party of NPCs alone for a destroyed one; the Bestiary, which lives outside the saves, learns nothing (`Bestiary.Met`, `Watched`, `Killed`); and `SaveLoad.SaveToSlot` writes nothing. A joining NPC's combat figures are worked out before it joins and is shown (`EclMenu.AddCreatureToParty` calls `CharHlp.RecalcStats` before `JoinParty`): the 4th-level fighter's record, RESTAL's and TARRAN's, says THAC0 -91 and +102 damage, SSI's leftovers. Paladins lead no one in the demo (`CbtActs.UseLeadership`, #1124): a led NPC would come under the player. Natively DEMO.DAX is found beside the program, else in the data folder, as RAVFULL.DAX is (`DaxIo.FolderOf`). Tests: `tests/tdemo.pas` (with archives whole, short of a block and cut short), and the scenarios `demo` (to the end), `nodemo` (no DEMO.DAX: the main menu stays) and `demoabort` (a key mid-walk and mid-battle, then the memory, a new character's rolls and a load), for which `tests/regress.sh` stages DEMO.DAX.

**The shop's Buy screen (mocks/new-shop.txt).** Buy at a shop's counter opens `ShopBuy.BuyScreen` (a new overlaid unit) in place of SSI's Buy list, `Shop.ShopBuyItemSelection`, which is gone. The screen is three boxes sharing their edges: the tabs on row 1, the stock on rows 3 to 15, and a panel on rows 17 to 22, with `SHOP: SHOWING ALL BUY EXIT` on the bottom line (the filter in force: `SHOWING ALL` or `SHOWING USABLE`). The tabs are All, Melee, Ranged, Armor, Ammo and Misc, keyed L, M, R, A, O and I (`ShopBuy.tabKeys`, given to the list as `MenuCore.extraKeys`); the tabs take the bottom line's colours: the current tab in inverse video (black on white), the others light green with their keys white. Each tab but All and Misc lists a set of item types: Melee 1..40 but darts (9) and quarrels (28), Ranged the thrown weapons (hand axe, dagger, dart, hammer, javelin, spear) and 41..47 (the bows, crossbows and sling), Armor 48..59 and bracers (77), Ammo quarrels and arrows (`MELEE_TYPES`, `RANGED_TYPES`, `ARMOR_TYPES`, `AMMO_TYPES`). Misc lists every type none of those does, so a type the sets do not name (a helm, a potion) is never left off every tab but All. S toggles to Showing Usable, which drops what the buyer's class mask does not allow (`ShopBuy.CanUse`: the base item's `classMask` against `itemLimits`). Changing the tab, the filter or the buyer rebuilds the list (`FilterStock`, at most `MAX_SHOWN` = 128 items) and keeps the highlight on the item it was on when that is still listed, or goes back to the top; an empty list shows `** NO ITEMS **` and offers Show All and Exit. A line is the price right-aligned in six columns, a blank and the label cut to 27 columns (`RowText`); an item priced 0 still sells for 1, as SSI's list made it.

The list is a `MenuCore.MenuVertical` under the new one-shot flag `MenuCore.lineScroll`: Up and Down (and Home and End, as everywhere) past the box's first or last row move the list a line instead of wrapping within the page, and stop at its ends; Page Up and Page Down still turn a page, but the bottom line offers no Next or Prev. The box's last column holds a scroll bar while the list is longer than the box (`MenuCore.DrawScrollBar`: the thumb `*` in white, the track `-` in dark gray), with a blank column before it, and a page a shorter list would leave part empty is brought up to fill the box. `MenuCore.ScrollThumb` sizes the thumb to the share of the list shown, at least a row, and places it by the top of the window, touching the bar's top only at the list's start and its bottom only at its end.

`movesToCaller` returns each move, and the panel is redrawn for the highlighted item: the party number and name (`CharDisp.NameColor`, now in CharDisp's interface), cut a blank short of `GOLD` in column 18, then the character's gold right-aligned to column 29 and the pool's to column 37, in light green with no commas; `CURR AC  3    DMG 1d8+1` and `NEW  AC  3    DMG 1d6` from column 5 (`DrawFigures`), the armor class right-aligned to column 14 and the damage from column 23; and the readied armor (with the shield at the right edge), weapon and ammunition in light green as the View screen shows gear. The NEW figures come from `ShopBuy.TryOn`, which readies a stack copy of the item on the character as `FxEngine.ReadySet` would make room: what holds its slot or ammunition slot is put away, the first of two rings for a third, then readied items held in a hand, in list order, while the hands it needs are not free. `CharHlp.RecalcStats` gives the figures, then the copy leaves the list, everything put away is readied again and the stats are recalculated; no effect handler runs. The shield is drawn in light red when the hands rule would put it away. `NewTexts` turns the result into the line: an item no one readies (`Wearable` false: a scroll, a potion, a weapon-slot type with no hands) repeats the CURR figures; one whose class mask fails or that a cursed item stands in front of gives `--` for both; arrows or quarrels the readied weapon does not shoot (`CharHlp.FindDamagingRangedWeapon` does not pick the copy) give `--` for damage. A bow readied with no arrows shows its own damage, and arrows that fit show the bow's damage with their plus, as `CharHlp.WeaponAdjust` counts it. Buy (B) or Enter buys through `Shop.BuyItem`, the old Buy loop's body (the scroll's spells first, then the character's coins or the pool). Alt-1 to Alt-8 make member n the buyer (`GameVars.curChar`), where the old list opened member n's items (#1195). Tests: `TShopBuy`. The `shop` regression scenario's stock checkpoint shows the new screen.

`TextRend.ClearScreen` now calls the new `Border.ForgetJoin`: `Border` remembers a corner at the map window's lower-right, (16,16), so that a later edge across that cell puts it back (#1172), and the shopkeeper's screen set it, so the Buy screen's panel edge drew a stray gold corner there after its full-screen clear.

**Appraise leaves no stray corners on its frame (forge #1).** Appraise at a shop's counter (`Money.AppraiseMenu`) cleared only the text window's inside, so the joints of the shop screen's windows stayed on its full-screen frame: top centre (16,0) and both sides of row 16. The new `Border.ClearToFrame` clears the whole screen (forgetting the kept joint, `ForgetJoin`) and draws the full text window's frame. Appraise uses it, and so do the nine places that did `ClearScreen` and `DrawBorder` of the text window by hand: View, More Info, an item's details, Items, Dual Class, the Bestiary, the Companion tools (twice) and character creation.

**F1 help on the Buy and Items screens.** `KeyHelp.ShowPage` shows a table of key lines in one untitled box, rows 1 to 22 so that its bottom edge clears the menu line (the Command Keys window's sits on row 24, under it), with `DrawLine` and `WaitExit` now shared with `ShowKeys`; a line with no key goes on from the one above, and one with neither is a blank row. `KeyHelp.SHOP_KEYS` (the Buy screen's: the moves, Buy, the filter, the six tab keys, Alt-n as the buyer, Exit) and `ITEM_KEYS` (the Items screen's: the moves, the item actions, the weapon-set keys 1 and 2, Alt-n, Exit) hold the text; `TKeyLine` widens to a 9-column key (for `B, ENTER`) and 28 columns of text. `MenuCore.MenuVertical` now takes `helpToCaller` as it takes `memberKeysToCaller`, at entry and for every bottom-line wait, and returns F1 as `C_KEYS.F1`. `ShopBuy.BuyScreen` and `ViewChar.UseItemMenu` set it and redraw after the window. The Command Keys window's Alt-1 to Alt-8 lines become one, `ALT-n  View PC n's items (1 to 8)`, as on these pages; with 12 lines it keeps the groups' blank rows even with every line shown. F1 on a menu that does not take it now says "No help on this menu." in place of "Help is on the map and camp menus." Tests: `TKeyHelp` (both tables fit the window and list Alt-n, the weapon sets, the filter and the tab keys).

## 2. Gameplay and rules fixes

This section covers changes to character rules, spells, equipment, training, and other systems that directly affect play.

**Fix does nothing in an anti-magic area.** `Camp.CampFixMenu` heals with the clerics' spells, so where magic does not work it now says "Spells cannot be cast in this area." and nobody rests, as Cast in camp refused already. The anti-magic test, the GBVM's antimagic word, was written out in four places (`Camp.CanDoSpellActivity`, `CharHlp.CanUseItems`, Combat's Cast option and CombatAi's item use); they and Fix now ask `CharHlp.AntiMagicArea`. #877.

**Ability rules run to 25 (Deities & Demigods).** So that the code ports to the later Gold Box games as it is, every ability rule now covers scores up to 25, with the 1st-edition values up to 18 and Deities & Demigods above:
- Constitution hit-point bonus: a fighter, paladin or ranger class now gets the warrior Constitution bonus whatever the character's other classes, as the PHB works the bonus out per class before dividing by the class count; before, `ConHpBonus` and `ConHpTotal` gave it only to single-class warriors. `ClassCal.ConHpPerDie` gives a class's adjustment per hit die for Constitution 3 to 25 (`conBonusTbl` was 3..20, and the warrior extra stopped at +2 for 18): +3 at 17, +4 at 18, +5 at 19..20, +6 at 21..23, +7 at 24..25, as Curse's fix for the same bug does. #616. a43f9c9.
- Thief skills: `dexAdj` ran Dexterity 9..19; 20..25 come from Deities & Demigods. Cleric bonus spells: `AwardWisdomBonus` stopped at Wisdom 17; it now adds `GDConsts.wisBonusSpells`' rows cumulatively from 13 to the cleric's Wisdom (PHB to 18, D&DG 19..25, spell levels 1..7). Clerics here cast levels 1..3 (`Types.CASTER_LEVEL_LAST`), and only those take slots, still only where the level already has some. b0d6276. The running totals for 18..25 at every spell level are pinned in TClasCal for the later games. dd34e93.
- Hit-die floor: from Constitution 19 a hit-die roll of 1 counts as 2, from 21 a 1 or 2 counts as 3, from 23 anything below 4 counts as 4, in any class. `ClassCal.HitDieFloor` gives the floor, and `RollFirstLevelHp` (creation and training) rolls the dice one at a time and raises each to it before the level-1 minimum; below 19 every roll is as before. 9be4553.
- Poison saves and regeneration: `AbilAdj.ConPoisonSave` (+1 at 19..20 up to +4 at 25) is added to the two poison saves the engine rolls (a monster's poison touch and a stinking cloud). `AbilAdj.ConRegenMinutes` gives a point every 60 minutes at Constitution 20 down to every 10 at 25, and `EffConRegen` re-arms at the creature's interval. The Manual of Bodily Health gave regeneration instead of the hit-point change at Constitution 20 or more, so the table above 20 never paid out, and each further Manual stacked another regeneration effect; it now applies the hit-point change at every Constitution and adds regeneration only to a creature without it. e02aa7e.
- Strength and Dexterity's combat adjustments already matched D&DG to 25.
Differs from SSI: SSI's tables stopped at 18 to 20 and multi-class warriors lost their bonus. #646 (PR).

**Ability scores stay within 3..25 in play.** `AbilAdj.ClampScore` keeps a score within `SCORE_MIN..SCORE_MAX` (3..25), and every place play changes an ability score goes through it: the Manual of Bodily Health's +1 Constitution, Enlarge and Strength setting and restoring Strength (`EffExtraStr`, `AdjustForNewStrength`), Friends' Charisma and its restore, and mummy rot's Charisma loss. Raise Dead lowered Constitution down to 0; it now stops at 3. The disease Strength drain keeps its own stop at 3, where the creature becomes helpless; creation and Modify keep their own limits; scripts cannot write ability scores. #615. 4ab81cb.

**Keoghtom's Ointment revives only a character the poison killed.** Since the POR-0016 fix the ointment brought back any dead target carrying POISONED. POISONED survives death from any cause, so a character kept up by Slow Poison and then killed by a wound or by bleeding out got up again. The handler now notes whether Slow Poison is still on the target (`SLOW_POISON_END` or `SLOW_POISON_HIT`) before curing, and revives only when it is not, the rule #423 gave the temple's Neutralize Poison. #475 (caused under #16). 5626d78.

**Join keeps a readied stack's Ready.** Joining merged every matching stack into the one Join was chosen on, and that stack kept its own state, so a readied stack merged into an unreadied one lost its Ready (readied arrows 83 joined from an unreadied 20 gave 118 arrows, none readied). A readied stack merged away now leaves the result readied; the count of readied stacks never goes up. #595. cf01d69.

**A random javelin is a Javelin of Lightning one time in five.** `GenerateRandomItem`'s javelin case took word index 144 of the random-item templates one time in five. Templates start at 8n - 7, so 144 straddled the end of template 18 (Bag of Holding) and the start of 19 (Cloak of Elvenkind), and the javelin was named from neither (name ids 0, 229, 167, weight 88, value 25, junk spells). It now takes template 14 (index 105), the Javelin of Lightning, which nothing had selected. POR-0165, #638. f4a2cb0.

**Camp's two rests set the rest clock the same way.** The memorize and scribe rest (`CampRestMenu`, SSI's code) put every whole hour into the rest clock's hours with days 0, while the Fix rest split its minutes into days, hours, turns and minutes; a memorize rest past a day showed 24 or more hours until the countdown carried them. Both now start the clock through `Clock.SetFromMinutes`, so the Rest Time line is always in range; the time rested is unchanged. #644. 99753e5.

**Share pays out only what the pool holds.** See section 3 (POR-0151).

**Racial level caps read from a table; a level at or past the cap counts as capped.** `CharHlp.AtLevelCap` now reads the racial caps from a table by race and Strength band. SSI's tests matched some levels past a cap and not others, which only a character over its cap (after a Strength drain) could reach; at or past the cap now always counts as capped. TCharHlp checks the table against SSI's tests for every race, class, Strength and level. f8bcdf0.

**Scribing needs magic-user levels.** Clerics, fighters, thieves and the rest were told they had no copyable scrolls. A character with no magic-user levels is now told "Scribing a scroll requires arcane training." in the message window. #671 (open PR #673). 97f6859.

**A cleric cannot be true neutral; a cleric/thief takes only what both classes allow.** The 1st-edition Players Handbook (p.13) bars clerics from true neutrality, but SSI's `GDConsts.alignByClass` offered all nine alignments to the cleric and to every cleric multiclass. The cleric, cleric/fighter, cleric/fighter/magic-user and cleric/magic-user rows now list eight, without TN. Cleric/thief, which only half-orcs can be (and the race menu does not offer half-orcs, as in SSI's game, so the row waits for them), takes the intersection with the thief's row, as fighter/thief, fighter/magic-user/thief and magic-user/thief already did in SSI's data: LN, LE, NG, NE, CN and CE. Cleric/ranger keeps LG, NG and CG; druid and monk, never offered at creation, keep SSI's rows. The alignment menu is built from the row's count, so nothing else changes. Alignment is a plain byte in the save and is not checked on load, so a true neutral cleric made before this keeps its alignment (no migration). TClasCal checks that no class with a cleric part lists TN and that every multiclass a race is offered lists only alignments each of its classes allows. Differs from SSI: SSI's Pool let a cleric, or a cleric/fighter, cleric/fighter/magic-user or cleric/magic-user, be created true neutral, and its table gave the cleric/thief all nine alignments. #202 (PR #687). 44060d9.

---

**Class ability minimums follow the PHB.** `GDConsts.classMin` gave every class Charisma 0, cleric, magic-user and thief Constitution 0, paladin and ranger Dexterity 0, and the multi-class rows only their 9s, so with the racial floor of 3 a Charisma of 3 to 5 was allowed. `classMin` now holds the eight single classes with the 1st-edition PHB minimums (Charisma 6 for every class, 17 for the paladin and 15 for the druid; Constitution 6 for cleric, magic-user and thief; Dexterity 6 for paladin and ranger; and the rest), and `ClassCal.ClassAbilityMin` gives a multi-class character the highest minimum of its classes (`GDConsts.classParts`). `ClampToLimits` and `StatMinimum` use it; the table is 54 bytes smaller. TClasCal checks it. SSI's code. CURSE-0058, #740.

**Thief skills need leather or no armor and no shield.** Nothing tested readied armor or a shield, so a fighter/thief in plate with a shield kept full thief skills. `ClassCal.ArmorBarsThiefSkills` is true for body armor other than leather or any shield, and `RecalcThiefSkills` then sets all eight skills to 0, so every reader (picking locks, ECL CHECK PARTY, `SpecialReadZ1`) sees it. `RecalcStats` now calls `RecalcThiefSkills` for characters with thief levels, so readying armor or a shield or a Dexterity change updates the skills. The More Info Thief page keeps the parts and adds "Armor or shield bars these skills". `CbtActs.IsBackstab` uses the same test, so a shield now also stops a backstab. TClasCal checks it. Differs from SSI: SSI never limited thief skills by armor. CURSE-0068, #743.

**Demi-human fighter limits use the character's own Strength.** `CharGen.TrainingMenu` had its own copy of the racial caps, tested with "=" against `strCur`, which Gauntlets of Ogre Power, Enlarge and Strength raise and disease lowers: a gnome fighter in the gauntlets trained past 5, and a dwarf fighter 8 below Strength 17 matched no branch and trained to 9. It now calls `CharHlp.AtLevelCap` (">=" bands), which bands on the new `CharHlp.PermanentStrength`: the restore value kept in the Enlarge or Strength effect whose strength is below $80, or `strCur` when there is none, less any disease loss. `StrengthModifier` and `StrengthEffectToStrength` move from FxEngine to CharHlp for it. TCharHlp checks it. SSI's code. CURSE-0315, #702.

**Experience is cut only when the character trains.** `TrainingMenu` cut experience to one below the level-plus-two threshold before the hall's checks and before "Do you wish to train?", so No, Escape, "We don't train that class here" and "Not Enough Experience" all kept the cut. It is now made after Yes, with the fee. SSI's code. CURSE-0313, #701.

**Free training stops when no class can train.** With free training on (STING, J) and every class at its limit, `TrainingMenu` reached Yes/No with a mask of 0, and `GainLevels` added 1 hit point per Train with no level (hpMax a Byte, so it could wrap). It now says "No class can train further" and stops. The creation hang in Curse's record does not apply to PoR. SSI's code. CURSE-0317, #703.

**Starting Constitution follows the DMG's aging table.** `CreateCharacterMenu` lowered Constitution at mature, middle-aged and old but never gave the young adult's +1, so every age ended one low. The +1 is added first, as in Curse. SSI's code. CURSE-0318, #704.

**Temple Raise Dead: the survival roll, and no raising a poisoned corpse.** `Temple.RaiseDead` always succeeded and quietly removed the poison from a poisoned corpse. A corpse still poisoned is now refused, uncharged, with "is still poisoned." (PoR #423: Raise Dead on a poisoned character normally fails; Neutralize Poison first, which revives a character the poison killed). Otherwise the raise rolls d100 against the new `AbilAdj.ConResurrectionSurvival` (PHB 40% at Constitution 3 up to 100% at 18, and 100% to 25), and a character who fails "does not survive." and becomes `C_STATUS.GONE`; the fee is spent. `ConfirmCureCost` is split into `PayCureCost` and `ReportCure` so a failed raise is not reported cured. TAbilAdj checks the table. Differs from SSI: a raise can now fail for good (Curse leaves a failed character dead, to be paid for again). CURSE-0168, #751; CURSE-0232, #752.

**A paladin's protection from evil has its 10-foot radius.** Creation gave paladins effect 8 (Protection from Evil), which covers its holder only. `CreateCharacterMenu` now gives the permanent effect 45 (Protection from Evil 10' Radius), which `FxEngine.TriggerEffect` already lends to adjacent creatures, and `SaveLoad.LoadChar` turns an existing paladin's permanent effect 8 into 45. SSI's code. CURSE-0066, #742.

**Weapon damage types follow the PHB.** The shipped ITEMS file gives the battle axe the piercing type and the bec de corbin, military fork, partisan, ranseur, spetum, short sword, trident and arrow the slashing type, which the half-damage-by-type effects (94, 115) read. `CharHlp.FixWeaponClasses`, run by `GameInit` after ITEMS is read, sets them by the 1st-edition types (the bec de corbin pierces, as the lucern hammer does; weapons that both pierce and slash stay slashing). The `WPN_` constants move from EffHndlr to `C_Item`, with names for the items. TCharHlp checks it. SSI's data. CURSE-0082, #746.

**Launchers add their plus to hit only.** `CharHlp.WeaponAdjust` added a bow's or crossbow's own plus to damage as well as to hit. It now finds the ammunition (`FindDamagingRangedWeapon`): the launcher's plus is to hit, the arrows' or quarrels' to hit and damage, and other weapons keep their plus on both. (Base item 5, the bill-guisarme, carries the arrows bit; readied arrows no longer add their plus to it.) TCharHlp checks it. SSI's code. CURSE-0071, #744.

**Protection from Normal Missiles against magical missiles.** `EffProtMissiles` stopped a non-magical missile and did nothing against a magical one. A magical missile at range now does 1 less per die, no die below 1, as the PHB spell. The per-die cut is `Dice.ReduceDamagePerDie`, which `EffRingFireRes` (2 per die) and `EffEfreetiFire` (1 per die) now share in place of their own loops. TAtkRoll checks it. SSI's code. CURSE-0079, #745.

**Only Large monsters take vs.-Large damage.** `CbtActs.DoAttack` used a weapon's damage against large targets for a defender with the Large bit or a two-tile icon, so the wolf, wild boar, basilisk and large scorpion took it. It now tests the Large bit alone (`Types.ICON_LARGE`), and DataPat sets the bit on the two-tile monsters that are Large in the Monster Manual (see section 7). SSI's code and data. CURSE-0091, #748.

**Dwarves and gnomes against ettins.** `GDConsts.giantFoes` lacked ETTIN, so a dwarf or gnome got no -4 against it. Added. SSI's data. CURSE-0118, #749.

**Rear AC keeps a Dexterity penalty.** `CharHlp.RecalcStats` built `acBehind` without the Dexterity adjustment at all, dropping a penalty (Dexterity 1..6) with the bonus. A negative adjustment is now added; the bonus and the shield still do not count from behind. TCharHlp checks it. SSI's code. CURSE-0064, #741.

**Curing a disease gives back the Strength it took.** `EffDiseaseStr` lowered `strCur` an hour at a time and kept no count. The disease node (effect 43) now counts the points taken in its strength's high nibble (up to 15), with the caster level, capped at 15, in the low one, and when a cure removes it (`Magic.RemoveDiseaseEffects`, `FxEngine.RemoveDiseases`: Cure Disease, Heal, camp Fix, the temple, a paladin) the handler adds them back. The Strength that Enlarge, Strength and the gauntlets store no longer includes the loss (`AdjustForNewStrength`, `EffExtraStr` take it out and put it back), so one of those ending mid-disease no longer undoes the loss. `CharHlp.DiseaseStrLoss` reads the count. TFxRemov checks it. SSI's code. CURSE-0333, #705.

**Haste and Slow cancel everywhere.** `EclMenu.MoveExtremes` let Haste win over Slow, and `ViewChar.PrintCombatStats` halved and then doubled, losing an odd half. `CharHlp.HasteSlowRate` is the one rule (doubled, at most 255, when hasted; halved when slowed; unchanged with both), used by both and by `EffHndlr.EffHasted` and `EffSlowed` for movement and attacks. TCharHlp checks it. POR-0173, #778.

**The prime-requisite bonus reads the character's own Strength.** `ClassCal.ExperienceShare` tested `strCur`, so magically raised Strength earned a warrior the 10% bonus. It reads `CharHlp.PermanentStrength`. Nothing in PoR raises Wisdom, Intelligence or Dexterity by magic. TClasCal checks it. POR-0174, #779.

**Wisdom 13 for a half-elf's cleric multi-class, by race.** The creation roll loop forced Wisdom 13 on every cleric multi-class, and the modify screen never applied it. `ClassCal.ClassAbilityMin` now takes the race: a half-elf cleric multi-class needs 13 (`HALFELF_MULTI_CLERIC_WIS`), a half-orc's the cleric's 9 (PHB p. 20); the roll and the modify screen share it. TClasCal checks it. POR-0175, #780.

**Coins stop at 65535.** `Money.GiveCoins`, `TakeMoney`, `TradeMoney` and `ShareMoney` could wrap a character's Word coin counts. `Money.Takable` (the lesser of `SpareCapacity` and `MoneyCal.CoinRoom` to `C_Money.MAX_COINS`) bounds them, and the rest goes to the pool, whose LongInt counts always hold it. TMoneyCa checks it. POR-0176, #781.

**Dispel Magic leaves diseases alone.** A dispelled Strength drain re-armed itself and took another point. `DispelMagicHandler` skips `C_Effect.DISEASES` (diseased, Strength and hit-point drain, rot), which `FxEngine.DiseaseImmune` now shares; Cure Disease removes them. POR-0182, #787.

**The bill-guisarme loses its arrows bit.** Base item 5 (and unused type 0) carried the arrows bit with no launcher bit. `CharHlp.FixAmmoBits`, run after `FixWeaponClasses`, clears the arrows and quarrels bits on every item that does not fire ammunition. TCharHlp checks it. POR-0186, #793.

**Charm Person and Hold Person take persons, by the 1st-edition list.** Both refused any target with `charType > 1` or a two-tile icon, so gnolls (two-tile) could never be charmed or held, while the one-tile Ogre Leader could. `CharHlp.IsPerson` is one test for both: not undead, and either a name on the 1st-edition list (the first nine `rangerFoes`, kobold to hobgoblin leader and the gnoll, `RANGER_PERSONS`, then `GDConsts.personFoes`: Grishnak and the lizardman) or one tile, not Large, and a player race or charType 0 (the race-0 human NPCs; the drider's race byte is elf, hence the size test). TCharHlp checks it against records from the monster data. Tyranthraxus the dragon is not a person; the human False Tyranthraxus is. See section 7 for the size data. POR-0185, #792.

**Dexterity 0's armor class.** `AbilAdj.DexterityArmorAdjustment` gave 0 for Dexterity 0 and -4 for 1..3; 0..3 now give -4, as `DexMissileAdjust` does. TAbilAdj checks it. POR-0199, #811.

**Random treasure kind 78.** It rolled 1d3+1 against cases 1..3, so the Wand of Lightning (template 7) was never made and a 4 made a bare item. It rolls 1d3. TRandItm checks 300 rolls. POR-0205, #817.

**Shield and Magic Missile immunity stop the wand.** `EffHndlr.EffShield` and `EffImmuneMM` (eff 91) tested `currentSpellBeing` for Magic Missile (15) alone, so the Wand of Magic Missiles (item spell 65, `Magic.WandMagicMissilesHandler`) went through a Shield and hurt creatures immune to the spell. Both test `C_SPELL.MAGIC_MISSILES` (15 and the new `WAND_MAGIC_MISSILES`), and the wand's description says Shield negates it. TShield checks it. POR-0210, #890.

**Spell preparation time.** `Camp.CampSpellPrepTime` summed pending spell levels in Bytes, and scroll levels are unbounded; the counts are Words. POR-0200, #812.

**Raise Dead, scribing and spell failure follow 1st edition.** The owner reversed the earlier "not in the rule book" closures. `Temple.RaiseDead` refuses a dead elf before asking the fee, uncharged, with "RAISE DEAD cannot restore <name>." and "Elven souls lie beyond its reach." (`CharDisp.PrintTwoLines`); half-elves can be raised. A poisoned corpse is refused with "<name> is poisoned, and RAISE DEAD cannot help." (#751). Scribing (`GameTime.CheckScribeDone`, when the scribing completes) rolls d100 against `AbilAdj.IntKnowSpellChance` (PHB 35% at Intelligence 9 to 85% at 18, D&DG 95..99% at 19..23, 100% from 24); a failure shows "<name> failed to scribe <spell>." and "<spell> has been lost." and takes the spell off the scroll. A cleric's clerical spell cast from memory (`Magic.CastSpellNC`, so camp, the combat menu, Quick mode and monsters alike) fails on `AbilAdj.WisSpellFailure` (Wisdom 9 20%, 10 15%, 11 10%, 12 5%) through `ClassCal.SpellFailureChance`, spending the spell with "<name> fails to cast <spell>."; paladins, rangers and scrolls are not subject, and every monster with cleric levels has Wisdom 14 or more. TAbilAdj and TClasCal check the tables. Differs from SSI. POR-0061, #61; POR-0059, #59; POR-0060, #60; CURSE-0168, #751.

**The javelin in melee.** The shipped javelin ($1A) was a launcher with no thrown bit, so a character could not strike with it next to a foe and the AI never chose it for melee. `CharHlp.FixAmmoBits` makes it a thrown weapon ($16), which also gives it Strength's to-hit and damage, as the spear and hand axe have. Holy water, oil and boulders stay thrown-only. TCharHlp checks it. POR-0189, #798.

**Snake Charm charms snakes.** It took `charType = 14`, which the data shares between the giant snake and the large and huge scorpions; it tests the giant snake's name (`GDConsts.SNAKE_NAME`). POR-0193, #802.

**Magic Missile's missiles.** `Magic.MagicMissileHandler` fired caster level div 2 missiles, so a 1st-level caster's did no damage and each later missile came a level late. It fires (caster level + 1) div 2, one at levels 1 and 2 and two at 3 and 4, as 1st edition and the rule book give it. POR-0208, #859.

**Silence's saving throw, the same under both compilers.** `Magic.ResolveSpell` read `saveResult` through a Boolean view of the spell table (`saveEffect`). Silence's 3 is neither False nor True: Turbo Pascal rolled the save and then compared 3 with True and found them different, so the save never stopped the silence, while Free Pascal took it as True and let the save negate it. `ResolveSpell` and `HoldPersonHandler` now read the byte against `C_SPELL.SAVE_NONE`, `SAVE_NEGATES`, `SAVE_HALF` and `SAVE_SPECIAL`, a save negating the effect for `SAVE_NEGATES` and `SAVE_SPECIAL` (Silence, as the rule book has it for a creature the spell is cast on), and the Boolean view is gone. #860.

**A recruited NPC is not drained of 255 levels.** Every monster record SSI shipped (all 291 blocks of `MONxCHA.DAX`) holds $FF in `drainedLevels` and `drainedHps` (bytes 116 and 117), filler where a player's record counts its drained levels. An NPC who joins the party is loaded from that record and saved with it, so Repair (#1125) read 255 drained levels off Genheeris and quoted a healthy party 255 Restorations, 1,785,000 gold pieces; Restoration (the spell or the temple's) on such an NPC gave it one hit point and a level it never lost, and Fix took them as drained levels (a scroll of Restoration for each, and a cleric NPC's "level drained" line, `FixPlan.DrainedOutOf`). No character loses 255 levels, so `DiskRec.CharFromDisk`, through which every saved character and every monster is read, takes `drainedLevels` = `DRAINED_FILLER` ($FF) as no drain and clears both bytes: a save holding such an NPC is mended when it loads. `Temple.RepairParty`'s "The party needs nothing the temple can give." now waits for a key (`CharDisp.MessageText`, `MessageWait`) instead of `TextRend.PauseOnly`, which at the fastest game speed cleared it at once; `Temple.Look` is in the interface for the tests. Tests: TDiskRec ($FF read as none, a real drain kept), TTemple (a healthy party, the NPC's record through `CharFromDisk` and `Look`, quotes nothing; one drained level, one Restoration); scenario `repair` (Brian's save from the issue). #1155.

## 3. SSI quirks fixed (and the ones labelled)

The byte-for-byte reconstruction initially preserved SSI's oddities and marked them with `Note:` comments. Phase 3 revisited every one. Harmless leftovers were cleaned up, genuine bugs were fixed, and the few quirks kept at first were repaired in a later pass.

**Quirks without effect removed.** `Portrait.DisplayCharPortrait` built head and body numbers as strings and never used them; `Money.TakeMoneyMenu` counted into `cnt`, never set and never read; `EffHndlr.EffGauntlets` tested `q^.id < $80`, always true for `C_Effect.STRENGTH`, now written as what it came to; `EclMenu.GetEclVar`'s `vHi` held the low byte and `vLo` the high one, now `lo` and `hi`. No behaviour change. f0178a8.

**CGA text colour on repeated glyphs.** `TextRend.RenderChar` mapped a CGA colour through `attrTable` once per copy, so a run of the same glyph drew every copy after the first in another colour. The colour is mapped once. POR-0155, #625. b808e70.

**Restoration in the spell book.** The spell book listed known spells 1..55, so Restoration (56) never showed. It lists all 56. POR-0153, #623. b808e70.

**Plain '2' and '-' during the AI's turns.** During AI turns a plain '2' toggled quick magic and a plain '-' ran the STING victory cheat, as Alt-M and Alt-X do: the extended-key prefix was read and then forgotten. Both now need it. POR-0154, #624. b808e70.

**Share pays out only what the pool holds.** `Money.ShareMoney` divided each coin type by the creatures `PoolMoney` empties (npc 0 or an animated party member) but paid a share to every creature without `C_Npc.FLAG`, hired NPCs too, so with a hired NPC more went out than the pool held. The divisor (`CountSharers`, was `CountPoolers`) now counts the creatures that are paid. POR-0151, #621. 74825ad.

**CalcDistance restores the target count.** `CharHlp.CalcDistance` rebuilt the target list to measure, then put the old list back but left the rebuilt list's count. It restores the count too. POR-0152, #622. 74825ad.

**The rest fixed or labelled.** Fixed without effect on play: CombatSt's terrain classes repeated eight values in later case arms that the first arm always took; `DaxIo.WriteChar` indexed the 16-entry CGA attribute table with the whole attribute byte and now maps the low four bits (POR-0156, #626); `GDConsts.insertDiskMsg` is sized by `Types.LAST_DISK`. Labelled "SSI quirk, kept" at the time: the Surprise result, fleeing hostiles' diagonals, `Blit.FillPicRect`'s extra row, the $80 string value's address in `GetEclVar`, and `RobItems`' cumulative weight penalties. No `Note:` comment is left. d9dbbc1.

**The six kept quirks fixed (POR-0159..0164).** Each had been kept only so the port matched v1.3; judged on its merits each is a bug, and all are now fixed. Differs from SSI in each case:
- ECL SURPRISE: both rolls succeeding now stores 3, both sides surprised; SSI overwrote it with 2. POR-0159, #629.
- Fleeing hostiles take the party's facing; SSI's arithmetic turned north into NW and south into SE. POR-0160, #630.
- `FillPicRect` with a negative y fills from row 0 to the rectangle's bottom inside the picture; SSI filled a row more and skipped the bottom check. No caller passes a negative y. POR-0161, #631.
- `GetEclVar` gives 0 for a string value, whose text is in `gbStrings`; SSI read the GBVM at an address made of the packed length and a stale byte. POR-0162, #632.
- ECL ROB: each item's weight penalty comes off the robbery's chance (`CharHlp.TheftChance`), not off the running chance, so heavy items no longer shield the items after them. POR-0163, #633.
- Removing the Gauntlets of Ogre Power takes off the first Strength effect that is not superseded while the wearer is at 18/00; SSI tested the effect id against $80, always true, and could take off a superseded one. POR-0164, #634.
bf00fc8.

**Fatal errors quit to DOS in text mode.** Seven fatal errors restored the screen, printed a message and quit, in three variations. `Blit.RestoreVideoMode` swaps the current and saved modes, so the four that restored twice left DOS in text mode, but `Put8x8Symbol`, `Show3DSprite` and `Load24x24Set` restored once and `ExitGame` then set graphics mode again: their message was wiped and DOS left in graphics mode. SSI's v1.3 has the same sequences. `GameIo.FatalError` sets the starting text mode directly, prints, waits for a key and exits through `ExitGame`; all seven call it. POR-0167, #641. 06edb0d.

**Space in combat treats animated creatures the same from the menu and the AI's turn.** The combat menu's Space put every non-NPC creature back under manual control, animated ones included, while Space during AI turns (`CombatAi.CheckKeystroke`) left animated creatures on quick-fight through `CharHlp.ComesUnderPlayer`. SSI had the same split. Both now call `CombatAi.AllToManual`; Alt-M in both places is `CombatAi.ToggleQuickMagic`. POR-0166, #640. 206471b.

**The temple's "you left some money here" prompt.** The temple's copy of the go-back prompt cleared the window before drawing its question, so the priest's words vanished before they could be read; with `Money.GoBackForIt` shared by the shop, temple and post-combat loot, it now shows both lines. POR-0157, #627. 4d84fd9.

**"will be gone forever".** Dropping an item said "Your Long Swordwill be gone forever"; a space was added (POR-0158, #628, 563481a). That then gave two spaces, because `CharHlp.ItemLabel` already ends the label with one, and the extra space was removed (#655, 1ebfea6).

**A 39-column bottom menu clears column 39.** `MenuCore.HorizontalMenu` blanked the rest of the bottom line only when the query and menu were under 39 columns, so a line exactly 39 long left column 39 as the previous line had it; Camp's Rest menu showed "EXITT". The test is now `<= 39`. The `< 39` is SSI's own, in the v1.1, v1.2 and v1.3 sources. #502. 9c30121.

**A random Potion of Extra Healing is named in full.** `RandItem`'s template 1 had SSI's name parts (187, 167, 64), "Potion of Extra"; every Potion of Extra Healing placed in the data, and Curse's and Gateway's templates, have (185, 187, 64), "Potion Extra Healing", which the template now has. Found while building the magic shop, which sells it. #1164.

**Darts +1 in a stack.** `CharHlp.ItemLabel` gives the plural s to the first name part of an arrow or quarrel stack; darts fell to the general rule, which puts it on the second part of a two-part name, so a stack of Darts +1 read "5 Dart +1s". Darts now join arrows and quarrels: "5 Darts +1". Plain darts, one name part, read "Darts" as before. TMagShop checks the label. #1165.

**"Potions of Healing", and Fix names the item once.** The plural rule SSI gave `CharHlp.ItemLabel` put the s on part 1 of a three-part name, so a stack of potions read "2 Potion of Healings", rings and the rest of the "X of Y" names the same; only the Flask of Oil was special-cased, to "Flasks of Oil". Any name whose part 2 is "of" (`C_ITEM.NAME_OF`) now takes the s on part 3 when that is shown, which covers the flask (its parts are 172, 167, 99), so its special case is gone: "2 Potions of Healing". The name parts and the plural moved into the new `CharHlp.ItemName(item, count)`, which gives just the shown parts, plural for count, with no marker, count or trailing space; `ItemLabel` is the marker, the count and `ItemName` for the quantity, then a space, as before. Fix's report took the item's label, so it read "SHADOW USED 2 POTION OF HEALINGS   ON MORRIGAN" for one potion of a stack of two; it now uses `ItemName(item, 1)`. TCharHlp checks the potion at one and two, the label, a partly identified stack, the flasks and darts. #1170.

**Fix's report lines start with "Fix:".** The five lines `Camp.CampFixMenu` notes for what Fix used ended "... during Fix.", which ran long in the message window; they now start with it: "FIX: MORRIGAN LAID HANDS ON SHADOW.", "FIX: MORRIGAN LAID ON HANDS.", "FIX: MORRIGAN CURED SHADOW'S DISEASE.", "FIX: SHADOW USED POTION OF HEALING ON MORRIGAN." The drain lines, "Fix used more than this list shows." and the nothing-done messages are unchanged. #1171.

**The party list is current while Fix's report is up.** `Camp.CampFixMenu` healed everyone, then showed its report, and redrew the party list and the time (`CharDisp.DisplayParty`, `PrintMiscInfo`) only after it. A report longer than the message window waits for a key between pages, so for as long as each page was up the list showed the old hit points of anyone healed by a Potion of Healing (`Magic.SpellPotionOfHealing` doesn't redraw) or by the clerics' Cure Light Wounds (`FxEngine.RestoreHitPoints`, called directly), and the clock the time before the rest; laying on hands and the scrolls' cures redrew through `CharDisp.PrintHpRestoreMsg`. The list and the time are now redrawn just before the report, and again after it, since a member raised by Animate Dead may slip away (#1190).

**The map window keeps its lower-right corner.** `Border.DrawBorder` puts a corner symbol at a window's corners and edge symbols along its sides, and the last border drawn wins where two meet. The wilderness drew the message window last, so its top edge wiped the map window's corner at (16,16); the 3D and camp screens drew it in the right order, but a message window opened alone (`CharDisp.OpenMessageWindow`, Cast at camp) wiped it the same way, as after Rest in camp. `Wilds.DrawWildWindows` now draws the message window first, as `CharDisp.DrawNoncombatWindows` does, and `Border` remembers a corner drawn at (16,16) until a `ClearBox` covers that cell (a full-screen window, the combat windows): an edge drawn across it while it is remembered puts the corner back (the new `Border.PutCorner`). The plan put the corner back in `OpenMessageWindow` and Cast by interface state, but the temple's own screen opens bordered messages under the shop state with no map window, where it would have drawn a stray corner. Checked on screen: the `wilderness` scenario in EGA and VGA, `camp` after Rest, and no change at (16,16) in combat, banetemple, shop, vault, loadsave and journal. #1172.

**The out-of-memory save.** See section 1 (POR-0168).

**A random javelin.** See section 2 (POR-0165).

The picture cache's disk-blind key (POR-0134) is in section 9.

---

## 4. Combat and the combat AI

Here are the changes to targeting, movement, spell use, free attacks, and the decisions made by monsters and party members under Quick mode.

**The combat AI walks round walls and bodies to its target.** The AI headed straight at its target each step, falling back on a few fixed side-steps, so a wall or a dead end left it strafing back and forth. `Pathing.RouteStep` now finds, breadth first over the arena, the first step of a shortest walk that brings the creature's body next to its target's: a cell is open when it is on the arena, not a wall and no other creature's (`CbtMap.OccupantAt`), for bodies of every size, with the steps nearest the heading tried first. The AI takes that step when it can pay for it (`CanStepDir`, split out of `AiCanStep`); otherwise, and when fleeing, it moves as before. TPathing checks routes on open ground, round a wall, out of a dead end, through and blocked gaps, and for a 2x2 body. Differs from SSI. #668. d184cc3.

**A party member under Quick bandages a dying teammate.** As in Curse, the AI's move and attack loop starts, for a party member, by bandaging the first dying teammate, which ends its turn. `Combat.BandageDying` moves to Combat's interface. Differs from SSI. #654. ae01b88.

**A hit that interrupts a spell takes it from the caster.** When a creature casting a spell with a casting time is damaged, the game prints "<name> lost a spell" and removes the spell. `CharHlp.RemoveSpell` took only the spell id and cleared it on `GDVars.curChar`, which at both interruption sites is someone else (the attacker in `CbtActs.ProcessAttackResult`, the caster of the damaging spell in FxEngine's damage path). So the interrupted caster kept the spell and the attacker lost a copy. `RemoveSpell` now takes the creature, as in Curse, Secret and Pools. POR-0148, #540. 7ea2a30.

**An AI caster hurt by its own delayed spell keeps its other copies.** `CombatAiControl` resolved a spell begun earlier with `spellCast` still set, which `EndActions` cleared only afterwards, so an AI-run caster caught in its own spell's area counted as interrupted and lost a second memorized copy. The AI path now copies and clears `spellCast` before it casts, as the manual path does. POR-0149, #556. f248c91.

**An AI caster with a pending spell resolves it before anything else.** `CombatAiControl` tried `SelectItemToUse` before the pending spell, so an AI-run caster could use an item instead, and the spell was dropped yet stayed memorized. The pending spell now comes first and is the whole action, as on the manual path. POR-0150, #557. c89d49e.

**Under Quick, the bottom line shows how to leave it and whether its magic is on.** While any of the player's characters fights under Quick, each AI turn shows "Quick: On (Space) Magic: On/Off (Alt-M)", labels in light magenta and the rest in white, and the moves-left prompt no longer writes over it. Alt-M redraws the line; with no one under Quick it still says Magic On or Off. #665. c06be1b.

**Monsters strike the creature they closed on.** `CombatAi.AiMoveAttack` dropped the creature's target lock when the target was out of action or had `hostile = 0`. A monster's lock is always on the party, so it was always dropped, and the attack fell through to a 1dN pick among the enemies in reach. The test is now `hostile = creature^.hostile` (a lock on its own side), as `AcquireTarget` has it. SSI's code. CURSE-0286, #715.

**A free attack on a creature stepping away is one attack, at no cost.** `CbtActs.MoveOpportunityAttacks` gave each enemy left behind a `CombatAttack`, which runs every attack the enemy has left and then ends its turn (`EndActions`), so stepping away from an enemy that had not acted used up its round. It now allows exactly one attack in the chosen phase, and puts back the enemy's attacks, phase, initiative, movement, pending spell and guard once it has struck, if the enemy is still up. SSI's code. CURSE-0279, #711.

**Escape after a bump attack undoes only the later steps.** `Combat.MoveMenu` saved the movement, facing and start cell on entry, and Escape restored them even after an attack made by moving into an enemy, so a fighter could attack, press Escape and move again with its full move. An attack that is made now takes the current values as the new starting point. SSI's code. CURSE-0280, #712.

**A cursed missile weapon no longer hangs the combat AI.** With a pure missile weapon and an enemy adjacent, `AiMoveAttack` re-equipped and returned with the creature still able to act. `EquipWeaponAndShield` never swaps a cursed weapon, so `CombatAiControl`'s loop called it again for ever. When the weapon is still a pure missile weapon after re-equipping, the turn now ends (`FinishRound`). SSI's code. CURSE-0282, #713.

**A hostile cleric heals only its own side's dying.** `CbtActs.FindCureTarget` tested the side for living candidates but not for the downed bodies, and `downed` holds the party's. So an enemy (or charmed) caster with Cure Light Wounds healed a dying party member. The body must now be of the caster's side. SSI's code. CURSE-0285, #714.

**The AI's item ratings stay inside the spell table.** `CombatAi.SelectItemToUse` passed an item's effect, less 23 above 56, to `DecideCastSpell`, which indexes `spellTable[1..67]` without a check. Shipped items use 80..90 (entries 57..67), but any effect above 90 read past the table. Such an item is now not rated, as `SelectSpellToCast` already leaves such spells out. SSI's code. CURSE-0258, #708.

**A hold lets go of a victim that is no longer immobile.** `FxEngine.HoldTarget`, the start of the bite-and-hold and blood-drain handlers, kept the hold until it was removed or either side dropped. A victim that lost `IMMOBILE` some other way was still bitten or drained. The hold is now released when the victim lacks `IMMOBILE`. TFxRemov checks a hold that lasts and one that goes. Latent in PoR (nothing shipped removes `IMMOBILE` early). SSI's code. CURSE-0259, #709.

**Side counts and Animate Dead's side byte.** `CharHlp.CountTeams` and `InflictDamage` indexed `teamCount` (entries 0 and 1) with the whole `hostile` byte. Animate Dead stored the corpse's old side as `hostile * 16 + caster level`, so a caster of level 16 or more carried into it, and `EffAnimateDead` gave the corpse a `hostile` of 2 or more when the spell ended. The level is now capped at 15 and the side read back with `and 1`, and the counts use `hostile and 1`, as `CombatSt` places creatures. TCharHlp checks the counts. SSI's code. CURSE-0261, #710.

**An animated corpse that flees no longer reads its freed effect.** For a corpse leaving combat (`C_STATUS.RUNNING`), `EffAnimateDead`'s `InflictStatusMsg` removes the 0-HP effects again and frees the Animate Dead node, which the handler then read for the old side. The side is now read first. The double free was fixed earlier (`RemoveEffect`, 921147b). SSI's code. CURSE-0326, #716.

**Space and Alt-M in combat.** See section 3 (POR-0166) and the plain '2' and '-' fix (POR-0154).

**Combat slot 0 and the hold effects.** See section 9 (#515, #530).

---

**Penalties lower an attack roll without forcing a miss.** An effect forced a miss by setting `gAttackRoll` to -1, and `AttackRoll` and `AttackRollNoAttacker` took any roll below 0 for that, so a penalty of 4 turned natural 2s and 3s into misses whatever the THAC0. A forced miss is now the flag `GDVars.gAttackAvoided` (set by `EffBlinking`, `EffDisplaced`, `AvoidRanged`), and both routines share `FxEngine.RollToHit`. Script attacks with no attacker needed roll + 60 - THAC0 greater than the AC, not at least it; they now use the same ">=". TAtkRoll checks it. SSI's code. CURSE-0331, #718; CURSE-0332, #719.

**A coughing creature keeps its Shield and Blindness AC.** Under `C_Cond.DEFENDER_AC`, `EffStinkCough` ran after Blindness and Shield and reset `acCur` from a fresh `RecalcStats`. `TriggerEffectsWithCondition` now runs it first in that condition. SSI's code. CURSE-0329, #717.

**A free attack keeps the attacker's sweep.** Checked for #770 (POD-0023): since #711 a free attack on a retreating creature costs the monster nothing, and breath, gazes and other specials (`MONSTER_SPECIAL`, at the start of `AiMoveAttack`) read none of what it changes. `DoAttack` also zeroed `sweepBudget` and set `madeAttack`, which were not put back, so a fighter that took a free attack could not sweep weak foes on its own turn; they are now restored too. #770.

**Wards hold against a monster's touch.** `EffHndlr.PoisonTouch` and `ParalysisTouch` added their effect with a bare `AddEffect` after a failed save, so a paralysis ward (effect 109) never stopped a ghoul's, thri-kreen's or drider's touch, and a poison-immune creature (effects 111, 125) died of a poisonous hit on a natural 1, which skips the SAVING_THROW condition that makes its save succeed. `FxEngine.EffectImmune`, the check `AddEffectWithMessage` made inline, is now shared, and both touches run it (`EffHndlr.TouchImmune`); an immune target "is Unaffected". Magic resistance (105, 106) is part of that check, so it now rolls against these touches too. TFxRemov checks it. SSI's code. CURSE-0086, #767; CURSE-0336, #720.

**Anhkheg acid is acid.** `EffAnhkhegAcid` never set `elementMask`, so the spit took the type of the last damage dealt ("from Fire", with fire protection's save and cut). It now sets `ELEM_ACID`. SSI's code. CURSE-0338, #721.

**Readying a Cloak of Displacement writes through no nil pointer.** `EffReadyItem` called the item effect's handler with a nil node, and `EffDisplaced` wrote its strength at 0000:0003, in the interrupt vector table, on every ready. The handler now gets the node just added. SSI's code. CURSE-0114, #737.

**Monsters' readied items take effect.** `EclMenu.LoadCreatureData` never ran the ready handlers for a loaded monster's readied items. It now calls each readied item's special handler (special above $7F), as `ToggleReadyItem` does, for combat loads and `AddCreatureToParty`. In the shipped data this gives the level-6 magic-user its Cloak of Displacement, Tyranthraxus (17 to 18/00) and the dwarven fighter their Gauntlets of Ogre Power, and the commandant and al-Hyam Dazid their Rings of Fire Resistance. `EffGauntlets` says "is stronger" only for the current character. Differs from SSI: these monsters are now stronger. CURSE-0089, #747.

**Fire protection against Burning Hands.** Burning Hands deals the caster level as fixed damage and rolls no dice, and the per-die fire protections (effects 61 and 113) and the mummy's fire vulnerability (122) worked from whatever die count the last unrelated roll left in `gDamageDice`. `BurningHandsHandler` now sets it to 1, as Curse does. PoR has no fixed-damage fire breath. SSI's code. CURSE-0152, #750; CURSE-0205, #760.

**Each stinking cloud has its own number.** `StinkingCloudHandler` counted the caster's clouds on the way to the list's tail without checking the tail, so a second cloud could share the first's number and `EffCastStink` cleared the older cloud when the newer one's effect ended or was dispelled. `FxEngine.FreeCloudNumber` gives the lowest number no cloud of the caster uses. The cast effect's strength packed the caster level in its low nibble with no cap, so a level above 15 carried into the number; `FxEngine.CloudStrength` stores it capped at 15 (the duration still uses the full level). TClouds checks both. SSI's code. CURSE-0296, #761; CURSE-0357, #762.

**Dispel Magic walks no freed effect.** `DispelMagicHandler` kept the next effect across each `RemoveEffect`, and dispelling Animate Dead kills the corpse, whose 0-HP clean-up (`RemoveEffectsHp0`) can free that node. After a removal the walk goes on only while the saved node is still in the list (`FxEngine.HasEffectNode`, which also replaces `RemoveEffect`'s two copies of that walk and `TimeOutEffects`' nested `InList`). TFxRemov checks it. SSI's code. CURSE-0370, #763.

**An aborted item spell is not spent.** In combat `Magic.UseItemSpell` replaced the cast's result with `EndActions` (always True) before deciding whether to spend the scroll spell, stack or charge, so an aborted cast still used it up. The cast's own result now decides; the turn still ends. As Curse (curse#409) and Secret (secret#359). #697.

**Slow Poison stands up only a downed target.** `SlowPoisonHandler` revived every poisoned target not animated: a standing one "got back up", a stoned or disintegrated one was set Okay, a fled one stood up after combat. It now acts only on a target that is down (`DEAD` or disabled) and not fled, stoned or gone; the revive-or-wake step it shares with Keoghtom's Ointment is `StandUpOrWake`. As Secret (secret#444). #698.

**Monsters reach any party member they can reach.** Checked for #769 (POD-0019): hiding never wins a fight, since morale and flight do not react to the party being out of reach. But a monster with a route could still end up guarding: when its locked target was reachable only through another party member's cell, when one of its own side blocked a corridor, or when the shortest way ran through a Stinking Cloud that `CanStepDir` then refused. `Pathing.RouteStep` now walks to the nearest of a set of goal creatures (`MarkGoals` builds the "next to a goal" grid, at the old cost), may plan through given bodies and keeps off given terrain. `CombatAi.RouteToEnemy` tries the locked target, then the nearest enemy it may attack (which becomes its target), then a walk through its own side's bodies, closing up behind them and guarding when its next step is onto an ally. `AvoidsCloud` is shared by the planner and `CanStepDir`. TPathing checks a shut-in target, the nearer of two goals, a queue in a corridor and a cloud in a gap. #769.

**Protection auras are for the carrier's side.** `FxEngine.TriggerEffect` lent Protection from Evil and Good 10' (45, 46) to anyone next to the carrier, so a paladin's foes were protected too. A carrier now counts only for its own side. Prayer (49) stays lent to all, since `EffPrayer` gives the other side its -1; Silence (21) affects everyone. POR-0177, #782.

**Protection from Evil or Good applies once.** Effects 8 and 45 (and 9 and 46) both triggered. `FxEngine.TriggerProtection` runs the creature's own protection, or failing that the aura, once; `EffectSource` is the search it shares with `TriggerEffect`. And the -2 to be hit never applied: it ran under `DEFENDER_AC`, before `RollToHit` rolled afresh. The protections now run under `ATTACK_ROLL_DEF`, after the roll, for weapon and spell attacks alike, and `EffHndlr.Protection` (shared by `EffProtEvil` and `EffProtGood`) needs the attacker on the other side. TProtect checks all three. POR-0178, #783; POR-0183, #789.

**Displacement.** `EffDisplaced` took round 0 with an attack roll of 0 for its combat-start reset, so a first-round attack modified to exactly 0 reset it instead of missing; the reset is now `EffHndlr.ResetDisplacement`, called for every creature at `CombatSt.CombatStart`. And readying the cloak ran `EffDisplaced` on the new effect, spending the free miss at once; `EffReadyItem` no longer does that for displacement. TDisplac checks both. POR-0184, #791; POR-0179, #784.

**Displacement against the DMG, and the displacer beast against the Monster Manual (#1183).** Checked against DMG p. 140 and the Monster Manual's entry.
