A natural 1 ran no defender effects in `RollToHit`, so the free miss stayed unspent and the next attack was displaced too; the natural-1 path now calls the new `FxEngine.SpendDisplacement`, which sets `C_EFFECT.DISPLACED_SPENT` (the strength bit $10, named after Curse's) on the creature's effect-89 node. `EclVm.EclDamage`'s attack-roll path (a scripted trap or volley) calls `EffHndlr.ResetDisplacement` for every party member first, so the volley's first attack on a cloak's wearer misses however the last combat left the bit. #1136 had granted the displacer beast (MON7CHA block 68) effect 89 in `EclMenu.LoadCreatureData`; the Monster Manual gives the beast only "-2 on opponent's attack dice", not the cloak's free miss, so that grant is gone. Its AC stays SSI's 2 (`acBase` 58), which is the book's AC 4 with the -2 folded in: every attack roll compares against AC, so the odds are the same. Its saves were 9/10/11/11/12, a 5th-6th-level fighter's +2; the book says a 12th-level fighter's +2, and DataPat (data version 46) makes them 5/6/7/6/8. A save whose Bestiary learned "Displaced" for the beast keeps it. TDisplac checks the natural 1; TBeast's displaced creature is a cloak wearer now.

**A paralyzing touch's save modifier.** `ParalysisTouch` was given the drider's -2 and rolled at +0; it now passes it, as `PoisonTouch` does. POR-0180, #785.

**Magic resistance against non-spell effects.** Magic cleared `dsCurrentSpellBeing` after every spell, so a gaze or touch met magic resistance adjusted by `GetCasterLevel(0)`, an arbitrary level. A non-spell effect now meets the listed figure; spells still adjust 5% per caster level from 11 (`BASE_LEVEL`, `C_SPELL.ITEM_CASTER_LEVEL`). TMagRes checks it. POR-0181, #786.

**Loaded monsters' aligned weapons and boulders.** Since #747 `EclMenu.LoadCreatureData` runs readied items' handlers; those for aligned weapons and boulders only check whether the creature may ready the item, and would shock or print. They are skipped. No shipped monster carries one. #788.

**The died count.** In `Combat.EndOfRound`, `Stats.BledOut` sat outside `if overkill > 9` (a missing begin/end), so a dying party member's Died count rose every round. It is inside. #790.

**Start-of-action effects once per turn.** `C_Cond.ACTION_START` effects (Silence, the cough, bite hold, blood drain, charm) ran in `CreatureAction` and again every time `CombatMenu` was built, so several times in one manual turn. They run in `CreatureAction` only. And Alt-R, accepted by the menu but answered by no command, only redrew it; it is now refused like any unbound key (`C_Keys.ALT_R` is gone). POR-0204, #816; POR-0206, #818.

**Combat placement and distances.** `CombatSt.FindPlacement` could test `placed` before setting it, on a first quad partly off the grid, so a creature could be left unplaced. `CbtMap.CalcDistance` read a stale `targetList[1]` when the list came back empty; it searches only `targetCount` entries and returns 255. `Pathing.BuildTargetList` started `bestDist` at 255, so a line of sight that long was never recorded (out of reach on the 50x25 arena); it takes the first line found. TPathing checks the target list. POR-0201, #813; POR-0196, #808; POR-0195, #807.

**Nil checks in combat and loot.** `FxEngine.ConsiderStinkingCloud` passes an explicit nil node rather than whatever the last `HasEffect` left (nil, so nothing changes), and `PostCbt.LootTakeItemMenu` leaves its loop on no item before using one. POR-0202, #814; POR-0203, #815.

**Magic resistance against the charm gaze and touches.** `EffCharmGaze` set the current spell to Charm Person, so magic resistance used the gazer's mage level; it no longer does, and the gaze meets the listed figure as other gazes do (#786). And since #767 magic resistance ran in the touch checks: `EffHndlr.TouchImmune` now marks the check mundane and `MagicResistance` skips it, so a monster's poison or paralysis touch meets wards and immunities only; spell paralysis and the fear auras are still resisted. TMagRes checks it. POR-0190, #799; POR-0191, #800.

**One Prayer per side.** The aura search stopped at the first Prayer in reach. `FxEngine.EffectSource` takes a side filter (`C_Effect.PRAYER_SIDE`), and `TriggerPrayer` runs one Prayer of each side in reach. TProtect checks it. POR-0192, #801.

**The AI's turn guard follows movement.** `CombatAi.AiMoveAttack` stopped a turn after 20 passes, cutting a hasted fast monster's move short; the guard is half the turn's movement plus 20. #803.

**Haste and Slow's re-run.** `Magic.HasteSlowLogic` re-ran its targets' rate effects on a leftover attack count; it sets each target's own first. POR-0188, #797.

**Every death counts.** `EffHndlr.InflictStatusMsg` (poison, a drain, mummy rot) set DEAD or GONE without counting Died; `Stats.Death` (formerly `BledOut`) counts it there too. TStats checks it. #796.

**Aiming an area spell counts who it will catch.** While the player aims a spell of area mode 8..14, line 23 names the spell and counts the creatures it will catch at the cursor, the caster's enemies in yellow and its allies in light cyan, the colours of their names in combat, in Brian's format "SLEEP: 3 OF 2 ENEMIES, 1 ALLY" (`CbtActs.ShowAreaTally`): a side with no one is left out, and "NO TARGETS" stands for both. `CbtMap.RestartSpellPreview` gathers them on each cursor move and hands them to `CbtActs.ReportAreaCatch` through the hook `CbtMap.aimReport` (set by GameInit, as `boltTracer` is). A blast gathers them with `Pathing.BuildAreaList`, which `TargetArea` and `AreaHitsNonEnemy` now use too, over the whole arena; a bolt or cloud counts the creatures on the preview's cells. Only Sleep has a most: `CharHlp.SleepMost` runs `CharHlp.SleepTakes`, through which `Magic.SleepHandler` now spends its pool, over every pool 4d4 can roll, and `CharHlp.CatchText` shows it as "OF n" when it is below the side's count, then the side's noun. No immunity is shown: Stinking Cloud's "(n IMMUNE)" went, and a creature immune to sleep counts as one that may sleep, as it takes its share of the pool. A group a first Sleep has put down counts a most of 0, which the rules give: Sleep passes over the asleep. `FxEngine.CloudProof`, split out on the way, is shared by `ConsiderStinkingCloud` and `CombatAi.AvoidsCloud`. TSleep and TClouds check the rules. #840.

**A silver shield turns a petrifying gaze.** `EffHndlr.SilverShielded` is True for a creature whose shield slot (`C_EQUIP.SHIELD`) holds an item with a Silver name component (`C_ITEM.NAME_SILVER`). `EffPetrifyGaze` asks it after the mirror check, of whoever the gaze then meets: the gaze is lost ("is shielded from it!") with no saving throw. The charming gaze is unchanged. `EffHndlr.HasName` tests an item's name components, and `IsMirror` uses it. TEffHndl checks it. #981.

**The combat AI casts spells where they obviously do something.** The AI chose a spell by random picks over passes of falling `aiPriority`, accepting any offensive spell with an enemy in range, and aimed with up to 20 random picks; since #792 made Hold Person and Charm Person take only persons, auto-combat held scorpions and skeletons, and it silenced kobolds who never cast, and re-cast Bless on the blessed. `CbtActs.SpellCanAffect` now says whether a spell would obviously do anything to a creature, by what could be seen across the table only: its kind (`CharHlp.IsPerson` for Hold and Charm, no undead for Sleep or Cause Disease, Sleep's largest pool through `SleepTakes`, the snake for Snake Charm, `FxEngine.CloudProof` for Stinking Cloud), its visible conditions (under the spell's effect already, or helpless for a spell that leaves it so), and for Silence whether it was seen casting this fight (`CbtActs.seenCasting`, set in `CastSpellCombat`, cleared in `CombatStart`); for the party's caster, the immunities its Bestiary has seen too. Items, saves and resistances are hidden and never count. `CbtActs.BestAim` aims there: an area spell at the enemy whose area catches the most it can affect and no one else, any other at random among those it can affect, passing over the ones picked already for a spell of several targets. `CombatAi.SpellImpact` scores each memorized spell by how many it would affect (a buff, which the AI casts on itself, 1 while the caster is not under it; Cure Light Wounds 1 with someone to cure), an area spell needing 2, and `SelectSpellToCast` draws one at random by impact times `aiPriority`, so the big plays come up most and the AI still fumbles now and then; item spells go through the same score. Both sides use it. `TSpcAff` covers `SpellCanAffect`. #1086, #1087.

**The combat AI's follow-ups from the play-test: Silence, a second Stinking Cloud, the elemental buffs.** Kirben found three wasted casts after #1104. Silence, an area spell (`aiRadius` 1), was held to the floor of 2, so the AI never silenced a lone enemy caster even after seeing him cast; `CombatAi.SpellImpact` now exempts it, since one silenced caster is the point. Stinking Cloud counted every target not `FxEngine.CloudProof`, including creatures already nauseated (`C_EFFECT.HELPLESS`, not the spell's own effect, so the helpless veto missed it) and creatures standing in a cloud that `Combat.EndOfRound` already tries every round, so the live cloud was the best aim there was; `SpellCanAffect` now takes `not CloudSpares and not InCloud` (`InCloud` is `ConsiderStinkingCloud`'s own `ProbeDirection` test). `BestAim` also counted the 3x3 block round the aim, while the cloud fills the 2x2 block with the aim cell at its upper left (`GameDefs.cloudDir`); the new `CbtActs.CloudCatch` counts those four cells, each creature once, skips a cell the cloud cannot fill, and rejects a block holding anyone not an enemy (the radius friend check stays too). Every buff but Cure Light Wounds scored 1 whenever the caster lacked it, so a cleric cast Resist Fire against kobolds: Resist Fire and Resist Cold now score only once an enabled enemy has been seen doing that element's damage this fight. `FxEngine.seenElements`, one byte of `elementMask` bits per arena slot, is marked in `NonWeaponDamage` when the acting creature (`GameVars.curChar`) is on the other side from the victim, saved or not, and cleared in `CombatStart` beside `seenCasting`; it lives in FxEngine rather than CbtActs, as the plan had it, so FxEngine need not use CbtActs. The poison, disease and blood ticks set no fire or cold bit, so they mark nothing that counts. Protection From Normal Missiles scores only while an enabled enemy has a ranged weapon readied (`CharHlp.HasRangedWeapon`), as the #1086 table said. `TSpcAff` covers the cloud rules and `CloudCatch`; the new `TAiScore` covers the buffs and Silence through `SpellImpact`. #1086, #1087.

**The party's AI weighs only items it has identified.** `CalcWeaponPriority` added 8 a plus whether or not the weapon was identified, and scored a cursed weapon 0, so Quick mode picked out unidentified magic weapons and steered round cursed ones the player could not know about. The new `CombatAi.KnowsItem` is true for an identified item (`unidMask` 0), or for a creature not on the party's side, whose monsters know their own gear. A weapon the creature does not know is scored as the mundane weapon: no plus, and none of the aligned-weapon, Javelin of Lightning or curse vetoes, which only identifying shows; an unknown shield scores as a mundane one (1); and `SelectItemToUse` passes over an unidentified item, whose spell the player could not choose it by. The AI chooses no armour, so nothing else read hidden data. `TAiScore` covers it. #1143.

**AI casters cast nothing in an anti-magic area.** The Cast menu is not offered in an anti-magic area (`CharHlp.AntiMagicArea`), and `SelectItemToUse` already checked it, but `CombatAi.SelectSpellToCast` did not, so enemy casters and Quick mode cast in the Lizard Men's keep. It now builds no spell list there. Magic items and armour in an anti-magic area are a separate question, still open. Refs #1120.

**Allow Gear Changes.** Under Quick mode `CombatAi.EquipWeaponAndShield` re-equips the best-scoring weapon and shield at the start of every AI melee turn and when a bow-armed character has an enemy beside it, taking the ranged weapon when no enemy is adjacent, and nothing put the old weapon back after the fight: a ranger readied with a two-handed sword fought, and started the next fight, with his long bow. The new setting Allow Gear Changes (`C_OPT.OPT_GEAR`, POOLOPT.CFG `GEARCHANGES`, `GameVars.gearChangesOn`, on by default), under the Quick Mode heading after Use Magic, turned off makes `EquipWeaponAndShield` return at once for a creature that `CombatAi.KeepsGear`: one that comes under the player (`CharHlp.ComesUnderPlayer`), so NPCs, animated dead and monsters re-equip as before. One guard covers both callers; a character holding a bow with an enemy adjacent then ends its turn, the path #713 made for a cursed bow. `EquipWeaponAndShield` moves to CombatAi's interface. Tests: `TGear` (the guard, the sword kept with a better bow packed, NPCs, animated dead), `TCfgFile`. #1127.

**Weapon sets.** Joonas's idea (#1209), from Gold Box Companion. On the Items screen `1` and `2` tag the highlighted weapon, shield, arrows or quarrels for set 1 or 2 (`ViewChar.TagSet`; the same key again clears it, and `CharHlp.SetTaggable` refuses anything else with "Weapons and shields only": a weapon is an item of slot 0 with a hand or two, as ITEMS gives some unused types slot 0 and no hands). A tagged item's line ends in its set's digit in column 38 (`CharHlp.SET_LABEL_COL`), under a `Set` heading, the name cut to make room, and the item line ends in ` 1 2` on such an item when it fits with Exit; the keys work either way. Not at the vault or a shop's counter (`ViewChar.TagsSets`). In combat `1` and `2` ready the set (`FxEngine.ReadySet`): every readied item of the other set is put away, then whatever stands in the way of an item of the set (its slot, its ammunition slot, the hands it needs), and each item of the set is readied with `ToggleReadyItem`'s checks, now `FxEngine.ReadyCheck`; untagged items not in the way stay, a cursed one stays ("It's Cursed"), the first refusal shows its message, and an empty set says "No set 1". As Brian decided, a swap costs what readying through Use costs, nothing, the attacks left rechecked (`CbtActs.RecalcAttacksLeft`); the 1st-edition DMG gives no time for changing weapons that we found (unverified page by page). `Combat.CombatMenu` adds ` 1 2` to its line for a creature with a tagged item when it fits (it does not with Move, Cast and Turn all offered) and answers them as `READY_SET_1` and `READY_SET_2`, since `'2'` is Alt-M's scan code; KeyHelp lists `1, 2` "Ready Set (Combat Only)" on every menu, in the function keys' group (`KeyHelp.KeyGroup`, so it costs no blank row of its own), and its window is a row taller (`BOTTOM_ROW` 23, 19 rows for lines), so that with every line shown all 18 fit once `GroupGaps` (#1210) drops the blank rows, with a row to spare for F4. `MenuCore.MenuHorizontal` under `reinterpret` turned every digit into a keypad scan code, even one that was an option's hotkey or in `extraKeys`; a digit that is either now comes back as itself. That gives the Items screen's 1 and 2 too, where `MenuVertical`'s keypad 2 and 8 had moved the highlight; the arrows still do. The tag is `TItemRec.weaponSet`, past SSI's 63 bytes (in the variant part, as a field cannot follow it; the scroll's three spells never reach it): `DiskRec` packs items to `weaponSet` and reads it as none, so the .itm files and the vault stay SSI's byte for byte, and the `.chx` file, now version 5 (`CharExt`; version 4 and older still read, with no sets), keeps each item's set by its place in the list; `CharExt.HasExt` counts a tagged item, and `ReadExt` sets the items' tags, so it runs after the items are loaded, as `SaveLoad.LoadChar` already called it. The tag follows the item through Trade and Halve (whole-record copies); Join gives an untagged stack the joining stack's set; a deposit in the vault, an item from a monster's loot and an item taken from the loot lose it (a thrown weapon handed back by `CbtActs.ReturnThrown` keeps it). Quick mode is unchanged. Tests: `TWeapSet` (what takes a tag, the toggle, the label and line, `ReadySet` with set 1 and 2, an untagged dagger and shield in the way, a cursed sword, an empty set, Join, the 63-byte disk form, the `.chx` at version 5 and 4), `TRecLay`, `TKeyHelp`, and the `weaponsets` scenario. #1209.

**Next and Prev in the aim menu cycle the enemies.** `CbtActs.CombatAimMenu`'s Next and Prev stepped through every creature `Pathing.BuildTargetList` found, nearest first, the party included, skipping only those `CanTarget` vetoed, and the aim started on the nearest creature, normally the attacker. As in Pools, `CanTarget` now takes `allowAlly`: without it a creature of the attacker's own side (`hostile` the same) is refused, the attacker itself always allowed; every other caller, Manual's cursor among them, passes True. The aim list keeps the creatures `CbtActs.AimCycles` passes: the attacker and, with the new setting Aim Cycles at ENEMIES (`C_OPT.OPT_AIM`, POOLOPT.CFG `AIMCYCLES=ENEMIES` or `ALL`, `GameVars.aimEnemiesOn`, ENEMIES by default; `CfgFile.FlagValue` names its two values), the enemies it may target, or for a spell that is not offensive (`spellTable` `offensive` 0, the new `allies` parameter, passed by `SelectSpellTarget`) its own side, which Pools does not do; ALL keeps everyone, as SSI did. The aim starts on the attacker's current target (`combatAddr^.other`) when the list holds it on the side wanted, else on the nearest enemy, or for a spell that is not offensive on the caster. Manual and the cursor keys still reach anyone. Differs from SSI. Tests: `TCbtActs` (`CanTarget`'s flag, `AimCycles` both ways and for both sides), `TCfgFile` (the ENEMIES and ALL values). #1137.

**Thrown weapons go back to their throwers.** A single thrown weapon (hammer, axe, spear, javelin) dropped into the loot as it was thrown (`CbtActs.MakeAttack`), and nothing kept who threw it, so the player took each back by hand. MakeAttack now notes each drop of a party member's with its thrower (`CbtActs.NoteThrown`, up to `MAX_THROWN` = 24 a fight, in memory, as combat is never saved; `CombatSt.CombatStart` clears it). With the new setting Retrieve Thrown (`C_OPT.OPT_RETRIEVE`, POOLOPT.CFG `RETRIEVETHROWN`, `GameVars.retrieveThrownOn`, on by default), `PostCbt.RetrieveThrownWeapons`, after the results and before the NPCs' share whenever the loot will be offered, hands each back with `CbtActs.ReturnThrown`: when the item is still in the loot, the thrower still in the party (alive or not, as PoR's characters keep their items) and able to carry it (`ViewChar.CannotCarryItem`), the copy goes into its items through `CharHlp.AddToInventory` (joining a stack under Auto Join) and is readied when the weapon hand is empty and the hands and class allow; the loot node is freed, `lastMissileItem` moving on past it. Each is told, "Rex retrieves Hand Axe.", three rows apart, with the continue prompt when the window fills and after the last. A stack thrown from is used up as before, its last missile only dropping. The setting's label is "Retrieve Thrown", as "Retrieve Thrown Weapons" does not fit before the value column. Differs from SSI. Tests: `TCbtActs` (a monster's throw not noted, the axe back and readied, a second one back unreadied, a thrower gone from the party, one at the item limit), `TCfgFile`. #1118.

**The combat minimap.** Joonas's idea (Gold Box Companion): the whole 50 x 25 arena as a small map in the combat panel. The new unit `CbtMini` (overlaid) draws it at 2 pixels a cell, 100 x 50 pixels, centred in text cells 24-36 by 15-21, below the panel's name, hit points, AC, weapon and status (rows 1-11). `CbtMini.Build` colours each arena cell: walls (terrain cost 255) `Blue`, terrain 0 (off the arena) and floor the colour `Border.ClearBox` leaves the panel (`Black`, palette index 0), the creatures in play (`creatureArr` with an `arenaLoc` size) over their whole footprint (`Pathing.GetIconTileDelta`) as their names are coloured, those out of action first so the living stand over them: the party `LightCyan`, enemies `Yellow`, out of action `LightRed`, the creature whose turn it is `White`, and the aim cursor's cell `LightMagenta`. `CbtMini.Show` builds the grid and draws it one 8 x 8 text cell at a time through `VidOps.PutPixelRow` and `Blit.ShowPic`, as `BldShow` draws its cells, in every video mode (CGA maps the colours through `GameDefs.attrTable`, so Blue is black there and only the creatures show); its grid and scratch cell are taken from the heap for the drawing only, and it draws nothing without the memory. It draws when `Combat.CombatMenu` and `Combat.MoveMenu` wait for a key, and in `CbtActs.AimTargetMenu` and `ManualAimCursor` with the target or cursor marked; the messages of an action write over it, and the next of those draws it again, so nothing is saved under it. The setting Minimap (F3) on the Combat page (`C_OPT.OPT_MINIMAP` after Retrieve Thrown, POOLOPT.CFG `MINIMAP`, `GameVars.minimapOn`, off by default; Combat Log and Log Lines move to 33 and 34, which POOLOPT.CFG's keys by name leave as they were) turns it on, and F3 at the combat menu (`C_KEYS.F3`, answered as `SHOW_MAP`) turns it over and writes POOLOPT.CFG at once, as Ctrl-S does the Sound setting. The label names its key, as #1215 asks. The Command Keys window (F1) lists "F3 Minimap (Combat Only)"; with every line shown (STING, the Companion Tools, the combat log and a sound device on the map menu) its 17 lines no longer fit with blank rows between the groups, so `KeyHelp.GroupGaps` drops those rows then. On EGA and VGA the combat palette (`CbtMap.SetCombatPalette`) shows index 0 as dark grey and 8 as black; the map's floor is index 0, as the panel's background is, so the two match. Brian chose the blue walls and F3 ("let's see how it looks"). Tests: `TCbtMini` (walls, terrain 0, each side's colour, a 2 x 2 footprint to the arena's corner, the living over the fallen, a slot out of play, the cursor, the layout's pixels), `TCfgFile` (the Combat page's lines, the label, MINIMAP off by default), `TKeyHelp` (F3's line, the group rows), and the `minimap` scenario. #1210.

**A full-width combat view.** Joonas's idea (Gold Box Companion, #1211): the arena across the whole screen, with the creature and the messages on lines under it. Brian chose 13 x 6 squares with three text lines. One window, (1,1)-(38,21), holds the arena on rows 1-18 (13 columns of 24 pixels, the 13th cut to two of its three cells by the window's edge, as #1186's wilderness view) and, under it, row 19 for the creature (`CharDisp.CombatPrintCreatureInfo`: the name in its colour, `HP`, `AC`, then a status for one out of action or helpless, else the readied weapon, two spaces apart and cut to the line) and rows 20-21 for the messages. The plan put the text box at (1,20)-(38,22), whose border would have taken row 23, the line combat keeps for the range, the spell and the aim's catch; the text lines go straight under the arena instead, inside the window's border. The setting is Wide View on the Combat page (`C_OPT.OPT_WIDE_COMBAT` after the Minimap, POOLOPT.CFG `WIDECOMBAT`, `GameVars.wideCombatOn`, off by default; Combat Log and Log Lines move to 35 and 36); no key acts on it, so its label names none. `CombatSt.CombatStart` reads it at each fight. The buffer: #1186's swap of `GameVars.viewBuffer` for a larger one moved out of `Wilds.ShowWide` into the new unit `WideView` (overlaid), `Enter(rows, cells)` and `Leave`, which both views use; combat takes 144 lines by 38 cells (`CbtMap.WIDE_ROWS`, `WIDE_CELLS`) and gives it back in `Combat.CombatDoneCleanup`, the one way out of `CombatMain`, and a heap that cannot spare it gets the 7x7 view for that fight. The wilderness's wide view is narrowed before any script command that starts combat (#1186), so the two never meet. The view's shape is `CbtMap.SetLayout`: `viewLastX`, `viewLastY`, `viewFullX` (the last whole column), `halfX` and `halfY`, 6, 6, 6, 3, 3 or 12, 5, 11, 6, 2, and `CenterX`/`CenterY`, which replace the literal 3s and 6s of `BuildViewBuffer`, `ViewCoordsValid`, the spell preview's area (`AREA_CELLS` 64 to 80, for a blast over the whole view), `CharDisp.CombatAnimateMovingIcon` and the `ox + 3` re-centres in Combat, CbtActs and CharDisp. `BuildViewBuffer`'s band for `ScrollView`'s delta is now `3 - delta` cells in from the view's edges (the last whole column on the right), which in the 7x7 view is SSI's delta cells from its centre; 0 and 255 keep the centre alone. It now always keeps the whole view on the arena, where it used to clamp only when the view moved: the first view of a fight (`CombatSt`'s corner, the first creature minus the half) could start off the arena's edge. `MapTiles.DrawTerrain` clips a tile that runs past the buffer's right edge (the 13th column; the sprites already went through `BlitTileClipped`). The messages: `CharDisp.MsgLeft` gives their first column; under the wide view `PrintNameString` puts the name and the text on one line, a row up to `COMBAT_MESSAGE_TOP` starting a message on the first line and a later row carrying on at the line after the last part (`NextMsgRow`); past the second line the lines are cleared after the text delay, unless the game has waited over its text since they were written (`TextRend.pauseCount`, counted by `PauseOnly` and `DisplayAndPause`), and a part that runs past them waits on the text delay rather than a key (`TextRend.pageByDelay`, set by `SetLayout`). `CbtActs.ProcessAttackResult` goes through the new `CharDisp.PrintNameLine` (the defender and the damage) and `PrintMsgTail` ("and is Dying"), which print as before beside the 7x7 view; `ClearCreatureInfo` replaces the aim cursor's `ClearBox(23, 1, 38, 21)`. So a hit reads "ORC Attacks" over "ANNA and Misses", and "ANDREA creates a poisonous cloud" over "ORC is killed". The minimap (#1210) needs the panel the wide view does without, so it is not drawn there (`CbtMini.Show`), and F3 at the combat menu then says "No minimap in the Wide View" on the bottom line and leaves the setting as it is; the Minimap's POOLCFG text says so. Memory, measured in goldbox-player's 1 MB machine without EMS (STING Ctrl-Y's heap free and largest block, and the System page), against the build before this change: on the System page 176 bytes less (VGA 192,864 free and 143,824 the largest block, against 193,040 and 144,000; EGA 254,480 and 190,392 against 254,656 and 190,568); in the cloudkill fight in VGA 128,968 free with the largest block 98,048, against 144,696 and 142,032 in the 7x7 view, and in EGA 210,184 and 190,392 against 218,136 and 190,568; in `wildfight`'s fight in VGA, with #1218's smaller blocks for the second tile set, 144,048 free and the largest block 55,584, against 159,600 and 99,392 with Wide View off. The buffer is taken from the top of the heap, so in VGA it takes its 43,776 bytes out of the largest block. Back on the wilderness map after that fight (Alt-Q, Continue Battle No, the loot's Exit) the heap is the same with Wide View on or off, 64,848 free with the largest block 38,584, so the view's buffer leaves nothing behind. Tests: `TCbtView` (both shapes, `ViewCoordsValid` at their edges, `BuildViewBuffer`'s corner at the arena's four corners and its middle, the band at delta 3 and 2 with the cut-off column outside it, a corner off the arena brought in, `pageByDelay`), `TCfgFile` (the line, the label, WIDECOMBAT off by default and read), and the `widecombat` scenario. Checked in EGA and VGA captures, the messages from the emulator's video recording: the fight, the settings turned off and on, the aim with Cloudkill's preview and the orc on the info line, the messages, F3, and a wilderness fight. #1211.

**The round stops when the last enemy falls.** `Combat.CombatMain` played every round to its end, so when the last enemy died early in a round each party member still to act had a turn with nothing to fight before `EndOfRound` asked "Continue Battle:". As Pools of Darkness and Death Knights of Krynn do, CombatMain now counts the teams (`CharHlp.CountTeams`) after each creature's turn, and the first time a side is empty (the new `CharHlp.SideEmpty`) it stops the round; `EndOfRound` then runs in full (the clock, round-end effects, bleeding) and asks the question. That happens once a fight: after Yes every round is played to its end and the question comes at its end, as before. A side emptied by the party's fall or flight stops the round too, and EndOfRound ends the fight without asking. EndOfRound now decides `done` from the count itself rather than keeping CombatMain's stop, so a round-end effect that puts a creature back on a side keeps the fight going. Always on, with SSI's question kept. Under NORAND the dice stream changes, since the remaining picks no longer roll. #1194.

**Paladins lead NPCs.** Death Knights of Krynn (a Knight, by order) and Pools of Darkness (a paladin, by level) let each leader in the party roll for each NPC on its side at the start of a fight; PoR had nothing of the kind, so an NPC was always the computer's unless a script handed it over. The new `CbtActs.UseLeadership` runs in `CombatSt.CombatStart` once the creatures have combat records (after placing, before the combat screen is drawn): each paladin who `CanLead` (a player's own character on the party's side with a usable paladin level, `CharHlp.GetClassLevel`, Okay and enabled) rolls d100 for each NPC who `CanBeLed` (an NPC on the party's side under the computer's control, Okay, enabled, Intelligence above 3) and is not led yet, and leads it when the roll is under `LeadershipChance`: `LeadershipBase` by paladin level, 20 at 1 to 3, 40 at 4 to 7, 80 from 8 (Brian's ladder; Pools keys the same three numbers to levels 1, 2 and 3+, which made it 99% for any Pools paladin), plus 5 for each point of Charisma above 13. `TryLead` puts the NPC under the player's control (`quickfight` 0) and marks it in the new `TCombatRec.led`; `PostCbt.PostCombatCreatureCleanup` gives a marked NPC back to the computer after the fight. Unlike Pools, nothing first puts every NPC back under the computer, so an NPC a script gave the player (`CHAR_CONTROL`) stays the player's. As in Death Knights of Krynn, the first success opens a window titled "Using leadership ability", each adds "<paladin> leads <NPC>.", and it waits for Enter; Pools dropped the message. Pools calls it before `CombatStart`, but PoR's combat records are only allocated there. With Game Level Cap on, a paladin stops at 7, so the best chance is 40 + 5 a point of Charisma above 13. No setting: it is a class ability, like turning undead. #1124.

## 5. User interface: menus, screens, keys and messages

These changes make the game clearer and more consistent without changing its underlying rules: better prompts, safer key handling, and screens that expose information the game already knows.

**Menus highlight in inverse video (#1160).** Ported from goldbox-tp7's PoR port (ccee3767, f064fcfb, a7732981). The video drivers drew every glyph on black: `Types.TDrawGlyphProc` takes a paper colour now, `(col, row, fg, bg, glyph)`, and each driver fills the glyph's clear pixels with it (EGA per plane, the set bits from fg's plane bit and the clear ones from bg's; VGA the byte; CGA bg's colour pattern, through `attrTable`, under the cleared bits; Tandy both nibbles). `TextRend.RenderChar` takes fg and bg, and the new `DisplayStringOn(col, row, fg, bg, s)` writes on paper; `DisplayString` and `PrintSpaces` still draw on black. `MenuCore.MenuHorizontal`'s current option and `MenuVertical`'s highlighted line (`HighlightLine`) are drawn black on hlCol, the other options' hotkeys still hlCol on black; `PartyMnu.DrawCmd`'s current command is black on white; `Options.DrawSetting` draws the highlighted line's chosen value (ON or OFF, or the picked one of a stepping line's two choices) black on white, its label white; `ChgClass.DrawClassLines` draws the highlighted class's name black on yellow, its requisite lines still yellow; `CharEdit.DrawLevels` the selected class black on light magenta. Four screens that draw their own highlight, left as colour changes in tp7, match: `JrnlView.DrawLabel` (the Journal list), `BeastMnu.DrawList` (the Bestiary), `SaveMenu.DrawList` (the selected slot's letter and colon) and `GameTime.DisplayRestTime` (the rest timer's selected field), all black on white. SSI's start-up menu, whose bottom-line options are black on black so none shows, is unchanged. TBlit checks, for Tandy and CGA in DOS and VGA natively, that a glyph drawn black on yellow leaves the screen the opposite glyph drawn yellow on black does, and that ink on paper of its own colour fills the cell.

**A menu option lights whole, from its hotkey's word (#1216).** #1198 had `MenuCore.HorizontalMenu`'s `DisplayOption` light only the space-delimited word holding the option's hotkey, right for `coMbat sYstem` but cutting a script option of several words to its first: the bandits' one-option PRESS <ENTER>/<RETURN> TO CONTINUE lit PRESS, and the Nomad camp's ADVANCE CAUTIOUSLY lit ADVANCE (the port's own `C_MSG.CONTINUE_PROMPT` has no hotkey and stayed all inverse, so the same prompt looked two ways). The span is now `StrUtil.OptionSpan`: from the start of the word holding the hotkey to the end of the option's last word, the word before the one holding the next option's hotkey (the last option to the end of the menu, trailing spaces left out; two hotkeys in one word part at the character before the next). A menu without hotkeys still lights all of it. Curse's and Secret's reconstructions invert the option's whole range and never took #1198's rule. Tests: `TStrUtil` (coMbat and sYstem, Advance cautiously and aTtack, the continue prompt, Yes No, trailing spaces, two spaces between options, two hotkeys in a word).

**Shops offer Id on every item again.** #861/#891/#892 offered Id only on an item with a hidden name part (`unidMask`), so that it and Info never both wanted I; only magic items hide name parts, so the menu told which items were magic. At a shop's or temple's counter (`C_IFC.SHOP_TEMPLE`) `ViewChar.ItemMenuLine`, now in the interface, offers Id on every item and Info on none, and I always identifies; an item with nothing hidden gets SSI's "I can't tell anything new about your ..." for its 200 gold. Elsewhere Info is offered as before. Detect Magic in shops is left for later. Tests: TItemMnu. Port bug (caused under #861). #1115.

**The Companion Tools open on the map; Map and Notes are one tool.** F6's Tools screen no longer lists its tools: in a 3D area it opens on the whole area's map, "Companion Tools" centred in the frame's top where the area's name was, the name now heading the legend, with "Tools: Map Character Exit" (in the wilderness, the frame and title alone). Map is the former Notes tool: a cursor from the party's square, moved with the arrows, with "Map: Write Clear Exit" and the cursor's square and note below the map; Exit returns to the Tools menu on the same map. The read-only Map tool is gone. #691.

**The Companion Tools' map was slow where events repeat.** `EvtKind.Kind` worked an event byte's kind out afresh on every call, following each matching dispatch's code for up to 160 instructions, and the map calls it twice for each of the 256 squares and again on every cursor move. In the Temple of Bane (script 24), whose 168 event squares share 8 event bytes with long code paths, the map's 512 calls took about 3 seconds at 40,000 cycles (New Phlan's, 0.33 s). `Kind` now keeps each event byte's answer in a 256-byte table that `Prepare` empties, which brings Bane's to about 0.1 s. `TEvtKind` checks that an answer is kept until `Prepare` and then forgotten. Port-only. #692.

**The Companion's status edit left the character's action state behind.** The character editor's Health tool set `status` (H heals to Okay, S steps through the statuses) but not `enabled`, the in-action flag that the party list's red name (`CharDisp.DisplayCreatureName`), combat placement (`CbtMap`), the combat AI and the effect engine go by. A character healed with it stayed red and was placed as a body in combat; one set down stayed in action. Both branches now set `enabled` to 1 exactly when the status is Okay or Animated, as the temple, the healing spells and `CharHlp`'s damage leave it. The `companion` scenario checks the name red after S and cyan after H. Port-only. #694.

**The Companion's Add tool chooses the bracers' AC.** `CharEdit.ChoosePlus` lets Left and Right set a weapon's or armour's plus by its "+N" name part; bracers are named by the armor class they give instead (RandItem's plus 4, 6 and 8, "AC 6", "AC 4" and "AC 2"), so they were skipped and kept RandItem's roll. They now step through those three from AC 6, with the plus, name part and value (3000 gp a point of plus, as RandItem prices them) set together, and the hint reads "Left and Right change its AC." `C_Item` names the bracers' item type (`BRACERS`, 77) and the three name parts, which RandItem now uses. The `companion` scenario adds Bracers of AC 2. Port-only. #772.

**Up and Down step through the party.** On the start menu only Home and End stepped through the characters added; Up and Down now do the same (#590, bdb935b). Then `Party.SelectPrevNextChar`, which every menu that switches character uses (Camp's menus, the shop, the vault, post-combat and the script menus), takes Up as Home and Down as End; before, Up and Down selected the head of the party. The start menu's own mapping was then no longer needed (#653). Party Order's Place mode moves the selected character up with Up as well as Home and down with Down as well as End (#650). 14829ff.

**Icon editor.** Exit was drawn on the bottom line as one of the editor's own items while keys were read through an empty menu, so E did nothing; the bottom line is now a real `MenuHorizontal` menu with E as Exit's hotkey, cleared while the grid is up (#585, c4897b7). The menu now reads Keep Exit: Keep (K) asks "Is this icon ok?" and ends with the icon as designed; Exit (E, or Escape) ends with the icon as it was when the editor opened, putting back head, weapon, size and colours (#586, 4f38cd6). The character's name at the top of the left pane is cyan rather than item green (#587, 41897c3). When an item has more than 16 options the grid shows "(more)" rows (the 32 weapons are now three pages: 1-12, 13-20 and 21-32); the font has no arrows, so the rows read "(more)" (#589, a75e6de).

**Portrait picker: Keep instead of Okay.** The menu reads Keep, with K as its hotkey; it shows the pick alone and asks "Is this portrait ok?" as Okay did. #588. e0d8e0a.

**Menu prompts and settings notes.** In the owner's batch, the Alter, Camp, Aim, Settings, More and View prompts gain a trailing space, and the STING and NORAND notes on the Settings screen likewise. 038f32e.

**An aligned weapon's shock message in two colours.** `CharDisp.PrintMessageWait` printed the whole message in light green. It now prints through `MessageText`, which shows a part in a colour of its own and carries on where the last part stopped, and `MessageWait`; the shock message (`FxEngine.NonWeaponDamage`, for an aligned weapon its wielder may not use) prints the item and the character's name in light green and "shocks", the damage and "has been unequipped" in white. #637. 3b91dfe.

**Camp's scribe and drop messages end with full stops.** The scribe menu ("has no copyable scrolls", "You already know that spell", "You are already scribing that spell", which read "scibing") and drop menu ("bids you farewell", "is dumped in a ditch", "Breathes A sigh of relief") end their sentences with a full stop. #648. 1ebfea6.

**The campfire shows as soon as camp's screen is redrawn.** The campfire is an animation the camp menu draws while it waits, so after the save menu the "Quit to DOS" prompt sat over an empty, black view. Redrawing camp's screen now draws the current frame at once. #666. 72ba6aa. (Camp had already been changed to redraw its screen after Save, before asking Quit to DOS, in 3b2c103.)

**Escape backs out of choosing who duels.** The three WHO prompts that choose who duels (the training hall's arena, the Pitt's buccaneer, the Lizardman Keep's champion) add Exit: Exit or Escape picks no one and sends the script back to the duel's question. Other WHO prompts still need a character, as SSI's did. #661. c6af0e8.

**Missing data files say which file, and the right drive.** A data file that could not be opened brought up only "Insert disk N in drive X:". `OpenWithRetry` now first shows "Not found: " and the file's full path (a path too long for the line keeps its end), then the prompt. The drive letter came from `gameDir[1]`, which GameInit and the introduction empty while they read from the current directory, so it was a stale byte; `DaxIo.DataDrive` gives `gameDir`'s drive, or the current drive while it is empty. 5f94e63.

**The introduction skips the Brazen Crows picture when RAVFULL.DAX is missing.** RAVFULL.DAX is not one of the game's own files; without it the game stopped at "Insert disk 1" before the title. The picture is now shown only when the file is in the directory the introduction reads from. 4d19483.

**Camp's messages stay without a key.** Fix's four messages (no clerics, no cleric able to heal, nothing left to heal, no healing possible), Scribe's "Scribing a scroll requires arcane training." and "<name> is in no condition to cast any spells / memorize spells / scribe any scrolls" (`CanDoSpellActivity`, also used by Cast outside camp) waited for a key and, for the last two, then cleared themselves. They now print in the message window, clearing it first, and stay there while the menu carries on. `CharDisp.PrintMessage` replaces `PrintMessageWait`, which nothing else used. Selecting another character in Camp's menus (Camp, Magic, Alter, Alter's Character and Party Order's Select) clears the message window, so a message about one character does not stay under the next; `Camp.SelectOtherChar` gathers the five places that selected and redrew the party. #681.

**Escape leaves camp's portrait picker unchanged.** From camp (Alter, Character, Portrait), Escape now closes the portrait picker and restores the head and body the character had, discarding whatever was being tried; before, the picker had no way out but choosing a portrait. During character creation it still has none, so a new character always gets a portrait picked. `CharGen.CustomizePortrait` takes `canCancel`. #686.

**The Journal's menu reads JOURNAL: and offers Quests.** `JrnlView.JournalMenu`'s bottom line had no prompt; it now has JOURNAL: in magenta, as the other screens' prompts are, and a Quests option before Exit. Quests turns to the Quests page (`ShowQuests`): a QUESTS heading over a box the width of the frame, with JOURNAL: QUESTS EXIT below. Its Entries turns back to the Journal as it was (`DrawJournal`, split out of `JournalMenu`, redraws the list and the pane's page), and its Exit or Escape leaves the Journal (#874). Entries and Exit share an E, so `MenuCore.unmarked`, the options with no hotkey, became a set of option numbers (`TOptionSet`) where it held letters. The pane's preview (`TickPreview`, MenuCore's idle hook) is off while the page is up. The mock-up is mocks/jrnl-entries.txt. #852.

**Statistics screen, View menu, Settings screen, Load/Save menu, Fix messages, Journal "(more)", scribing message.** See sections 1, 2 and 6.

---

**Extended keys no longer run treasure, shop and temple commands.** The loot menu, its Take menu and the shop and temple counter (`PostCbt.LootMenu`, `LootTakeMenu`, `Shop.CounterMenu`) ask `MenuHorizontal` to return extended keys, which come back as their scan codes with `ext` set, and the case arms took them as command letters: Delete ($53) as S shared the money, F10 ($44) as D cast Detect Magic (even with Detect not offered, and its spell id unset), F7 as A appraised, Shift-F1 as T took, keypad 6 and 9 as M and I in Take. An extended key now only steps the character (`Party.SelectPrevNextChar`); the letters are taken only without `ext`, and Take, Share and Detect only when offered. SSI's code. CURSE-0275, #730; CURSE-0303, #731.

**Escape at Appraise's Sell/Keep no longer sells.** `Money.AppraiseMenu.SellOrKeep` kept the stone on K and sold it on any other key, Escape included, after the gem or jewelry had already left the pool. The prompt now repeats until Sell, or Keep when someone has room, is chosen. SSI's code. CURSE-0306, #732.

**Other keys keep the selected character.** `Party.SelectPrevNextChar` stepped back on Home or Up and forward on End or Down, and for any other key selected the head of the party. Camp's menus and the script menus pass it every extended key, so a function key, Page Up or Down, Insert, Delete or keypad 3, 4, 6 or 9 jumped to the first member. Any other key now keeps the current character. SSI's code. CURSE-0308, #733.

**Backspace in "How much ...?" erases the digit.** `Money.InputNumberMax` kept a column one past the last digit, so Backspace blanked an empty cell and the deleted digit stayed. The number is now drawn afresh after each change. SSI's code. CURSE-0309, #734.

**Typed text starts after the prompt.** `TextRend.ReadString` moved its column before drawing each character, so the first appeared one column right of the prompt and a 40th went past the line. It now draws, then moves; Backspace moves back, then blanks. SSI's code. CURSE-0312, #735.

**Alt-R is not Ctrl-S.** `GameIo.ReadGameKey` ran the sound toggle (and, under STING, the debug keys) on every byte it returned, and the menus and the AI's key check read an extended key's scan byte with a second call, so Alt-R (scan 19, as Ctrl-S) toggled the sound and rewrote POOLOPT.CFG, and under STING Ctrl-2 (scan 3) quit the game. After a #0 with a scan byte waiting, the next call now returns that byte without the controls (`DiscardPendingKeys`, which reads it itself, clears the flag). SSI's code. CURSE-0346, #736; CURSE-0158, #764.

**Escape in a script's list menu is not a choice.** VMENU (`EclVm.EclVMenu`) stored the menu's index whatever the result, so Escape stored the highlighted line as if chosen. The menu is now shown again until a line is chosen. SSI's code. CURSE-0348, #757.

**Train Character is offered only where training is.** `CharGen.MainMenu` only ever turned the entry on in the persistent `menuItems` typed constant, so after a training hall it stayed on. It is now set on every redraw from the hall's mask or free training. SSI's code. CURSE-0323, #765.

**The Drop message fits for long names.** "<name> breathes a sigh of relief." runs to 41 or 42 characters for a 14- or 15-letter name, past the 40-column bottom line. Those names get "<name> sighs with relief."; shorter ones keep SSI's line. Differs from SSI: the wording for those names. CURSE-0324, #766.

**Dropping money updates the sheet.** View's Drop redrew only the money, leaving the Encumbrance and Movement shown for the old load. It now redraws the combat stats too. SSI's code. CURSE-0311, #700.

**Selling gives the seller only the coins that fit.** When a sale overloaded the seller, `ViewChar.SellItem` said the money went to the pool but still gave the platinum (all of it when it fitted) and the gold. `Money.GiveCoins` (in place of `GivePlatinum`) gives platinum, then gold, as far as `CheckOverload` allows, pools the rest with one message and recalculates the seller; the appraiser's Sell uses it too. SSI's code. CURSE-0307, #739.

**Scribing marks only scrolls.** `Camp.CampScribeMenu`'s marking walk set the pending flag on any item whose spell byte matched, and on a non-scroll item those bytes are its charges, effect or special, which gained 128 while nothing was scribed. It now checks `ItemReadable`, as the other scribe walks do. SSI's code. CURSE-0298, #738.

**A 255-character message ends.** `TextRend.DisplayMessageWindow` counted with Bytes, so after a 255th character the index wrapped and the message reprinted for ever. The counts are Integers. SSI's code. CURSE-0299, #729.

**An arrow key answering a pause does not leak.** An extended key answering `DisplayAndPause` (the last of STING Ctrl-Y's four pages, say) left `scanPending` set after the type-ahead drain ate the scan byte, so the next arrow came back as its scan byte's letter and the menu lost it. `GameIo.ReadAnyKey` reads an extended key whole, as Curse's does (curse#596); `DisplayAndPause` and `DiscardPendingKeys` use it, and `ReadGameKey`'s drains drop an extended key whole. Found by reading the code. CURSE-0371, #768.

**One-key waits read an extended key whole.** DaxIo's `PromptBox`, BldShow's pause, Money's `InputNumberMax`, HeapErr, GameBoot's startup-disk prompt, `GameIo.FatalError`, `TextRend.ReadString` and SaveMenu's description entry read with `ReadGameKey`, so an arrow or function key left its scan byte for the next menu. They use `GameIo.ReadAnyKey` (MenuCore and the AI's key check still need the scan byte). POR-0172, #777.

**Party paging with no character selected.** `Party.SelectPrevNextChar` fell back to the head of the party and then stepped from the nil current character: Down read through nil, Up landed on the last. It steps from the fallback. TParty checks the steps. POR-0194, #806.

**Script menu digits.** `MenuToOptions` (moved from EclMenu to StrUtil so TStrUtil can test it) added 32 to digits as well as letters, so a digit inside an option's text became a stray capital hotkey ("12" became "QR"); a hotkey digit after `~` was never shifted. It lowercases letters only, and a closing `~` at the end of the string reads nothing past it. No shipped menu has a digit. POR-0198, #810.

**CHECK PARTY with no creatures.** `EclVm.EclCheckParty` divided by the member count unguarded; its Find Traps (`C_Gbvm.CHAR_FIND_TRAPS`) and movement arms are one loop that reports 0s for an empty list. POR-0197, #809.

**A spell shows once on the spell lists, with its copies.** The Cast list (`Magic.AddAvailableSpells`), Spells in Memory and the Memorize screen's pending list gave each memorized-spell slot its own line, so a cleric with three Bless memorized had three Bless lines. `Magic.AddMemorizedSpell` gives a spell one line, where its first slot falls, with " (xN)" after it for two or more copies (`MemorizedCopies`); casting still removes the first slot holding the spell (`CharHlp.RemoveSpell`), and the list is built again after each cast. The longest names (31 letters) with a count of 10 or more are cut to keep the line inside the window's 38 columns. The Cast list's scroll spells, a line for each spell on each scroll before, get one line each too, in the order the inventory first holds them, counted across the usable scrolls (`FindScrollCopies`); the line reads the scroll with the fewest spells on it, the first in the inventory among equals, so part-used scrolls go before full ones. #833.

**The Settings screen has Game, Paths and System pages.** `Options.OptionsMenu` draws one page at a time in a single frame, the bottom line's Game, Paths and System choosing it; Up, Down, Left, Right and Enter work the Game page as before, past its Quick Mode heading. The new settings are `GameVars.portraitsAll`, over `monsterPics` and `portraitsOn` (`ViewChar.DisplayChar`, `EclMenu.DisplayMonster` and a save's settings byte take both); `musicGroups`, the title and battle music under `musicOn` (`SoundDrv.PlayMusic`, with `ApplyMusicSettings` ending a tune just turned off); and `soundGroups`, the footsteps, ranged, melee, death and wall-hit effects under Sound, which play through `SoundDrv.SoundEffect` with their kind (C_SOUND.GROUP_*). An attack's hit and miss take the ranged kind when `CbtActs.CombatAttack` has a missile or thrown weapon or a sling, else melee. POOLOPT.CFG gains ALLPORTRAITS, TITLEMUSIC, BATTLEMUSIC, FOOTSTEPS, RANGEDSOUND, MELEESOUND, DEATHSCREAM and WALLHIT, and the later games' CURSEDATA, CURSESAVE, SECRETDATA, SECRETSAVE, POOLSDATA and POOLSSAVE, which the Paths page shows and only the configuration program will set. The STING and NORAND notes moved to the System page. #835.

**Spell Effects, a sound setting of its own.** `C_SOUND.GROUP_SPELL` joins `GameVars.soundGroups`; the Game page lists it under Sound after Wall Hit (`Options.OPT_SPELLS`), and POOLOPT.CFG keeps it as SPELLSOUND, on by default. Every sound a spell makes plays through `SoundDrv.SoundEffect` with it: `Magic.CastSpellNC`'s Fireball, Lightning Bolt and general cast sounds, `Magic.HoldPersonHandler`'s cast at each further target, and `CharDisp.PrintNameStringAnim`'s effect flash, plus its damage flash while `GameVars.currentSpellBeing` names a spell; a breath's or a touch's damage still follows Sound alone. #854.

**The character screen shows attacks a round.** `ViewChar.PrintCombatStats` adds Att/Rd under Damage, from `TCharRecord.attacks`, the half attacks `ClassCal.RecalcBaseSkills` takes from the best of the character's classes, written by `StrUtil.HalvesStr` (2 is 1, 3 is 3/2). It is the classes' rate, not a bow's rate of fire or Haste's. TStrUtil checks it. #836.

**F1 shows the Command Keys window.** `KeyHelp.ShowKeys` clears the screen and draws the window full screen, framed as the Quests page and the Load/Save menu are: a title box over a box to row 22, the function keys from a blank row into it and, after a blank row, the Ctrl keys, with Help: Exit on the bottom line (mocks/f1-help.txt). The lines come from the `KeyHelp.KEYS` table, each with when it shows (`KeyHelp.LineShown`): F5 and F8 only from the map menus, F6 only while `GameVars.companionOn` is set, F7 (the combat log, "Action Log (Combat Only)", #903) only while `GameVars.combatLogOn` is, Ctrl-S only with a sound device, STING's Ctrl-Y only under STING. `Explore.MapMainMenu` (3D and wilderness) and `Camp.CampMainMenu` set the new `MenuCore.helpToCaller`, show the window on F1 and redraw their screen, as F6 does; on any other menu outside combat F1 says help is on the map and camp menus, through `HorizontalMenu`'s new `Unavailable`, which the F5 and F8 message shares. TKeyHelp checks the lines. #855.

**The party menu has two columns; Dual Class is always on it, and a human changes class once.** `CharGen.MainMenu` draws the start-up and training-hall menu through the new overlaid `PartyMnu` unit (mocks/training-hall.txt): CHARACTER (Create, View, Modify, Drop, Train, Dual Class) on the left, PARTY (Add, Remove) and GAME (Save, Load, Settings, Exit to DOS) on the right, Begin Adventuring (B) on the window's last row, and `GameDefs.GAME_VERSION` alone, right-aligned, on the bottom line. SSI's single column of thirteen entries ran off the window at a hall with a human selected. `PartyMnu.ChooseCmd` reads the keys through `MenuHorizontal` (`extraKeys`, `arrowsToCaller`, `enterToCaller`): Up and Down move a white highlight down the CHARACTER column and then the PARTY and GAME column (`StepCmd`), Left and Right go to the nearest command in the other column (`OtherColumn`), and Enter or a command's key carries it out; Home and End step through the party (`Party.SelectPrevNextChar`), as Up and Down did. A command not offered is dark gray and skipped: Load with a party, the character commands without one, and Train away from a hall without free training (#765). `CharDisp.DisplayParty` puts the party menu's roster on rows 1 and up, the names from column 2, and `PartyMnu.MenuTop` moves the commands down under a party of six to eight. Dual Class is always offered: `ChgClass.Refusal` puts its reason on the bottom line for two seconds or until a key (`TextRend.FlashBottomLine`; `DelayOrKey` moved there from Intro) and the version comes back: no hall, not human, changed once already, not okay (free training or not), or drained of a level ("needs restoration first", since a drain falls on the class followed and the class left would need restoring after the change). Being checked when U is pressed, for the character selected then, it ends the stale entry a hall drawn for a human left offered to the others. A human changes class only once, as in Curse, Secret and Pools: `ClassCal.CanChangeClass` refuses a dual-classed character, `CanTakeClass` no longer asks about classes left, `ChangeClass` sets `formerClasses` to the one class left, and `HitDice` gives the class followed only its levels past that class; `formerClasses` stays a mask, so `.chx` files already written read as before. `ChgClass.ChangeClassMenu` follows the new mock-up: the title with the name, the class followed now, every class with a line for each prime requisite (white when the character qualifies here, dark gray when not, the yellow highlight on any of them), the rule, and a status line for the highlighted class: it does not meet the class's requirements, the hall cannot train it, "can't afford to change class (2,500 gp)" (`StrUtil.Grouped`, moved from Options), or what it costs; a character below its own class's requirements gets that status and Exit alone. TPartyMn checks the layout and the highlight's moves, TDualCls the refusals and no second change, TStrUtil `Grouped`. #879.

**The start-up menu is SSI's again; a hall in play keeps two columns.** `CharGen.MainMenu` takes `inGame`. The start-up menu (`fixmain`, after the title screen and after the party is destroyed) passes False and is SSI's again: the roster on rows 2 and 4 on, as SSI drew it, and from row 12 only the commands offered, one to a line, with START.EXE's names, the first letter in white and the rest in light green (`PartyMnu.DrawList`); a first letter, read through `MenuHorizontal` with "pool of radiance" and the version on the bottom line, carries a command out (`ChooseListed`), and Home, End, Up and Down step through the party. The port's addition stays: Game Settings (G). Dual Class is left off it; it belongs to a training hall in play. `PartyMnu.ListTop` lifts the list a row when the commands would run past the window. A training hall reached in the game (`EclVm.EclProgram`'s `PROG_TRAINING`) passes True and keeps the two-column menu, rearranged to the new mocks/training-hall.txt: PARTY (Add, Remove) and GAME (Save, Load) on the left, CHARACTER (Create, View, Modify, Drop, Train, Dual Class) on the right, Settings and Exit to DOS on a row of their own and Begin Adventuring (B) two rows below. The command constants follow the new order, so `StepCmd` runs down the left column and then the right; `OtherColumn`, the gray commands and the refusals are as before. The new `CharDisp.hallMenu` tells `DisplayParty` which roster to draw. TPartyMn follows the new layout. #885.

**The Cast, Memorize and Scribe screens share one layout.** `Magic.ChooseSpell` heads every spell list with "<name>'s Spells", centred in light cyan, where it named the list ("in Spell Book", which a cleric's are not, "to be scribed" and so on). `Magic.SpellSelection` draws the list from row 3 (`C_SPELL.LIST_TOP`), a level heading in the first column and each spell three columns in (`AddSpellToList`; the scroll, pending and known markers go in `MARK_COL` before the name), the summary line on row 22 (`SUMMARY_ROW`) above the window's bottom border, and the action as the bottom line's prompt, "Cast: Cast Info Exit", in place of "Choose Spell:". Memorize's list ends at row 16 (`MEMORIZE_LIST_BOTTOM`) above a second window from row 18 (`SLOTS_TOP`), both framed by `Magic.DrawListWindows`, which calls `belowListHook` for `Camp.DisplaySpellSlots`: "Spell Slots Remaining" in light cyan, then a light green row for each class with slots, cleric first ("Magic-User  1:0  2:0  3:0", a level without slots blank). The message window's "can memorize:" and the summary on its top row are gone. The new `MenuCore.fullPaging` gives a list longer than its window Next and Prev on every page and keeps the window's last row for "(more)" while lines follow the page; `MenuCore.PageRows` says how many lines a page then shows, which `Magic.SpellPage` uses to bring the list back on the right page after Info. The new `MenuCore.DrawMore` replaces the "(more)" copies in `SaveMenu`, `JrnlView` and `ViewChar`. The mock-ups are mocks/spell-cast.txt, spell-memorize.txt and spell-scribe.txt. #900.

**The Load/Save menu's Quit asks only "Exit to DOS?", and X is its Exit.** `SaveLoad.QuitToDos` takes `askSave`: the main menu's Exit to DOS still offers to save an unsaved party first (#660); Quit on the Load/Save menu (#872), at the saves already, asks only "Exit to DOS?". `SaveMenu.ChooseSlot` takes X for Exit (`EXIT_KEY`, through `MenuCore.extraKeys`), since E is a slot's letter; Exit stays unmarked on the line, as the Journal's is. #910.

**The main menu says when there is nothing to add or load.** `CharGen.AddCharacterMenu` reads the roster (`SaveLoad.LoadCharlist`) before it clears the window, and with none, or an empty one, puts "No characters found to add." on the bottom line through `TextRend.FlashBottomLine` (two seconds or a key, as Dual Class's refusals) over the menu it was chosen from. `SaveMenu.ChooseSlot`, which already returned at once when loading found no slot holding a saved game, says "No saved games found to load." the same way first. Both commands used to do nothing without a word. #1069.

**The title music stops at the main menu.** `Intro.Introduction` starts the title theme and left it to play out under the credits and the menu. That was so under DOS too: the native sequencer (`SndSeq`) is the assembly's step for step, and nothing stopped the tune on either. `fixmain` now calls `SoundDrv.StopMusic` once the introduction is over (or skipped by STING), before the first `CharGen.MainMenu`. #1070.

**The training hall menu's headings are light magenta, its roster spaced out.** `PartyMnu.Draw` draws the Party, Game and Character headings in light magenta, the prompts' colour, so the highlighted command's white stands out. `CharDisp.DisplayParty` puts the hall's roster on the start-up menu's rows, the Name/AC/HP headers on row 2 with a blank row above and below and the members from row 4; the hall keeps its own columns. `PartyMnu.MENU_TOP` moves to row 10 and `MenuTop` counts from row 4 (`ROSTER_ROW`); under a party of eight there is no room for the blank row between the roster and the headings, so `MenuTop` keeps Begin Adventuring on the window's last row. mocks/training-hall.txt gains the rows and a Color Key; TPartyMn checks the new rows. #1071.

**The spell summary line draws its keys in white.** `SpellInf.BuildSummary` makes the summary line as `Summary` did (which now calls it), and beside it a mask of each term's key, its first word: RNG, AREA, TGT, DUR, SAVE, or CAMP, CBT or DOORS alone. `ShowSummary` draws the keys in white and the values in light green, a run at a time, in the same columns as before. `SummaryKeys` returns the mask, which TSpelInf checks. #1072.

**The native game asks before quitting.** `SdlHost.Service` hands SDL_EVENT_QUIT and SDL_EVENT_WINDOW_CLOSE_REQUESTED (Command-Q, the app menu's Quit, the close button, Alt-F4) to the new `ConfirmQuit`, which shows SDL3's native message box (`SDL_ShowMessageBox`, with `SDL_FlushEvent` and the `TSDL_MessageBoxButtonData` and `TSDL_MessageBoxData` records bound in `Sdl3Min`, laid out with C's alignment under `{$PACKRECORDS C}` and size-checked at compile time against SDL_messagebox.h on 64-bit and 32-bit targets): "Quit Pool of Radiance? Progress since your last save will be lost.", Quit and Cancel, Cancel the default for Return and Escape. Quit, or a box that cannot be shown, ends the program as before; Cancel flushes the quit events still queued, since closing the last window sends both. A scripted run (POOLRAD_KEYS or POOLRAD_SHOT set) quits without asking. While `DataPick.FindGame` finds the data, `SdlHost.askBeforeQuit` is off and a quit ends the program at once, even with the folder picker open: on a Mac the question, a sheet on the game's window as the picker is, would otherwise wait for the picker to close. `SdlHost.KeyDown` drops keys pressed with Command or the Windows key (`SDL_KMOD_GUI`): they are the system's shortcuts, and Command does not stand for Alt. tools/macos-readme.txt gains a Keys section: Alt is Option, Command-Q asks. Native only: DOS sizes unchanged. #1073.

**Without a sound device the Sound page is grey.** With POOL.CFG's sound line None (S, N or a letter START does not know), `GameBoot` installs `SndNone` and the game plays nothing, yet the Settings screen's Sound page showed Music ON and let the cursor turn over Music, Sound and every sub-setting; only Sound read OFF, from `soundMode`. `Options.OptionsMenu` now sets `noDevice` when `GameVars.sound.mode` is `C_SOUND.MODE_DISABLED` and adds the whole Sound page to `unchangeable` through the new `CfgFile.Silenced`, so its lines are drawn in `OFF_COLOR` (dark grey, as the combat log's are without EMS), Up and Down pass them by, and Enter, Left and Right do nothing while the highlight sits on the page's first line; `ShownValue` goes through the new `CfgFile.ShownValue`, which shows Music and Sound OFF without a device, and the page adds "No sound device in POOL.CFG" under its lines. POOLOPT.CFG's MUSIC and SOUND are not written over (`Collect` still reads `musicOn` and `soundOn`, which `GameIo.SetSound` leaves alone without a device), so a device chosen later brings them back. POOLCFG under DOS does the same from the POOL.CFG it is editing (`CfgFile.NoSoundDevice(cfg.sound)`): the Sound Settings lines are drawn in the new `TuiKit.A_OFF` (light grey on the blue), never highlighted, passed by, and the keys but Escape and F10 change nothing; the About box says to pick a device on the Hardware page, and the Sound line's About text drops its "silent whatever this says" note. The native game always installs Digital Audio (#1097), so natively nothing is greyed, in the game or in `poolcfg`. `tests/regress.sh` gains `SOUND=none` (sound letter S) and now stages its rewritten config as POOL.CFG (it was staged as `regress-adlib.CFG` and the like, which the game never read and the harness now refuses, #1162); the `boot` scenario records the Sound page. Tests: `TCfgFile` (`NoSoundDevice`, `ShownValue`, `Silenced`, and `NextSetting` over a silenced page). #1156.

**Alt-1 to Alt-8 open a party member's items.** Kirben's request (#1175). The top row's Alt-1..Alt-8 arrive as extended scan codes $78..$7F (`C_KEYS.ALT_1`..`ALT_8`), under DOS and from `SdlHost` alike, and the map menus (3D and wilderness) and camp already hand extended keys to `Explore.SideScreen`, which now takes them: `Party.MemberAt(n)` finds member n (nil past `C_GBVM.PARTY_SIZE`, and then nothing happens), and the new `ViewChar.MemberItems` makes it the current character, as View does, starts Trade's recipient at it (`tradeTarget`, which only `ViewCharMenu` set and could otherwise be stale), and opens `UseItemMenu`, the list View's Items opens; a member with no items gets "<name> has no items." instead. `SideScreen`'s redraw puts the map or camp back. Combat, shops, the temple and the vault don't take the keys. `KeyHelp` lists "ALT-1  View PC 1's inventory" to ALT-8 as a group of their own after the function keys (`KEY_LINES` 16; the window's last line lands on row 21, inside the frame). Tests: TParty (`MemberAt`), TKeyHelp. #1175.

**Alt-1 to Alt-8 everywhere outside combat.** #1175 took the keys only in `Explore.SideScreen`. Now `Party.AltMember(c)` maps `C_KEYS.ALT_1`..`ALT_8` to 1..8 (0 for any other key), and `ViewChar.AltItems(c)` opens member n's items through `MemberItems` when `ifcState` isn't `C_IFC.IN_COMBAT` and member n exists, returning True for the caller to redraw; `SideScreen` calls it instead of its own copy. A new `MenuCore.memberKeysToCaller`, set just before a call and cleared on every return as `helpToCaller` is, makes `HorizontalMenu` end on Alt-1..Alt-8 with `ext` set even without `reinterpret` (View's menu runs without it, since the Up arrow's scan code is H, Heal), and makes `MenuVertical`, which dropped every extended key but the movement keys, return the Alt key's scan code with `sel` on the highlighted line; it is taken at entry, as `extraKeys` is. No other key returns #120..#127, since menu keys come back upper-cased. Callers: the counter (`Shop.CounterMenu`, shop and temple), the loot menu and its Take menu (`PostCbt.LootMenu`, `LootTakeMenu`), the vault (`VaultMnu.VaultMenu`) and the party menu (`CharGen.MainMenu`, the training hall) try `AltItems` in their extended-key branch before `Party.SelectPrevNextChar`, and redraw on True; the Buy list (`Shop.ShopBuyMenu`), the Take list (`PostCbt.LootTakeItemMenu`) and the temple's Heal list (`Temple.HealMenu`, whose windows are now drawn in the loop behind a flag) run `AltItems` and go round again, so the member opened buys, takes or is the patient next. `ViewCharMenu` sets the flag outside combat and shows the opened member's sheet afterwards, as after Items. Inside `UseItemMenu` (flag set outside combat) Alt-n doesn't open a second menu: it switches `creature`, `curChar` and `tradeTarget` to member n in place, puts the highlight on its first item and redraws, so Escape leaves to whatever opened the first list; Alt-n for the member shown or for no member does nothing, and for a member with no items it says "<name> has no items." and stays. Sell, Id and Deposit act on `curChar` already, so a member reached this way sells at a shop as from View. Script menus (`EclMenu`) and the camp's Magic, Alter and Fix menus are left out (Brian's decision): their screens can't be put back after a full-screen list. Combat's View and Use never set the flag. Tests: TParty (`AltMember`); scenarios `altshop` (new) and `altitems` (Alt-3 inside the second member's items). #1195.

**The View page shows drained levels and secondary statuses.** Kirben asked for drained levels to be shown somewhere: `drainedLevels` appeared nowhere. The character sheet (`ViewChar.DisplayChar`) puts a red ` (-N)` after the level of each class that lost N levels (`ViewChar.DrawLevel`, `DrainMark`), a dual-classed human's too (`DrawDualClass`), as Brian asked, so a triple class shows each class's loss, such as `5 (-1)/5/6 (-2)`. The counts come from `Magic.DrainedByClass`: an entry on the drain log (#1174) counts for the class it lowered; a drained level the log does not hold (a save from before the log) counts for the class Restoration would give it to (`Magic.RestoreClass`, looking ahead with the logged levels and each earlier one given back, and the levels put back after), which for a single-class character is its class. Exp starts in column 22, lined up with Encumbrance; when the marks run past column 20, it moves right to follow them; a triple class drained in every class still fits the row. The status line (row 22) adds the secondary statuses in red after the status, each joining `, ` in green as the status is (`ViewChar.SecondaryStatus`, drawn by `DrawSecondaryStatus`): `, Poisoned` while the character has the POISONED effect, which a character killed by poison keeps ("Dead, Poisoned"), and `, Drained` while it has drained levels, with no count, as Brian decided; the longest, "Unconscious, Poisoned, Drained", ends in column 37. Tests: TDrain (the counts for a single class, an old save, a triple class from its log and an unlogged level looked ahead; the statuses). #1161.

## 6. Saving, loading, quick save and the vault

This section covers the new load/save experience and the less visible work that keeps characters, saves, and vault contents safe when a write fails.

**Full-screen Load/Save menu, with descriptions, areas and times.** Loading and saving chose a slot from a one-line letter prompt. The new `SaveMenu` unit shows the slots full screen, titled "Load/Save Game Menu": each slot shows its letter and description, then the area and the time the file was written. Up and Down move a slot at a time and the list scrolls, with "(more)" when slots are hidden, and Page Up and Page Down move a screenful; the menu opens on the newest save. Loading lists saved games only; saving lists every slot, an unused one as EMPTY. Saving asks for a description (30 characters at most) starting from the slot's old one. The description and area go in `savgam<x>.inf` beside the save, a small versioned record. The area comes from the new `AreaName` unit: the Clue Book's names for scripts 0..29, shortened to 18 columns, keyed by the script a save reopens (`previousScriptId`) and, for the four scripts that run two maps, the map; anything else reads UNKNOWN AREA; a save without an .inf has its area read from the save file. Camp redraws its screen after Save. #604. 3b2c103.

**Load/Save menu revised: letters take a slot, a new layout, a one-step save prompt.** A slot's letter (A-J, Q) now takes it at once: saving opens its description prompt, loading loads it (#652). The list keeps a column clear inside its border, shows the time beside the description and the date under it, each letter in white; the bottom line reads Game: Next Prev Save (or Load) Exit, with Exit reached by the arrows only, since E is a slot (`MenuCore.unmarked`) (#657). The save prompt shows the slot, location and description, which reads SAVE GAME X while empty (#656). A description is typed, and shown, up to 27 characters (`DESC_SHOWN`), so the time fits beside it; the slot-info file still holds 30, and a longer old description is cut to 27 when shown or edited. 078b01f. Because every letter goes to the description, the save prompt's bottom line then became "Save: Exit" with no hotkeys: Enter saves and Escape goes back (#657 follow-up). 2748c77.

**F5 quick save and F8 quick load.** On the map menu (3D or wilderness), F5 saves into a new slot Q, described as QUICK SAVE, with no menu, and says so; F8 loads it. Q is listed after J on the Load/Save menu. F8 with no quick save says so. Elsewhere F5 and F8 say quick save and load are unavailable, and in combat they are ignored silently (`MenuCore.quickKeysToCaller`, set by the map menu). A quick save records the running script as the one it reopens. F8 sets `Explore.quickLoadAsked` and returns from `GameLoop`; the program frees the party (records, items, effects, usual spells and icon animations), loads Q as the main menu would, and runs `GameLoop` again. `LoadGame` and `SaveGame` are split into `LoadSlot` and `SaveToSlot`. #605. 7a3305d. Follow-up fixes: `quickLoadAsked`, `extraKeys` and `quickKeysToCaller` are now set in their units' initialization (`quickLoadAsked` could start nonzero, making the first move on the map run a quick load), and QuickLoad checks the quick save exists before freeing the party, resuming the game in memory if not. #605. a955646.

**The vault: The Hoarder's Respite in New Phlan.** New Phlan's tavern at (8,9) becomes a vault, after Secret of the Silver Blades and Pools of Darkness. The merchant greets the party ("WELCOME TO THE HOARDER'S RESPITE. LET ME LIGHTEN YOUR LOAD.", or on a return visit, one time in 20, "OH, YOU'RE ALIVE... I MEAN  WELCOME BACK!"). Its menu is View, Take (while there is anything), Pool, Share (while there is money) and Exit; in View, Drop becomes Deposit for money and items, which go into the vault. The vault holds at most 100 items. Storage follows Pools of Darkness: during play the vault is `tmpvault.dat` in the save directory (a small header with the money and a visited flag, then the items); saving copies it to `vault<x>.dat` beside `savgam<x>.dat`, loading copies it back, and starting the program forgets it. New interface state `C_Ifc.VAULT`. The data change is in section 7. New feature, not in SSI's Pool. #606. a955646.

**The script buffer grows to 9,984 bytes; the area's script is reloaded with a saved game.** The script buffer (zone 3) held 7,680 bytes ($9900..$B6FF), SSI's size from the 8-bit versions, and five area scripts were within 26 bytes of it, so data fixes had to be same-length rewrites. It now runs to $BFFF: nothing in the script address map lies between $B700 and the first special cell, $C04B, and scripts address themselves absolutely from $9900. `C_Gbvm.SCRIPT_FIRST` and `SCRIPT_LAST` name it; `LoadEclScript` and DataBld's cap follow `SizeOf(TZone3)`. A saved game still holds SSI's 7,680 bytes where SSI held them (`C_Gbvm.SAVED_SCRIPT_BYTES`), so save files stay 13,137 bytes and older ones load as before (#607). A loaded game now loads its area's script from the data, as Secret of the Silver Blades does, instead of the copy in the save, which kept a script from before any later data fix (a game saved in New Phlan kept the tavern where the vault now is). `EclInit` still keeps a loaded game's script words. A save now records the running script as the one it reopens, since `NEWECL` leaves the old area's id until the map menu returns (#609). Differs from SSI. d23c774.

**Deterministic saves.** Two saves of the same game differed byte for byte. Character, item and effect records were written raw, heap pointers included (loading ignores them); they are now written with their pointers nil, layout and pads unchanged, so the files still load here and in SSI's executable. `SaveToSlot`'s table of party names was not cleared, leaving stack bytes; it is cleared first. The vault's items are written without their link. 8048f48. A character's `equipped[]` slots, pointers into its items, were saved as they were and kept on load until something called `RecalcStats`; `CharHlp.LinkEquipped` (RecalcStats' slot rebuild) now runs once the items are read, and `SaveChar` writes the slots cleared. #620. 9a3f33c.

**A save no longer sets the menu animating over the view.** A save wrote the wall slots into zone 0, where the last of them is the GBVM's menu-animation flag, so the next menu drew the last picture loaded (the treasure, after a fight) over the 3D view. The slots now go to the file only, and a load clears the flag it reads in. The save files are unchanged. #651. 5d4e0cc.

**One "Put save disk" retry.** The save files were created in two copies of the same loop and the "Put save disk in C:" text built in six places; they are now `SaveLoad.RewriteSaveFile` and `SaveDiskMsg`. Loading a slot that cannot be opened now asks as the rest do (it showed the whole path with no colon). 35f21e7.

**The usual-spells file is read when write-protected.** The .mem file was opened read-write, so a write-protected one was taken for missing; it is opened read-only (via `FileUtil.OpenReadOnly`). #635. 55b640e.

**Saved map position written field by field.** `SaveGame` and `LoadGame` wrote and read five bytes from `gMapX` on as one block; each is now read and written by itself, in the same order, so the file is unchanged and the variables can move. 10f9b84.

**Exit to DOS, the destroyed party, the out-of-memory save.** See section 1 (#660, #658, #643).

**Quit on the Load/Save menu.** `SaveMenu.ChooseSlot`'s bottom line reads GAME: NEXT PREV LOAD QUIT EXIT (SAVE when saving), and Quit returns `SaveMenu.QUIT_CHOSEN`. It has no key, as Exit has none: Q is the quick save's slot and E a slot's letter. `SaveLoad.LoadGame` and `SaveGame` then ask through the new `SaveLoad.QuitToDos`, the main menu's Exit to DOS moved out of `CharGen.MainMenu`: "Exit to DOS?", and for a party not saved since it changed "Save first?" (#660), whose Yes opens the save menu and quits once the game is saved. Answered No, the menu opens again. Camp asked "Quit TO DOS" after every save, as SSI did; the menu's Quit takes its place, and `Camp.CampMainMenu` no longer asks. #872.
