**Q for Quit on the Load/Save menu; the quick save moves to slot Z.** The quick save's slot is now Z (`SaveMenu.QUICK_SLOT`, which `SaveLoad`'s own `QUICK_SLOT` became), listed after J, so Quit takes Q as its hotkey (`OPT_QUIT` is no longer `unmarked`, and `ChooseSlot` turns Q into `QUIT_CHOSEN`). At every start `DataBld.BuildDataFiles` first calls the new `SaveMenu.MoveOldQuickSave`, which renames a quick save left in slot Q, every file `SaveMenu.RecoverSlot` knows of (savgam .dat, .inf, .sts, .map, .nts, the vault copy and each member's files), to slot Z, savgamQ.dat last so a move the game was ended in is done again next time. A file slot Z holds without a savgamZ.dat is erased first; while savgamZ.dat is there, nothing moves. `RecoverSlot` and the move share the new `SlotSet`. TSaveMnu checks the move. #1080.

**Load-time guards.** `LoadGame` holds `gameDelay` to 9 (see section 9, #440).

---

**Saves are written whole or not at all.** `SaveLoad.SaveToSlot` truncated savgam<x>.dat before writing, checked none of its writes, always set `recentSaved`, and `QuickSave` always said "Quick save done.", so a disk that filled mid-save lost the old save without a word. FileUtil gains a safe write (`TempName` (the last character becomes `$`), `RewriteTemp`, `WriteChecked`, `CloseTemp`, `PutInPlace`), and `WriteRecord` and `Options.SaveOptions` are built on it. `SaveToSlot`, now a function, writes savgam<x>.dat and every member's files (`WriteCharFiles`) to temporary copies and copies the vault; only when all are whole are they put in place (`PlaceCharFiles`). Otherwise the copies are erased, the old save stays, the game says "The game was not saved." and `recentSaved` stays clear. TFileUtl checks the helpers. Differs from SSI: SSI wrote in place. CURSE-0361, #755.

**The vault keeps its contents when its ledger can't be written.** `Vault.WriteVault` rewrote tmpvault.dat in place and `ClearTreasure` then freed the items and money whatever happened; a failed `Rewrite` gave no message at all. It is now a function using the safe write, and the vault's Exit clears the pool only after a good write, else asks "Leave anyway?". `CopyOrDelete` copies the same way and reports, and `RestoreFromSlot` says "The vault's ledger was not copied." and forgets the session vault on failure. As Secret (secret#440). #699.

**Camp's Save asks to quit only after a save.** `SaveGame` is now a function, True only when the game was saved, so Camp's Save asks "Quit TO DOS" only then, and `CheckFileElseQuit` exits only after a save (otherwise it puts back the party's position and disk and asks again). The party menu's quit and HeapErr's out-of-memory save loop use the result instead of `recentSaved`. SSI's code. CURSE-0310, #722.

**Remove keeps a character whose save failed.** `SaveChar` is now a function, True only when every file was written and put in place; it adds the name to CHARLIST.TXT only then. Remove takes the character out of the party only after a good save, and `CreateCharacterMenu` asks "Try again?" before letting a new character go. SSI's code. CURSE-0359, #706.

**A device name is saved to a file.** A character whose file name came out as CON, AUX, NUL, PRN, COM1..4, LPT1..3 or CLOCK$ was written to that device. `FileUtil.IsDeviceName` recognises them and `DaxIo.SanitizeFileName` adds an underscore (CON_.CHA), as Curse does. `LoadChar` now names a single character's file with `CharFileBase`, as `SaveChar` does, so a name with two periods in a row ("A..B") is found again. TFileUtl checks it. SSI's code. CURSE-0363, #707.

**Read-only files open.** `DaxIo.FileExists`, `OpenWithRetry` and `OpenDax`'s second handle opened under the default FileMode 2, so a read-only ITEMS gave "Unable to read ITEMS", read-only pictures and maps failed and a read-only save was "damaged". They now use `FileUtil.OpenReadOnly`. GameInit no longer opens ITEMS twice, and GameBoot finds POOL.CFG with `DaxIo.FileExists` (130 bytes less data). SSI's code. CURSE-0364, #723.

**Members' own files are kept.** `LoadSlot` and `SaveToSlot` erased each member's .cha, .itm, .spc, .mem and .sts, SSI's guard against duplicating characters. The owner chose to drop it: `EraseCharFiles` is gone, `SaveCharlist` keeps a party member's roster line (unmarked), and `SaveChar` adds a name only when it is missing. Drop forever now erases the dropped character's files and roster line (`SaveLoad.EraseChar`), so it does not come back on the Add list. Differs from SSI. CURSE-0029, #754.

**A load from a script's party menu leaves the script.** After Load on the training hall's party menu, `EclProgram` only redrew and `CallEclScript` ran on over the loaded save's script bytes. PROG_TRAINING now stops the script when `CharGen.MainMenu` returns with a loaded game; `EclVm.Reloading` (a quick load, or `loadedSave`) makes `CallEclScript` run nothing and GameLoop return at once, and FixMain runs GameLoop again into the loaded area. SSI's code. CURSE-0273, #756.

**Disk prompts name their file, and Escape gets out.** `OpenWithRetry`, `RewriteSaveFile`, `SaveCharlist` and `CheckSaveDir` asked for the save disk until it worked, so a roster character with no file, or a save folder that could not be written, held the player for good. Every prompt now says what is wrong ("Not found:", "Cannot write:", "Cannot create:", with the path, its start cut by `DaxIo.PathForLine` when long) and then asks for the disk (`DaxIo.AskForDisk`). Escape at the save disk prompt gives up: Add takes a character whose file is gone off the roster (`SaveLoad.HasCharFile`), a save fails as #755's do, the roster stays as it was and the folder is not made. Escape at a game data prompt ("Insert disk N in drive X: (Esc quits)") quits to DOS (`GameIo.ExitGame`), and at the GAME.OVR or POOL.CFG prompt, before the screen is up, with `Halt`; `DaxIo.WaitForFile` replaces `OverlayPrompt` and GameBoot's loop. `LoadChar` no longer reads or closes a file that did not open. POR-0187, #794.

**A removed character is not made current again.** `savedChar` moves from EclVm to GDVars, and `SaveLoad.RemoveCreature` moves it to the new current character when it frees the record it points at (Curse #609), so a drop during a script's camp PROGRAM no longer leaves `NewEclDone` to restore a freed record. `EclLoadChar` relies on it. POR-0171, #776.

**Side files report a failed write.** `UsualSpl`, `Stats.WriteChar`/`WriteGame`, `SaveMenu.WriteSlotInfo` and the `WriteSlot` routines of Explored and MapNotes dropped `FileUtil.WriteRecord`'s result. They return it; `PlaceCharFiles` gathers the members' into `whole`, `SaveChar` says a character was not saved in full and `SaveToSlot` that "The game was saved, but not in full." MapTool's Clear uses the new `MapNotes.ClearNote`. TMapNote, TExplore and TUsualSp check it. #821.

**An empty vault's commit.** `Vault.CopyOrDelete` returned True when the source could not be opened even if erasing the slot's old vault failed; it returns `FileUtil.DeleteChecked` (True when no such file is left). TFileUtl checks it. #819.

**DataBld's remap table.** Checked for #822: a remap entry belongs to one disk, so a disk sharing another's spare id still needs its own, and a full table must fail. A comment says so. #822.

**Saves are transactions.** #755 wrote each file through a temporary copy, but `SaveToSlot` put the save file in place before the members', and the side files (.inf, .sts, .map, .nts, .mem) and the vault copy were written after, each on its own, so a failure left a mixed slot and "saved, but not in full". FileUtil's file set (`NewSet`, `AddToSet`, `WriteToSet`, `DropSet`, `CommitSet`) now carries every file of a save: the save file, each member's files and side files, the slot's side files and the vault copy (`Vault.AddToSave`), with the files the new save no longer has (unused member slots, empty lists) erased only on commit. `CommitSet` moves each old file to a backup (`%`), puts each copy (`$`) in place, then erases the backups, and undoes everything if any step fails; the save file goes aside first and into place last, and a new file with no old one leaves an empty backup as a marker. `RecoverSet` gives back the old save when the first file's backup is still there, so a save cut off by a crash or power cut is undone the next time the slot is listed, loaded or saved to (`SaveMenu.RecoverSlot`, `SaveLoad.RecoverChar` for single characters, which `SaveChar` and `EraseChar` also commit as sets). `WriteRecord` and the partial-save message are gone; a character's failed save says "Could not save <name>." TFileUtl checks every stage of an interrupted commit. #823, #824.

**The settings are written into a save folder not made yet.** `CfgFile.WriteOptions` wrote POOLOPT.CFG into the save folder without making it, so while the folder was not there (a new install, or natively a new per-user folder, #1064) a changed setting was lost at the next start. The new `FileUtil.MakeFolder` makes a folder and each one above it that is not there (IOResult 5, already there, counts; a drive's root is never made; natively a slash divides the path as a backslash does), `WriteOptions` calls it first, and `SaveMenu.CheckSaveDir`, whose own MkDir loop it replaces, calls it until the folder is there or Escape gives up. DOS START already made the save folder for a save, so DOS gets the same; DOS POOLCFG, which writes through `WriteOptions`, now makes a missing save folder too instead of saying it may be missing. `TCfgFile` writes into a folder two levels deep that is not there and reads the settings back. #1067.

**Temporary copies and backups go in a TMP folder.** `FileUtil.TempName` named a file's temporary copy by replacing the last letter of its extension with `$`, and `BackupName` named its backup with `%`. A dual-classed character's `.cha` and `.chx` both became `.ch$`. When `SaveLoad.WriteCharFiles` wrote both into one set, the `.chx` copy overwrote the `.cha` copy, and `CommitSet` failed: removing the character said "Could not save", left a stray `.CHX` and kept her in the party. The owner chose a folder instead of special characters. A copy is now the file's own name in the `TMP\` folder of the file's own folder (`FileUtil.TEMP_FOLDER`: `SAVE\TMP\savgamA.dat`), and a backup the same name in `TMP\BAK\` (`BACKUP_FOLDER`). No two files' copies or backups share a name, and a copy and a backup of one file never meet. Because the folder sits in the file's own folder, the rename into place stays on one drive (DOS renames across folders but not across drives). `RewriteTemp` makes `TMP\` first through the new `FileUtil.MakeTempFolder`, which `CfgFile.WriteOptions` and `SaveLoad.SaveCharlist` also call before they open a text copy themselves. `CommitSet` makes `TMP\` and `TMP\BAK\` before it moves any file aside. Both are left in place, empty, after a commit. A commit or a write that an earlier version was ended in is still settled: `Settle` and `Unfinished` also look for a backup at the old `%` name and put it back or erase it, and `Settle` erases any copy at the old `$` name. That code can go a release or two on. `C_OPT.MAX_PATH_TEXT` is now 79 less `LONGEST_NAME` less `WORK_FOLDERS` (the 8 characters of `TMP\BAK\`), so 59 under DOS, and POOLCFG still lets 79 characters be typed. `MakeTempFolder` refuses a name whose backup would not fit `TPathName`, so a longer path from an old POOL.CFG fails the write rather than cutting the name short. Natively, `FileUtil.OpenReadOnly` now checks for a folder by its NatFile name: with a backslash in it, Linux's `DirectoryExists` missed the folder and the open succeeded, so a folder at a backup's name read as a backup. `scripts/build.sh --test` clears the `TMP` folder the tests leave. TFileUtl checks the names, the folders, a name too long, a `.cha` and a `.chx` put in place together, and recovery from old-style names. #1084.

**DataBld's temporary archives go in TMP\ too.** DataBld wrote each merged archive as `<name>.$$$` in the game folder, read it back and renamed it to `<name>.DAX`; its writable check (`CanWrite`) left an empty `<name>.$$$` there, and a build cut short left the whole one (a regression run left `BODY.$$$`). The temporary archive is now `FileUtil.TempName` of the archive's full path, `TMP\<name>.DAX` beside where it goes (natively in the writable folder `DaxIo.FolderOf` picks from the bare name), through the new `DataBld.TempPath`, `TempLabel` (its name in DATABLD.LOG) and `RemoveTemp`. `CanWrite` makes the TMP\ folder first (`FileUtil.MakeTempFolder`) and fails as an unwritable folder does when it cannot; it keeps its own `Rewrite` rather than `FileUtil.RewriteTemp`, so DATABLD.LOG still gets the DOS error number. Renaming into place is unchanged (the old archive removed, then the rename), not `FileUtil.PutInPlace`: a merged archive is rebuilt whenever it is missing or stale, so a backup would protect nothing and leave copies in TMP\BAK\. `BuildDataFiles` erases any old `DATABLD.$$$` and `<name>.$$$` once at start, for a release or two. Natively the folder-write probe `poolrad.$$$` (`GameDirs`) is now `poolrad.tmp`. A regression run (DOSBox-X) built all 14 archives and left TMP\ empty and no `.$$$` file. #1150.

**A cut-short move of the old quick save keeps the files it moved.** `SaveMenu.MoveOldQuickSave` deleted slot Z's copy of every file, then renamed slot Q's copy if there was one. A move cut short leaves `savgamQ.dat`, which moves last, so the next start runs the move again; on that rerun each file the first run had moved existed only in Z and was deleted. It now deletes Z's copy and renames only when Q has the file, as Curse's port does. TSaveMnu's case for Z's left-over `.sts` is replaced by a rerun with the `.sts` and a member's file already in Z: every file ends in Z, and those two keep what they held. #1085.

**A save stamps today's date in DOSBox-X.** A save's date and time on the Load/Save menu are its `.dat` file's stamp, which DOS sets from its own clock. DOSBox-X moves the DOS date past midnight only when a program asks DOS for the date or time. Since #469 the game reads the BIOS tick count instead, so a game left running past midnight saved with the day it started. `SaveLoad.SaveToSlot` now calls `Dos.GetDate` once before it writes anything. One call per save does not bring back #469's savestate problem, which came from calling it on every menu pass. Natively the host's clock stamps the files, and the call is harmless. Not reproduced in DOSBox-X yet. #1126.

## 7. Data fixes to the shipped scripts and items (DataPat)

`DataPat` is the patch table `DataBld` applies to SSI's original data while building the merged archives. When `C_DATA.VERSION` increases, the game knows to rebuild those archives the next time it starts. The entries below explain both the player-visible correction and the script or item data changed to make it happen.

**The vault's script (data version 32).** A DataPat fix sends event 20 in New Phlan's event table to new code at the end of the script. Every other event-20 square (six other tavern doors) goes on to the tavern as before; at (8,9) the code does what the arms shop does (once a visit, the shopkeeper, the refusal after an attack on the city watch), stores 2 in [$6E6C], the shop cell, and runs COMBAT with no monsters, which `EclCombat` now takes to the vault. 52 bytes; the script stayed under the then 7,682-byte limit with 157 to spare. A game saved inside New Phlan before this kept the tavern until the party left and came back (later fixed by #609, section 6). #606. a955646.

**The Pitt's buccaneer's question gets its space (data version 33).** The PRINTCLEAR at $A90D (Podal Plaza, script 18) ran the closing quote into the next sentence: "WHO WILL IT BE?'WHAT DO YOU DO?". The packed string had an empty six-bit slot at its end, so the space fits in the same 66 bytes. The boot scenario strips the data version from the settings screen, since the golden build has its own. #670 (open PR #673). e747259.

**Stojanow Gate after it is taken (data version 34).** Once the party has taken the gate (`[$4A77]` holds both barracks and the bugbears, `$1C`, the script's own test), Stojanow's script (ECL2 block 9) no longer leaves its squares silent. Its event table sends events 1, 2, 7 and 13 to new code at the end of the script, which falls back to SSI's code while the gate is not taken. At (4,7) and (11,7), the guard rooms, "MAKESHIFT BARRACKS HAVE BEEN ERECTED HERE FOR HOUSING NEW PHLAN TROOPS." replaces SSI's guard-room text, once a visit as that text was. At (7,9) and (8,9), before the gatehouse, and (7,6) and (8,6), behind it, a guard's portrait (head 57, `$39`, the head New Phlan gives THE GUARD CAPTAIN; body 5, the lion tabard: HEAD.DAX and BODY.DAX block ids; disk 2 has neither head, and the merged HEAD.DAX gives disk 3's) and "THE GUARDS ON THE WALL RAISE A CHEER AS YOU PASS. 'HAIL, HEROES OF PHLAN!'", on stepping into the square in any facing, once a visit for each side. None waits for Return. The once-a-visit bits are `[$4A01]` bits 2 and 3, beside the guard rooms' bits 0 and 1: the cells `$4A00..$4A1F` belong to the running area's script and `EclMenu.EclInit` clears them whenever a script is entered by NEWECL (not on a load). Going north through the gatehouse (`LA101`, entry 1) moves the party as `LA16A` does but no longer runs the new square's event before the game loop redraws the view (the loop runs it once the view is drawn, as it always did a second time), so the cheer at (7,6) keeps its portrait. 331 bytes; the script is 6,787 bytes of the 9,984 the buffer holds. Differs from SSI. #683.

**Stojanow Gate's cheer without a portrait, once a visit (data version 40).** The cheer (#683) drew a guard's portrait over the view, which was jarring, and came once for each side of the gatehouse, so twice a visit. Its `SAVE $39, [$6DE1]`, `PICTURE $05` and `SAVE $FF, [$6DE1]` are gone (15 bytes), so the text appears under the view as it stands, and both pairs of squares now set the same bit of `[$4A01]` (`C_GBVM.STOJANOW_SHOWN`, bit `STOJANOW_CHEERED`, `$04`, beside SSI's guard-room bits 1 and 2), so the cheer comes at whichever pair the party reaches first and not again until the area is entered anew. The gatehouse's GOTO at `$A11F` goes to `LB340`, where the way through now starts. Part 7 is 166 bytes; the script is 6,772 bytes; DataPat holds 86 parts, 2,603 bytes. The stojanow scenario now records no cheer at (7,6). #1068.

**Dirten's temple (data version 34).** Dirten joins in the bishop's study (ECL3 block 0) and, if he is in the party when the Temple of Bane (ECL1 block 24, map 24) is freed, bids farewell and stays; SSI kept no record of it. New code at the end of the script now keeps the day the temple is freed in a reserved cell, `[$4A79]` (`C_Gbvm.DIRTEN_TEMPLE_DAY`): year × 360 + month × 30 + day + 1 from the clock (0 is no day), or `$FFFF` when Dirten was not in the party. If he was in the party but down at the end of the fight (SSI skipped the farewell then and removed him silently), "THE TEMPLE GUARDS ENTER AND SECURE THE AREA. A CLERIC RUSHES TO AID THE FALLEN DIRTEN." is shown instead, and the day kept too. On the altar squares (14,7) and (14,8) of a freed temple with a day kept: before 14 days, "THE EFFORT TO REBUILD IS ALREADY UNDERWAY. SOON THE TEMPLE WILL BE SERVICEABLE AGAIN."; from 14 days, Dirten's portrait (head 0, body 4), "WELCOME BACK! WE'RE STILL REBUILDING, BUT LET ME OFFER WHAT AID I CAN.", a press of Return (the temple's screen would clear the words at once), then his temple with `[$6DE2]` = 2, and the view restored. A game saved before this with the temple freed and Dirten having joined (`[$4A9C]` = `$FF`) but no day kept gets its day on the first visit to the altar, so its 14 days start then; a freed temple with no Dirten keeps SSI's altar. The cell was chosen from those no script and no unit uses (`$4A53..$4A58`, `$4A6E..$4A71`, `$4A79..$4A7B`), checked against the disassembly of all 29 scripts, their indexed table reads and writes and the units; zero in every saved game seen. In Pascal, `C_Gbvm.ENTER_TEMPLE` = 2 (`MoneyCal.TEMPLE_TENTH_OFF`) opens the temple a tenth off: `EclVm.EclCombat` passes the mode to `Temple.TempleMainMenu`, and `Temple.ConfirmCureCost` asks `MoneyCal.TemplePrice`, so the price shown is the price paid (Cure Light Wounds 90, Raise Dead 4,950); TMoneyCa tests the rule. Entering the freed temple was already peaceful: the blind orc and his guards (`LA402`), the ambush on leaving (`LAA67`) and the treasure ambush (`LAA53`) all stop once `[$4ABD]` >= `$FE`. 482 bytes; the script is 8,090 bytes. Differs from SSI. #682.

**Dirten after the temple is freed; the altar stays destroyed (data version 41).** Freeing the Temple of Bane before Dirten joined left him in the party for good: City Hall's summons to the bishop (ECL3 block 8, `$AABE`) held back only once the reward was paid (`[$4ABD]` = `$FF`), the bishop's study (event 5 of ECL3 block 0's event table, `$9E07`) never asked whether the temple was freed, and only the main fight's end (`$AC68`) bids him farewell, so #682's altar took the temple as freed without him. The summons now stops at `$FE`, the temple freed. The study's event goes to new code at the end of the script: with the temple freed, the bishop's portrait and "YOU ARE USHERED INTO THE BISHOP'S STUDY. BISHOP BRACCIO SPEAKS, 'YOU HAVE ALREADY CLEANSED THE TEMPLE OF BANE! DIRTEN WILL GO AT ONCE TO RESTORE IT.'", then `[$4A9C]` = `$FF` and `[$4A79]` = 0, the state #682's altar takes as freed with Dirten, so the first visit to the altar starts its 14 days. #682's jump at the altar (ECL1 block 24, `$A8B0`) goes to new code: for a temple freed without Dirten (`[$4A79]` = `$FFFF`) that he joined after (`[$4A9C]` = `$FF`, a game from before this), it finds him in the party and goes to #682's farewell (`$B7F5`), which keeps the day and removes him. Only that state searches the party, since its LOAD CHARACTER would change which member the temple serves. SSI kept the altar destroyed in `[$4A02]`, an area cell each visit clears, so the altar could be destroyed again on every visit; it is now `[$4A7A]` (`C_GBVM.BANE_ALTAR_DESTROYED`, the next free cell #682's note lists), written by both destroying paths (`$A9C6`, `$AA01`) and read by #682's altar (`$B7E6`). The new altar code clears it while the temple is not freed, so an altar destroyed in a fight the party fled stands again, as before. ECL3 block 0 grows 185 bytes to 8,038 and ECL1 block 24 112 bytes to 8,202. Differs from SSI. #1139.

**The Mad Man at the Temple of Tyr (data version 41).** With the Mad Man in the party, the temple's aged priest (ECL3 block 0, `$AFE3`) told the party to drop him and waited for Return, but never offered to take him. His words are now "...THAT MAN URGENTLY NEEDS OUR CARE.  LEAVE HIM WITH US AND WE WILL CARE FOR HIM.'" (the same 134 packed bytes, the rest null), and the wait (`$B06C`) goes to new code at the end of the script, after #868's, that asks "'WILL YOU LEAVE HIM IN OUR CARE?'". Yes removes him as the same script removes Skullcrusher (`$AD56`), by the index the party search (`$AF56`) left in `[$9802]`, his items with him, with "THE PRIESTS GENTLY LEAD THE MAD MAN AWAY."; No gives the temple's greeting again. Either way the offer of healing follows as before. 143 bytes. Differs from SSI. #1138.

**The records office's last word (data version 42).** In Valjevo Castle's records office (ECL5 block 4), reading the records (`$AF51`) goes through `ON GOTO [$4A68]` (`$AFB6`): the first finding, the second, and from then on "TO FIND ANYTHING MORE YOU WILL HAVE TO SPEND SO MUCH TIME THAT YOU WILL NEED TO CLEAR THE AREA FIRST." (`$B10A`). `[$4A68]` only counts the findings shown, and nothing in the script asks whether anything is cleared, so the text came however much was. It now reads "YOU FIND NOTHING MORE OF INTEREST IN THE RECORDS.", in the same 76 packed bytes, the rest null. Differs from SSI. #1093.

**The vampire's coffin needs something holy (data version 42).** SANCTIFY at the coffin in Valhingen Graveyard (ECL4 block 10, `ON GOTO` at `$B04E`) printed "YOU SCATTER THE DUST AND PLACE WHAT HOLY ITEMS YOU CAN SPARE INSIDE." and set `[$4A43]` = 251, which brings the vampire back weakened (#105 for #23, `$B126`), whatever the party carried. FRC1, the tabletop adventure, has holy water, relics or holy symbols left in the coffin. SANCTIFY now goes to new code at the end of the script that uses up a vial of holy water (type 85), or failing one a holy symbol, and goes on to SSI's sanctifying (`$B06E`); with neither, "YOU HAVE NOTHING HOLY TO LEAVE IN THE COFFIN." and nothing changes. 54 bytes. Two engine additions do it. A FIND ITEM selector above the base item types, `C_ITEM.FIND_HOLY_SYMBOL` = 255, selects any item with the holy-symbol name part (152), since holy symbols share their types (70, 71, 74) with other jewellery; no shipped script names a type above 96 (`CharHlp.ItemSelected`, `CharHlp.FindPartyItem`). USE UP ITEM (`C_ECL.USE_UP_ITEM`, `$80`, one operand: a FIND ITEM selector) uses up one of the first such item in party order: a stack loses one, anything else is removed, put away first when readied, and the owner's stats and the party list follow (`EclVm.EclUseUpItem`, `CharHlp.UseUpItem`); it sets the comparison flags as FIND ITEM does, so a script can try one item and then another. It is the scripts' general way to use something up. Curse's and Pools of Darkness's `$40` takes away every item of a type, and Pools has a `$41`, so it has a number no SSI game uses. TCharHlp, TDataPat and TEclTrc test it. Differs from SSI. #1096.

**Odran, the Valhingen mage (data version 42).** The magic-user who joins in Valhingen Graveyard (ECL4 block 10, `ADD NPC #24` at `$AE81`, MON4CHA block 24) was named LEVEL 6 MU; the NES version called him Magician. Once he has joined, new code at the end of the script, after #1096's, names him ODRAN: ADD NPC leaves the new member current, so a string SAVE to `[$6B00]` (`C_GBVM.CHAR_NAME`) names him, and a name given this way now shows in the party list at once (`EclVm.EclSave`). The game's text, and the record, which is also the mage the party fights when it sends him away (`LOAD MONSTER #24` at `$AEDA`), keep SSI's name. A party that already has him keeps the old name, which is saved with it. 17 bytes; the jump at `$AE86` leaves three unreached bytes. Differs from SSI. #1113.

**Pictures taken down when the party moves on (data version 42).** At the Zhentil Keep Outpost before the mission (ECL6 block 25), LEAVE (`ON GOTO` at `$A04E`) went to a bare EXIT (`$AB3E`, the tail of the cave's exit), so the riders' picture stayed until the next step; it now goes to `$ADC0` (SPRITE OFF, PICTURE `$FF`, EXIT), where the script's other LEAVEs go. #1116. The Valhingen mage who joins (#1113's new code) now ends with PICTURE `$FF` before EXIT, so his picture goes at once too; "YOUR PARTY IS TOO LARGE" still leaves him standing there. #1117. The engine's EXIT is left alone: where SSI meant the view restored, the scripts do it. Differs from SSI.

**The magic shop (data version 43).** New Phlan's event table (ECL3 block 0, `ON GOTO [$9800]` at `$9B4C`) sends event 22, the arms and armor shops' (`$A8BE`), to new code at the end of the script (`$B864`, after #1139's). Every square but (11,12) goes on to `$A8BE` as before. At (11,12) it does what the arms shop does, the greeting once a visit (`GOSUB $AE89`), the shopkeeper (head 42 on body 9) and the refusal after an attack on the city watch (`C_GBVM.WATCH_FOUGHT`), then asks "THE SHOP SELLS MAGICAL ITEMS. 'CAN I SHOW YOU OUR WARES?'"; No leaves as the shops' No does (`$AE6D`), Yes runs CLEARMONSTERS, PROGRAM 11 (`EclVm.PROG_MAGIC_SHOP`, the stock), `SAVE 1, [$6E6C]` (`C_GBVM.ENTER_SHOP`) and COMBAT, which opens the shop. The script grows by 117 bytes to 8,155. See section 1. #1091, #1158.

**Random javelin template.** Not a DataPat change but a fix to item generation; see section 2 (POR-0165).

**Tests for the patch table.** TDataPat checks the rules DataBld relies on: the parts use every byte of `patchBytes`, each names a family DataBld builds, its original bytes lie inside the block, its size is the block's after the parts before it, and a fix's parts are together. 41160a7.

---

**The Large bit on gnolls, huge scorpions and phase spiders.** For #748, DataPat sets the Large bit (offset 108) on the two-tile monsters that are Large in the Monster Manual but lacked it: MON2CHA 73, MON4CHA 39, MON8CHA 73 and 116. `C_DATA.VERSION` is 35. CURSE-0091, #748.

**Creature sizes (#792).** An inventory of the 104 monster records found SSI's Large bit nearly right. DataPat sets it on the Ogre Leader (MON1CHA 9, $01 to $81), an ogre chieftain, and no longer sets it on the huge scorpion (Medium, as the huge spider), which #748 had made Large. `C_DATA.VERSION` is 36. POR-0185, #792.

**The ninth posted proclamation is CCXIV (data version 37).** Outside City Hall (ECL3 block 0, `$AC58`) the script notes the proclamations posted so far: none paid gives LXIV, LXXVIII, CIX and LIX, and otherwise it ON GOSUBs one of nine string loads by the major rewards paid (`[$4AC1]`, `C_GBVM.COUNCIL_REWARDS`). SSI's ninth arm (`$AD21`) loaded "CXIV.", the fifth's, so CCXIV, the nomads, the one proclamation left, was never named. The packed "CCXIV." is five bytes to "CXIV."'s four, a byte more than the arm's room, so the DataPat fix points the ON GOSUB's ninth entry (`$AC8B`) at new code after #606's at the end of the script (`$B663`): SAVE "CCXIV.", str[$985E]; RETURN. 12 bytes; the script is 7,537 bytes. `JrnlQst.IsPosted` (#851) already counted CCXIV as posted at nine rewards. Checked with `gbt ecl disasm` on the patched block (the entry reads LB663, the SAVE "CCXIV.") and in TDataPat, which runs the new code through `EclVars.LoadEclVars` and checks the jump's target. SSI's data. POR-0209, #864.

**A fallen Skullcrusher leaves the party at City Hall.** Skullcrusher, who joins in the Textile House, leaves on the square outside City Hall (New Phlan, event 26), where the script searches the party for him ($AF56), says "I MUST GO TO MY MASTER NOW." and removes him ($AD56). The search passes over a member not in action (`[$6C00]` above 127) before it compares the name, so a dead or unconscious Skullcrusher was never found and stayed in the party, a body to carry until dropped at a training hall. A DataPat fix to ECL3 block 0 sends the search's call ($ABB8) to new code at the end of the script, after #1091's: the same search without the in-action test ($AF56 stays, since the Mad Man's checks share it). The jump to his farewell ($ABC3) goes there too: standing, SSI's farewell as before; fallen, "CADORNA'S PERSONAL GUARD CART THE FALLEN SKULLCRUSHER AWAY." (Brian's wording), the same removal, his items with him, the Return prompt, and back to the search for another. Princess Fatima and Dirten were already removed whatever their state. Data version 44. The bytes were assembled and the patched block disassembled and read back; replaying the whole table against the shipped data applies every part. Scenarios `skullcrusher` (a standing Skullcrusher and an unconscious one, both leave) and `skulldead` (a dead one); against the golden build the fallen one stays, by design. #1168.

**The Manual of Bodily Health's spell (data version 45).** The Manual placed in Phlan (ITEM2 block 1, record 0) cast spell 67 by its effect 90, and Curse's numbers, which the 4th- and 5th-level spells use, make 67 Neutralize Poison. Its effect becomes 151, spell 128 (`C_SPELL.ITEM_BODILY_HEALTH`, #1121).

## 8. Video (the driver refactor, VGA), memory, overlays and EMS

This section follows phase 6 of the refactor plan: the new video-driver structure, VGA support, and the overlay and memory work that made both practical.

**Greyed-out text in CGA is magenta (#1185).** `GameDefs.attrTable` maps DarkGray, like LightGray, Yellow and White, to CGA's white, so text the game greys out (`PartyMnu.DrawCmd`'s commands not offered, `Options`' `OFF_COLOR` lines, `ChgClass`'s classes not open, View's resting class, the area map's "Dim" legend) drew as bright as the text beside it. `VidCga.TextColour` gives `DrawGlyph` magenta (CGA 2) for DarkGray ink and `attrTable`'s colour for the rest; `attrTable` itself stays SSI's, since pictures go through it. A checkerboard of white at half density was tried first and checked in a CGA capture of the Settings pages: letters such as E, L, S and G lost too much to read ("LOG LINES (50)"), and the regression harness could not read the text at all. TVidPic checks the mapping.

**A video benchmark, and the regression suite in CGA and Tandy (phase 6, step 0).** `bench/vidbench.sh` compiles `bench/vidbench.pas` and runs it in each video mode under DOSBox-X at a fixed 10,000 cycles, timing `RenderChar`, `BlitTileCell`, `FillPicRect` and `FlushView` on game-shaped workloads. The baseline: EGA is slowest at everything, and its `FlushView` takes 25.7 ms for the whole view against CGA's 2.0 and Tandy's 2.9. `VIDEO=cga` and `VIDEO=tandy` make `tests/regress.sh` run in that mode (POOL-CGA.CFG, POOL-TANDY.CFG), each with its own golden record; Tandy uses a Tandy machine. bd7a01e. All nine scenarios pass in both modes, so phases 1 to 5 changed neither mode's screens (loadsave needed goldbox-player 14eda0e). 6127810.

**Game code no longer asks which video mode it is in (step 1).** Every video-mode branch outside the video units moves behind Blit or a new overlaid unit, `VidOps`: Blit gains `FrameBytes`, `MaskBytes`, `ColourCount` and `HasPalette` (and `SetPaletteRegister` does nothing without a palette); `VidOps` holds the cold operations (`ModeName`, `InitRowTables`, `MergePic`, `OverlayPic`, `RecolorPic`, `PutPixelRow`); CbtActs, CharDisp, MapTiles, CharGen and Portrait take mask and frame sizes from Blit. Only Blit, TextRend, DaxIo, GameBoot and VidOps read the mode afterwards. ce0ae5d.

**A video driver record (step 2a).** `Types.TVideoDriver`, in `GDVars.video`, describes the mode: name, colours, palette, and picture layout as shift counts (`cellShift`, `screenShift`, `maskShift`), with `CellFill`. Resident units `VidCga`, `VidEga` and `VidTdy` fill it; GameBoot installs the one POOL.CFG names. `BlitTile`, `FillPicRect`, `OverlayPic` and the size queries read the record instead of branching. `BlitTile` keeps a fast path for a mask byte per picture byte (CGA, Tandy), and `FillPicRect` fills a one-byte cell row as a run and stores EGA's four-byte cells as LongInts. The commit reports speed against step 0 as: tiles -7% on CGA and -4% on EGA and Tandy; the view fill +54% on CGA, +144% on EGA and unchanged on Tandy; fill and flush +24% on CGA and +21% on EGA. Read as speed, the tile blitter is a little slower through the record and the fills are faster. db8ff7b.

**The drivers draw to the screen (step 2b).** The record gains `biosMode`, `ramVideoParas` and entries `Start`, `FlushSpans`, `ShowPic`, `DrawGlyph`, `ClearRect`, `ClearScreen` and `WriteChar`; the per-mode code from Blit's, TextRend's and DaxIo's case arms moves into the drivers. GameBoot sets up every mode the same way: install the driver, keep a system-RAM screen (Tandy's) out of the heap, set the BIOS mode, call `Start`. b9e7517.

**Cold picture routines in an overlaid unit per mode (step 2c).** The record gains `ExpandFrames`, `MirrorFrame`, `RemapColour`, `RecolorFrame`, `PutPixelRow` and `InitRowTables`, implemented in overlaid `PicCga`, `PicEga` and `PicTdy`. Nothing reads the video mode any more, so `GDVars.videoMode` and `Types.TVideoMode` are gone: the driver is the mode. Resident code 3,072 bytes smaller; overlays 3,731 larger, one mode's unit loaded at a time. 07c1ee9.

**TVidPic tests each driver's picture routines (step 4).** Each driver is installed in turn and a one-cell, two-row picture is converted and checked against bytes worked out from the mode's layout: frame and mask sizes, data and mask, mirror, remap, fill and a masked blit (39 checks per mode). `build.sh`'s test runner also looks in `obj` for object files such as SOUNDDRV.OBJ. 9945bc4.

**Ctrl-Y shows the heap that pictures hold (step 5).** `Blit.AllocPic` and `FreePic` count picture memory (`GDVars.picBytes`, peak in `picPeak`), shown by STING Ctrl-Y after the heap line. Measured in EGA at the scenarios' ends, for weighing VGA's layouts. e0b713b.

**A VGA video driver: a byte a pixel, with a colour key.** `VidVga` (resident) and `PicVga` (overlaid) drive BIOS mode 13h, chosen by POOL.CFG's video letter V. Pictures take a byte a pixel (8 bytes a cell row), and a transparent pixel holds `Types.PIC_KEY` ($FF), as Dark Queen of Krynn does; the mask is still kept a bit a pixel, so code that checks or copies masks works unchanged. The 16 colours are DAC entries 0..15. The record gains `keyed` and `SetPalette` (EGA's BIOS palette call moves into `VidEga`; VGA writes the DAC). Blit's tile loop and VidOps' merge and overlay gain keyed paths, `Blit.ClearPic` clears a picture to transparent for CharGen's icons, and `TCellBytes` grows to 8 bytes. A picture too large for one 64 KB heap block continues in `TImgBuf.more`, followed by `AllocPic`, `FreePic`, `ExpandPicFrames`, `BlitTile` and `RemapPic`, with `Blit.FrameBlock` and `FrameCount` for MapTiles; of the game's 809 DAX pictures only SQRPACI's two wilderness tile sheets need it in VGA, and a single frame too large for any block stops with a message. TVidPic checks VGA and a split sheet (58 checks); `tests/regress.sh` runs VGA with `VIDEO=vga`. Heap measured: 156,864 free at boot (EGA 302,408), 84,896 at the end of combat. New feature: SSI's v1.3 had CGA, EGA and Tandy only. 2a8f97c.

**The second wilderness tile set loads when needed, and combat frees it.** SQRPACI block 2 (tile ids 128 and up) is drawn only by the wilderness and the ending, yet GameInit loaded it at boot and kept it all game (37 KB in EGA, 74 KB in VGA). `MapTiles.SecondSheet` loads it the first time a tile from it is drawn or the ending remaps it, and `LoadCombatTileSet` frees it (`FreeSecondSheet`). VGA at the end of combat: 158,672 bytes free with a 134,920 largest block, from 84,896 and 56,280. a745a44.

**The second wilderness tile set loads in 16 KB blocks.** In VGA on a 1 MB machine with no EMS, coming back to the wilderness map after a fight stopped at `OUT OF MEMORY: ENTER SAVES, ESC QUITS` (save G, the 32-step walk to the stirges, a Quick fight, No to Continue Battle, Exit from the loot; Ctrl-Y there `Heap free 108040 big 47816`). Combat frees SQRPACI block 2 (a745a44, above) and the map reloads it; in VGA its 128 frames take 73,728 bytes, which `Blit.AllocPic` split at 65,520 into a block of 113 frames and one of 15, and after the fight no free block held 65 KB though the heap held the 74 in all. `AllocPic` and `LoadPic` become the 65,520-byte case (the new `Blit.MAX_BLOCK`) of `AllocPicBlocks` and `LoadPicBlocks`, which take the largest block a picture may use; a frame larger than that still gets a block of its own. `MapTiles.SecondSheet` loads the set with `LoadPicBlocks` in blocks of `MapTiles.SHEET_BLOCK`, 16,384 bytes: 28 frames a block in VGA (five blocks), 56 in EGA and Tandy (three), 113 in CGA (two), a 27-byte header a block more. Every reader of a frame already finds its block through `Blit.FrameBlock`. Measured in VGA at 1 MB, back on the map after that fight: `Heap free 65232 big 38584` with the set loaded; in Move mode (#1186), whose wide buffer comes back beside it, `56976 big 28256` (main stopped with Out of memory there as well); before the fight, three steps from save G, `96848 big 57144`, pictures 211,816 (81 more than before, the extra headers). Two fights in one session leave the same figures after each (65,232 free, pictures 242,900), so the 31 KB more held after a fight than before it is no leak: it is 31,084 bytes, the loot screen's picture (PIC block 1, four frames of 88 by 11 cells, 4 x (7,744 + 27)), which `Portrait.LoadAnimation` keeps in `animData` until another picture replaces it. Not chosen: keeping the set loaded through wilderness fights, as SSI did, which would cost combat 74 KB in VGA. Tests: TBlit (a 128-frame VGA sheet under a 16,384 cap is five blocks, none over it, frame 127 the fifth's 16th; uncapped it is still 113 and 15; a frame over the cap gets a block alone; freeing returns every byte), and the scenarios `wildafter` and `wildmoveafter`, which `tests/regress.sh` runs only in VGA (their `#video vga` line, new): with `VIDEO` unset apart from the rest, as `VIDEO=vga` would, its report in `obj/regress-vga.json`, and under another `VIDEO` not at all. #1218.

**Overlays load from EMS when there is room.** GameBoot calls `OvrInitEMS` after `OvrInit`, so the overlays are copied into EMS when there is enough and load from there rather than GAME.OVR; without EMS, or with too little, they load from disk as before. `GDVars.ovrInEms` records which, and Ctrl-Y's buffer line ends "from EMS" or "from disk". Conventional memory is unchanged. `tests/regress.sh` takes `MEMSIZE=N`; the suite passes at 1 MB (disk) in EGA, CGA and Tandy and at 16 MB (EMS) in EGA. 3d10dc2.

**Overlay probation and the load count.** GameBoot turns on overlay probation (`OvrSetRetry`, a third of the buffer), so units a stretch of play keeps calling stay in the buffer, and counts every overlay load through `OvrReadBuf`; Ctrl-Y shows it. #612. 5226426.

**Phase 6 status.** The plan records phase 6's status and what is left. de5c623.

Phase 5's overlay working-set changes are in section 10.

---

**Wall symbol 45 in every slot.** `View3D.LoadWallSet` moved every wall symbol from 45 up into the slot's own 8x8 set, but `Put8x8Sym` puts 1..45 in set 0, so in slots 2 and 3 symbol 45 drew a glyph of another set. The remap now starts at 46 (`symSetFirst[1]`), as Curse's does; `GDConsts.symSetLast` is gone. SSI's code. CURSE-0340, #727.

**A picture that loaded nothing is tried again.** `Portrait.LoadAnimation` recorded the picture as loaded as soon as its block was read, so one whose first frame did not fit in memory (or had a bad header, or a count of 0) stayed blank. It is recorded after the frame loop, and only when a frame loaded. SSI's code. CURSE-0325, #726.

**A zero-sized box fill fills nothing.** `Blit.FillPicRect` with a height of 0 at a row of 0 or more ran its row loop 65536 times past the view buffer. It returns at once for a width or height of 0. TVidPic checks it. Latent. SSI's code. CURSE-0343, #728.

**Monster icons stay in slots 8..12.** `EclLoadMon` capped its slot at 25, so a sixth monster kind between CLEAR MONSTERS loaded over the COMSPR combat sprites (13 on), and a mirror duel (`EclMenu.CharFightsAlone`) after five kinds put its icon there too. `MapTiles.FIRST_MONSTER_ICON` and `LAST_MONSTER_ICON` name the slots; a sixth LOAD MONSTER is ignored and the duel's clone shares slot 12, as in Curse. Latent with the shipped scripts. SSI's code. CURSE-0248, #724; CURSE-0264, #725.

**The Sound Blaster falls back to the speaker.** `SndSb.PlayEffect` ignored whether the DSP took its start commands and returned True, so `Play` never fell back. It returns the result. #820.

**POOL.CFG's S is silent; B is the Sound Blaster.** SSI's IBM data card gives POOL.CFG's sound letters as P (the speaker), T (Tandy) and S (silent). Phase 7 step 4 (248f50f) took S for the Sound Blaster, so an SSI-written POOL.CFG set to silent played sound. `GameBoot` now installs `SndSb` for B and `SndNone` for S and any other letter, as the Curse port does with CURSE.CFG; natively `SndDig` plays T, A and B. `tests/regress.sh` writes B for `SOUND=sb`, and the sound table in docs/refactor-plan.md names it. A POOL.CFG written with S for the Blaster now runs silent until it says B. Port-only. #880.

## 9. Robustness guards from the source review

A unit-by-unit source review (issues #424 to #466) found places where damaged data, an out-of-range value, or an unusual path could read or write beyond a table. Most of these guards do not change normal play with the shipped data; they make failures predictable and keep bad input from turning into memory corruption.

**Wilds.** `BuildWildernessMap` read `wildMap` for the 5x5 cells from the corner with no bound; it now moves the corner onto the 44x36 map first, as `DisplayMapView` does. #466. 4726a68.

**Pathing.** `WalkLine` fails at once for an endpoint outside the 50x25 arena; `IsDirToward` counts any direction above CENTER as CENTER (it read past `dirDX`/`dirDY` and left its result unset for 9..254); `BuildTargetList` stops at 72 creatures; `bestK` and `bestJ` start at 0. #457. 762382c.

**PostCbt.** `DetermineLootExp`'s per-hit-point experience product of two Bytes was computed as an Integer and could wrap negative (200 * 200 gives -25,536); one factor is now a LongInt. #459. 9126e4c.

**GameTime.** `TimeOutEffects` summed minutes in a Word (overflowing at 46 days, or any months or years) and took segments as minutes; the total is a LongInt and segments count ten to the minute. An expiring effect is removed only while its id and duration still match, so a handler that frees one effect and allocates another at the same address cannot have the new one removed (caused under #144). `CheckMemorizeDone` and `CheckScribeDone` pass over a pending spell outside 1..`C_SPELL.LAST`. `AdvanceSpellPrep`, `AdvanceSpellPrepBase` and `CampRest` stop at the last of the 8 prep timers. #444. a2a78a7.

**Money comments.** `MakeChange`'s comment now puts the copper-loss point at about 429,581 gp (caused under #130), and `TakeMoneyMenu`'s no longer says the coin list leaks (fixed under #162). #456. 8cd006c.

**Ending.** `PlayFinalAnim` returns if the FINAL picture did not load, instead of reading frame 0 outside the table; `LoadGame` holds `gameDelay` to 9, the top of the speed menus, so `WinGame`'s cycle counts and other delay-scaled waits cannot wrap. #440. 0866afc.

**Intro comments.** `DelayOrKey` and the title-2 loop never read the key, so one key ends the rest of the introduction (v1.3's behaviour); the comments now say so. #451. c1a966c.

**GameInit.** `GameInit2` read ITEMS under `$I-` without `IOResult`, leaving an error pending for the next check and missing a short read; a failed or short read (fewer than 128 records) now stops with "Unable to read ITEMS.". #443. 5551afb.

**HeapErr.** `HeapErrorHandler` now sets its result (0) before anything else; for a zero-size request it returned whatever was in the slot. Turbo Pascal never calls it for zero bytes. #447. 9a3220b.

**GDConsts.** Class usability bits or-ed rather than summed (section 1). #445. 1683b84.

**GDVars.** The 45 readers of `baseItems[0..127]` indexed it with an item's Byte type unchecked; they go through `CharHlp.BaseItem`, which returns an always-zero overflow entry for a type past the table (new `Types.PBaseItemRec`). #446. 6b88e1b.

**Picture caches key on the disk and forget failed loads.** `Portrait.LoadAnimation` cached on (name, picId) though the archive opened is name plus the virtual disk, and recorded the key before the load, so a failed load was cached too; after a disk switch the same PIC id gave the previous disk's picture or nothing. `LoadPortraitBufs` had the same disk-blind key. The key now includes the disk digit and is set only once data is returned; each portrait slot records its disk and reloads when it differs, and a failed slot stays invalid. POR-0134, #408. 3dff605.

**CharGen.** `CreateCharacterMenu`'s header comment brought up to date (aborts free the character and list since #165); `dsStartingAge`'s class axis grows to 0..7 with the DMG's human monk age (21 + 1d4), since creation rolls age for any class up to MONK. #430. 537400e.

**CharHlp.** `CombatAnimateMovingIcon` read `steps[148]` on a 49-cell path; `steps` has a 149th slot left at "no move", and the filling loop stops at 148 steps. #431. 1eebf28.

**Camp.** `CampScribeMenu` refuses ("You can not scribe that spell.") a scroll spell past `spellsKnown` or with a table level outside 1..3 or a casting class outside 0..1, rather than indexing unchecked; `CampFixMenu`'s comment now says a conscious (status OKAY) caster. #427. ca0c18e.

**MenuCore.** `MenuVertical` paged on N and P whether or not Next or Prev was offered, which on a short list made the top negative and dereferenced nil; N and P now page only when offered. #455. bfd8a41.

**TextRend.** `RenderChar` draws nothing for a raw glyph index past the 177-glyph font; `DisplayMessageWindow` splits a word wider than the window and `PrintCharInWindow` stops at `lrCol`; `ClearRect` and `FillRectEga` do nothing unless the rectangle is on the 40x25 screen with its corners in order; `DisplayMessageWindow` requires `lrRow <= 24`. #463. 056a9bd.

**Shop.** `ShopBuyItemSelection` copied up to 40 characters of name into a `String[30]`; the copy now stops short of the price with a blank between. #461. a6d8efa.

**ViewChar.** `UseItemMenu`'s loop also ends when the item list is nil; `UseItemSpell` and `Magic.CastSpellNC` cast nothing for a spell id outside the 67-entry table. #465. 7f4895f.

**C_Keys comments.** `ALT_Q` and `ALT_R` move to the extended scan-code group with `ALT_X` and `ALT_M`; the build was byte-identical. #450. 778d431.

**Portrait.** `LoadAnimation` ends loading at a frame whose header or pixels run past the block or whose height or width is 0 (which would wrap `planeSz` and overrun), keeping the frames before it; a later PIC or FINAL frame is XORed only over the first frame's size. #458. c11fda5.

**Combat slot 0 outside CbtMap.** `CbtMap.CombatSlot` returns 0 for a creature not in the combat arrays; the direct `arenaLoc`/`gridX` indexers in other units now treat that as not on the map: `CbtActs.CombatMoveCreature`, `MoveOpportunityAttacks`, `StingVictoryCheat`, `CharHlp.PrintNameStringAnim`, `FxEngine.PutOutOfPlayWithStatus` and `Combat.MoveMenu`'s Escape undo. #515. a965314.

**Blood drain and bite-hold.** `EffBloodAttach` and `EffBiteHold` attach nothing when the attacker has no target or the target has no combat slot; `CbtActs.BiteAndHold` gets the guard `EffBloodDrain` already had (a strength of 0, or past `arenaCount` or 72, has no victim, and the hold is removed). #530. 7706b6c.

**Hooks start unset.** Writing tests for CharHlp found that `CharHlp.faultHook` was never initialised to nil (globals are not cleared at start-up), so in a test program `RemoveItem` called garbage; CharHlp's initialization now unsets it, and GameIo's unsets `saveSettings` and `showMemory`. In the game all three are set during start-up. e77b594.

---

**LOAD CHARACTER removes only a member it finds.** With bit 7 and both dump flags set, `EclVm.EclLoadChar` removed the current character even when the index found no one, and a removed character that was `savedChar` stayed there for EXIT, PROGRAM or `NewEclDone` to make current again. It now removes only a found member and moves `savedChar` to the new current character; `NewEclDone` keeps its starting character in `savedChar` (as Curse) rather than a local LOAD CHARACTER could not update. Latent. SSI's code. CURSE-0353, #759.

**Fleeing counts Haste and Slow on the first member.** `EclMenu.MoveExtremes` seeded the party's fastest and slowest from the first member's unadjusted movement. They now start at 0 and 255, so only adjusted values count, and an empty list reads no nil pointer. SSI's code. CURSE-0350, #758.

**Globals start at zero under Turbo Pascal.** Turbo Pascal 6 does not clear global variables: a test under DOSBox-X found a 30,000-byte global holding whatever earlier programs left (28,778 nonzero bytes, all of them after a program that dirtied memory). Free Pascal zeroes them. GDVars' initialisation now clears every variable from `video` to `inEncMenu` (which must stay last) under `{$IFNDEF FPC}`, before any unit that writes it initialises; among them `stinkHead` (the cloud list, walked when a caster's cloud effect ends before the session's first fight), `pageMain` (passed to the BIOS by the CGA, Tandy and VGA drivers) and `creatureList`. MenuCore's initialisation now also sets `enterToCaller`, which HorizontalMenu reads on Enter, and a new EclVm initialisation clears the monster state (`creatureCnt`, `monstersOn`, `monId`, `monPicId`) that only CLEAR MONSTERS and SETUP MONSTER set. The rest of the units' globals were traced and are set before use. TGDVars checks the GDVars range (failing all 8 checks against the old unit after a memory-dirtying program). #826.

**A creature's side is `hostile and 1` everywhere.** Prayer stored `hostile * PRAYER_SIDE` plus the caster level, Charm `hostile * 128` and the target's old `hostile * 64`, and Animate Dead gave the corpse the caster's raw byte, so a side byte above 1 (only a damaged save can bring one: every monster record holds 0 or 1) read back wrong. `CharHlp.Team` gives the side, and `FxEngine.PrayerStrength` and `PrayerSide` pack and read Prayer's, with the level capped at 15. TProtect checks it. POR-0207, #827.

**A game, new or loaded, starts with no monsters.** EclVm's monster state (`creatureCnt`, `monstersOn`, `monId`, `monPicId`) was reset only by CLEAR MONSTERS, SETUP MONSTER and, since #826, at startup, so a new game or a load kept what the last area set up: a script that loaded monsters without CLEAR MONSTERS first started from the old count, and fewer kinds loaded before the cap. `EclVm.ForgetMonsters` clears all four at startup and at the start of every `GameLoop`, which every new game and every load (main menu, training hall, quick load) runs. #829.

**EclVars: script reads with no value give 0 (2026-10-25 review).** `ReadGbvm`, `SpecialReadZ1` and `GetEclVar` left the result unassigned for the cells that have no value (`CHAR_CONTROL_HI`, `CHAR_LOYALTY` outside Charisma 3..25, `CELL_35F`, `CELL_WALL_COLOR`, an unlisted zone-4 address) and for a variable of an unknown type, so a script read whatever the stack held, and the DOS and native builds could differ; they read 0. `GetCell` and `PutCell` hold the per-zone cell access that `WriteGbvm`, `ReadGbvm`, `WriteGbvmString` (four copies of its loop) and `DerefGbString` each spelled out; `LoadEclList` loads the list after a count (the `Dec(eclIp)` five places wrote); CELL_FACING takes the value mod 4 in place of a subtracting loop; the party-index cells share an arm; the NPC morale bits, the last disk and opcode `C_ECL.UNUSED_1F` are named. #952.

**EclVm: named CALL targets and script entries (2026-10-25 review).** `EclCall` cases on the CALL address, named in C_GBVM (`CALL_REFRESH_VIEW`, `CALL_MIRROR_DUEL`, `CALL_FIGHT_ALONE`, `CALL_SOUND`, `CALL_WALL_AHEAD`, `CALL_MOVE_FORWARD`), where it cased on the address less $7FFF. The script header's entries are `C_ECL.ENTRY_MOVE`, `ENTRY_SEARCH`, `ENTRY_PRE_CAMP`, `ENTRY_CAMP_INTERRUPT` and `ENTRY_LOAD` in EclVm, EclMenu and EvtKind. `RestoreLoadedChar` is the block `EclExit` and `EclProgram` shared; `EclClock` passes `C_TIME.MINUTES`; `EclCompare` no longer copies its strings to the stack; `EclRandom` no longer takes `Random`'s Word through Real; `EclCheckParty` indexes Find Traps from C_GBVM. Behaviour is unchanged. #950.

**EclMenu: area cells by name (2026-10-25 review).** `EclInit` clears `C_GBVM.AREA_CELLS_FIRST..LAST` ($4A00..$4A1F) and `AREA_TEMPS_FIRST..LAST` ($6E79..$6E82) and reads the header at `SCRIPT_FIRST`, where it used the literals. `EclVerticalMenu` is gone (its one caller passed an empty menu; `EclVMenu` calls `MenuCore.MenuVertical`); `EclGosub` pushes in one branch; `LoadCreatureData` rolls the icon colours in one loop, in the same order. #951.

**EvtKind: an IF as an event's one instruction (2026-10-25 review).** `KindAt` overwrote its opcode with the one after an IF, so for a COMPARE/IF = dispatch whose instruction is itself an IF, the single-instruction check tested the wrong opcode: the Companion map followed the IF's skip path, code the event does not own, and dropped the instruction it guards unless that was a GOTO or GOSUB. An IF now hands the one instruction on to the one it guards. #953.

**SaveLoad (2026-10-25 review).** `SaveCharlist` rewrote charlist.txt in place without checking its writes, so a full disk left a cut-short roster; it writes charlist.tx$ and puts it in place (`FileUtil.PutInPlace`) only when whole. `LoadCharlist` never checked its `Reset` or read `IOResult`, which could leave an error pending for `SaveCharlist`'s `Rewrite` and ask for the save disk with nothing wrong; it clears a pending error first, gives up on an open or a line that fails, and leaves none behind. `LoadChar` loses its `askDisk` parameter (True at both callers) and the dead game-disk branch; `LoadSlot` uses `CharFileName` and `Types.MAX_PARTY` (#919); `SaveMinSize` counts the five position bytes by their sizes; small cleanups in `AddCharToParty`, `FreeCreature` and `CheckFileElseQuit`. The save layout is unchanged. #967. 9e4aa83.

**SaveMenu (2026-10-25 review).** The menu's per-slot tables are `SLOT_COUNT` (the length of `SLOT_LETTERS`) long instead of 16, 364 bytes of the data segment back; the boxes' columns are `BOX_LEFT`/`BOX_RIGHT`, the title is centred with `TextRend.DisplayCentered`, and `ReadSlot` seeks past `virtualDisk` by its size. #968. 4335f7e.

**FileUtil (2026-10-25 review).** `OpenReadOnly` clears an I/O error already pending before its `Reset`, as the unit's writers do: under `{$I-}` a pending error made the `Reset` do nothing and a file that is there read as missing (TFileUtl now checks it, and fails without the fix). `TempName` and `BackupName` share `WithLast`. #969. 4947c46.

**CfgFile (2026-10-25 review).** `Trim` (spaces and tabs off both ends) replaces the copies in `Tidy` and the native `CheckPath`; `ReadPoolCfg`'s four kept lines are `C_OPT.CFG_EXTRA_LINES`; its comment keeps to 120 columns. #970. 7c86179.

**Options (2026-10-25 review).** `ShownValue` reads the settings `DrawSetting` has just collected instead of collecting them again; `CountMerged` counts to `Types.LAST_DISK` (#919). #971. 3c36600.

**POOLCFG's keys and paths (2026-10-25 review).** Natively on Linux and macOS, `TuiKit.GetKey` took the last byte of an escape sequence cut short (Alt-[ or Alt-O, ESC and '[' or 'O' with nothing after) for its final character, so the key and its Escape were lost; it is now Escape, and the console's ESC [ 0 is Escape only with its '~'. Under DOS, `TuiKit.Init` reads the cursor and its shape before switching to 80-column colour text, so Done puts back the ones it found. In POOLCFG, `SetPath` marks a Pool path too long for START however it was set, and `ReadFiles` normalizes the defaults too: a default save folder in a folder too deep for DOS was written unmarked and START then stopped on it. `NormalizeRead` upper-cases a path read from POOL.CFG under DOS, as `CfgFile.CheckPath` does one typed in, so the same folder typed again is no change and gives no note that the saves stay behind. The path field takes 255 characters natively (`PATH_TYPED`), not DOS's 79. Consolidated: `TuiKit.MoveInList` for the four lists' Up/Down/Home/End (tests/ttuikit.pas), StrUtil's `NumStr` and `PadRight` for POOLCFG's own copies, `ReadState` for the Files box's two read-state cases, `LineName` for the Hardware labels. #982, #983.

**GameInit, GameBoot and GameIo (2026-10-25 review).** `GameInit.GameInit2` cleared two pointers' worth from `GameVars.mapTilePtr`, as if MapTiles' `mapTilePtr2` still followed it as in SSI's data segment, writing into the next GameVars variable (harmless only because that is `iconAnimation`, cleared on the next line); it now sets the one pointer. Its loading variables are locals (about 210 bytes of the data segment), the dragon-breath handler goes in with the other effect handlers as `C_EFFECT.DRAGON_BREATH`, `ResetGameState` starts the script at `C_GBVM.SCRIPT_FIRST` and drops a party-size store that zone 1's clearing had done, and `GameVars.norandArg` (set by GameBoot) replaces the two tests of `ParamStr(2)`. GameBoot loses `gfxReserve`, which nothing read; GameIo names its control keys (`C_KEYS.CTRL_*`) and shares `SetStartMode` between ExitGame and FatalError. #984, #985, #986.

**TpRand, Clock, Debug and MemShow (2026-10-25 review).** `TpRand.NextSeed`'s products pass MaxLongInt and are exact only because they wrap: the unit now turns Free Pascal's overflow and range checks off, and its comment says so. Clock's comments say a carry carries on upward in the same call and that a rest can hold 45 days. Debug drops an unused unit; MemShow works out where overlays load from only in the DOS build. #987, #988, #989, #990.

**Bestiary and BeastFil (2026-10-25 review).** `Bestiary.Mark` sets or clears a fact and says whether that changed the entry; `SetFact`, `LearnSize` and `BeastFil.Tell` call it in place of three copies of the bit arithmetic. `BeastFil.LoadFrom` marks the Bestiary read-only for the session when it drops an entry for want of heap (a named one the table does not have), so the next `Flush` cannot write a table short of the file over BESTIARY.DAT. #992, #993.

**BeastMnu (2026-10-25 review).** `DrawEntry` counts the ?? lines under the defenses and attacks only when the entry is not marked told (#868), so a told entry with 9 or 10 known facts keeps one column; `ListPages` and `DrawList` share `PageShape`. #994.

**JrnlList (2026-10-25 review).** The unused `IsImageOnly` is gone; the list's parts are `C_JRNL.LAST_PROCLAMATION` and `LAST_ENTRY`; `EntryLabel` and `EntryTitle` share `Heading`. TJrnl now checks each item's `LineCount` against the lines its text unit holds (its old check could not fail). The text units Journal1 to Journal4 were checked item by item (line counts, runs of 9, widths, the item comments) and are sound. #995.

**LogView (2026-10-25 review).** The comment on `lineCounts` says why it runs one past `CbtLog.MAX_ENTRIES`. #996.

**Portrait (2026-10-25 review).** `LoadPortraitBufs` loads each slot through one local routine; the disk digit comes from `StrUtil.NumStr`; `LoadAnimation`'s cache hit needs the record to hold frames; `Show3DSprite` takes its animation by reference; `DisplayPortraitHead` and `DisplayPortraitBody` are folded into `DisplayPortraitBufs`. #997.

**The 64-bit native game's attacks are counted where they are kept.** `Types.TAttackPhaseView`, CbtActs' view of a character's armor class and attacks as arrays, skipped 273 bytes to `acCur`, which is right only where a pointer is 4 bytes: 17 pointers come before it. In the x86_64 build its `acCurArr` and `curAtt` lay in the cloak's and the boots' `equipped` pointers, so `DoAttack` counted and spent blows in pointer bytes (a creature with no cloak made no first-phase blow) and `Bestiary.LearnArmor` read one as the defender's AC. The pad now grows by `PTR_GROWTH` for each pointer, and TRecLay checks the view against `acCur` and `curAttacks`. Unused types went (`TInterruptRegisters`, `TPortraitCacheSlot`, `TAgeRec`, `TGoldDice`, `TRaceLim`, `PDownedRec`, `TLoW`, six string types), `TGbvmClock.values` is a `TClock`, and `TSoundDriver`'s comments name every driver. #1045.

**GameDefs' tables are their contents' size.** `classBit` keeps the eight entries anything reads; `terrainInfo` drops `clearance`, 0 in every row, and `Pathing.WalkLine` starts its meter at 0; the name tables (`classNames`, `raceNames`, `alignNames`, `moneyNames`, `statusStrs`) are as wide as their longest names: 131 bytes of the data segment. Spell 7 reads 'Protection From Good', as the other Protection spells do. #1046.

**GameVars' paths are DOS paths.** `gameDir` and `saveDirectory` are `Types.TPathStr` (80 bytes on DOS, 256 natively), 352 bytes of the data segment, as long as POOL.CFG's paths can be. `breathAim`, which only `castTargetMenu` wrote, is `Magic.DragonBreathHandler`'s own local. #1047.

**Declarations.** C_EFFECT names the ready-item effects 130 (`READY_FLAME_TONGUE`, the well's Flame Tongue, #20) and 136 (`READY_UNDEAD_SLAYER`, the Two-Handed Sword +1, +3 vs. Undead) and notes that nothing adds 92 or 138 (#1048). TSaveLay checks zone 0's fields at C_GBVM's names for them, which nothing else used, and C_GBVM's `SETTINGS` gives the music bit (#1049). C_VIDEO's EGA mode is 0Dh (#1050). Sdl3Min holds `SDL_SCALEMODE_PIXELART`, which SdlHost declared, and drops four declarations nothing used (#1051, #1052).

**The 2026-10-25 review: video and pictures.** A unit-by-unit review of Blit, BldShow, VidOps, the four video drivers and the four picture units. Nothing changes on screen: a new test, TBlit, pins the output of every picture routine in all four modes (and the screen memory CGA and Tandy write) with hashes taken from the code before the review, and the refactors keep them. 65a410b.

**Blit.** The picture helpers only the overlaid Pic units call (`ComputeMask`, `MirrorBits`, `MirrorPairs`, `MirrorNibbles`) and `ExpandPicFrames` move to the overlaid VidOps; resident code is 496 bytes smaller. `MarkSpan` keeps the dirty-span bookkeeping BlitTile and FillPicRect each wrote out, and `ClearSpans` the reset FlushView and `GameInit.GameInit2` each did; BlitTile and RemapPic find a frame's block with `FrameBlock`. Guards: BlitTileClipped with a negative row stops at the destination's last row (SSI did not check it there), RemapPic leaves a frame past the picture's last alone, and AllocPic takes a frame of no rows or cells without dividing by 0. The descriptions take in VGA. #1034. 6dc17c8, 780aae6.

**The picture units (PicCga, PicEga, PicTdy, PicVga).** Their `ExpandFrames` each ran the same frame, row and cell loop over the DAX chunks; `VidOps.ExpandPicFrames` runs it once and hands each cell row and its mask bits to the driver's `ExpandChunk`, which stores that cell row in its layout (`VidOps.ChunkPixel` gives a chunk's pixel). PicEga's and PicVga's mask mirror is one `VidOps.MirrorCellMask`. RemapColour's dither test loses a redundant `isDithered`. #1039, #1040, #1041, #1042. 6dc17c8, 3a75def.

**VidCga and VidTdy.** Both modes build one row table, `GameVars.bankRowSeg`, in place of `cgaRowSeg` and `tandyRowSeg` (400 bytes of data segment back), and Tandy installs CGA's `FlushSpans` and `ShowPic`, which count with the driver's shifts. CGA's DrawGlyph has one loop for both banks; Tandy's writes each font row through the row table in place of a case over four bank arrays, and its CellFill computes the fill byte. #1036, #1037. 3a75def.

**VidEga.** The second display page was always page 0, so DrawGlyph wrote every byte twice, WriteChar called the BIOS twice and ClearRect and ClearScreen also cleared page 1 (FlushSpans' staging area); they draw once, on page 0, and `pageNum`, `egaOtherPage` and `SetupEgaOtherPage` go. DrawGlyph uses `GameDefs.egaPlaneBit`. #1038. 6e98c6d.

**VidOps.** `MergePic` checks that the destination is as wide and as large as the source (CharGen checked first, SaveLoad did not; the data keeps them in step); `RecolorPic` refuses an empty picture, which VGA's and Tandy's RecolorFrame would loop over 65536 times. #1035. 443d25d.

**BldShow.** Open's memory check counted 32 bytes a cell picture; VGA's are 64. It counts `Blit.FrameBytes(8, 1)`. #1043. 31d1c53.

**The Sound Blaster with no AdLib plays its music on the speaker.** With a DSP answering and no OPL2, `SndOpl.Install` fell back to the speaker, and `SndSb.Install` then set the mode back to `MODE_ADLIB` while `SndSb.Play` sent the music to `SndOpl.Play`: the music played to an AdLib that was not there, and was silent. `Install` now keeps the mode `SndOpl.Install` leaves and remembers its Play (`musicPlay`), which `Play` uses for the music, the effects with no recording and silence; a recorded effect silences the music with `musicPlay(0)`. `ReadBlaster`'s indexes are Words, so a 255-character BLASTER cannot wrap them, and the bounce buffer is sized to the longest sample that crosses a 64 KB page instead of the longest in the file. Port-only. #1030.

**SBSFX.DAT is read in one place.** `SndSb.LoadEffects` and `SndDig.LoadEffects` each had SBSFX.DAT's layout and its reading. The new overlaid unit `SfxFile` has them (`Load`, `CheckHeader`, `GetEntry`, `TSfxEntry`), and `C_SOUND` names the formats (`SFX_PCM8`, `SFX_ADPCM4`) and the last effect (`SFX_LAST`, `MUSIC_TITLE - 1`). `SndDig.DecodeAdpcm` takes the buffer and an offset, where it indexed past a one-byte open array, and a second entry for an effect frees the first one's bytes. Tests: `TSfxFile`. #1030, #1031.

**The sound sequencer's loaders, clock and ports, once each.** `ProgramSpeakerVoices` and `ProgramTandyVoices` (sndspk.inc, sndtdy.inc) jump to one `program_voices` in sounddrv.asm, as the native `SndSeq.ProgramVoices` already was one; `UninstallSoundTimer` calls `tandy_off` and the new `spkr_gate_off`, which `SetSoundDevice` shares. The timer's rate, spelled 2367, 236697 and 1193182/5041 in `SoundDrv`, `SndSeq` and `SndChip`, is `C_SOUND.PIT_HZ` over `TIMER_DIVISOR`, and the ports `SndSeq` writes and `SndChip.Write` decodes are `C_SOUND.PORT_PIT2`, `PORT_GATE` and `PORT_TANDY`. `SoundDrv.SoundSuspend` calls `SilenceAll`; `C_SOUND`'s effect ids are grouped and commented. TSndSeq's digests are unchanged. #1028, #1029, #1032, #1033.

**DataArc, the archives' names (2026-10-25 review).** The game's disk count was a private `SOURCE_DISKS = 8` in DataArc, DataBld and BldShow, a literal 8 in `DaxIo.SourcesMatch`, `FamilyUsable` and `Options.CountMerged`, and `['1'..'8']` in `DataArc.MatchFamily`; all use `Types.LAST_DISK`. A per-disk archive's file name, built by hand as `SourceName + '.DAX'` in DaxIo, BldShow, Options and Sage and by DataBld's own `SourceName`, is `DataArc.SourceFile`. TDataArc checks it. #963. 176376b.

**DataBld (2026-10-25 review).** `DecodeRuns`, a copy of `DaxIo.DecompressDax`, is gone: `PatchBlock` decodes a block to fix with DaxIo's decoder. The 36000-byte packed-block limit at which `LoadDax` ends the game is `C_DATA.PACKED_LIMIT`, and `MAX_LOADABLE` (35718) is computed from it. #961. d258374.

**DaxIo (2026-10-25 review).** `LoadDax` read a directory entry before asking whether one was left, so an archive with an empty directory, or one that is not a whole number of 9-byte entries, had its first data bytes taken for an entry, whose id could match and whose packed size could end the game with 'tempsize'; it now reads only whole entries inside the directory, each as one `DataArc.TDirEntry`. `DataDiskMsg` builds its line from the disk number instead of eight typed-constant lines (240 bytes of the data segment) and names no disk for a `virtualDisk` outside 1..8. `FileExists` answers False for an empty name without opening standard input. SSI's code. #962. d5e6002.

**A character's file name loses every unwanted character.** `DaxIo.SanitizeString` made one pass and moved on after each removal, so of two in a row only one went: "SIR  BOB" was saved with a space in its file name and "A....B" as `A.B.CHA`, which DOS reads as a name and an extension. `StrUtil.RemoveAll` takes its place and removes every occurrence, including those a removal brings together; `SanitizeFileName` uses it, and `SaveLoad.CharFileName`'s extra pass for periods is gone (`LoadParty` names members through it). A character saved before under a name with a doubled character is looked for under the new name. TStrUtil checks it. SSI's code. POR-0224, #960. 4c729c4.

**DataPat (2026-10-25 review).** Every part was checked against the shipped v1.3 archives in table order (sizes, offsets, original bytes) and each of the 452 instruction comments decoded from its bytes; all agree but #868's Slums part, whose comments gave the GOSUB's operand address ($A757) for the GOSUB's ($A755). Comments only; the bytes and `C_DATA.VERSION` are unchanged. The table was 3,564 bytes of typed constants in the data segment for the whole game; DataPat is now overlaid, and its `FillParts` and `FillBytes` give the same table as `Part` calls and `Put` calls on string constants, which Turbo Pascal keeps in the unit's code. `LoadTable` puts it on the heap for `BuildDataFiles`, which checks that it adds up and frees it when the archives are built (the data segment's free space goes from 18,386 to 21,938 bytes; the overlay file grows by about 17 KB, loaded only while archives are built). TDataPat loads it. #964. 9dcb2f5, 2cfad9d.

**DiskRec (2026-10-25 review).** `FindPointers` and `CHAR_POINTERS` count the equipped-item pointers from `C_EQUIP.WEAPON..BOLT` rather than 0..12 and 17. TDiskRec checks the count. #965. c93e4be.

**ViewChar: the 2026-10-25 review.** `UseItemMenu`'s Deposit/Drop case called `CheckItemAvail` again when the first call refused in the vault, falling through to the Drop branch: No to "is it Okay to lose it?" for a scroll the character was scribing from asked again, and Yes to the repeat offered to drop the scroll forever instead of depositing it. It asks once and then deposits or drops by the interface state; the loop's inner `itemsAddr <> nil` test, which repeated its own condition, is gone. `ViewItemDetail`'s labels are literals in the overlay rather than a typed constant (210 bytes of the data segment back), `CannotCarryItem` tests `C_ITEM.CARRY_MAX`, `TradeTreasureMenu` and `DropMoneyMenu` pass `LightGreen` to `InputNumberMax`, and `IdentifyItem` pauses in its one paid block. Left: `ItemInfo` finds an item's list line as CharEdit's `LineOf` does (PR #959); a MenuCore helper for both waits for #959. #973.

**CharDisp, TextRend, Border and Sym8x8: the 2026-10-25 review.** `ShowBottomLine`, `PrintBottomLine` and `PrintNameStringAnim` say they pause on the text delay, not wait for a key; `SelectChar` no longer tests for `C_KEYS.ESC`, which MenuHorizontal never returns (#974). TextRend's `ClearRect` has back its comment, which sat over `DelayOrKey`'s body, and `LoadGameFont`'s `compSize`, LoadDax's unpacked size, is `size` (#976). `Border.DrawWindow` says it centres between `ulCol` and `lrCol - 1` because that is SSI's placement (#977), and Sym8x8 drops a `{$I-}` (#978).

**MenuCore: the 2026-10-25 review.** `MenuVertical` holds `idx` to the list and moves the page down to it on entry: `GameVars.vertTop` is every list's, and an `idx` below the page drew the highlight under the box (`Magic.SpellPage` did this by hand). No caller was seen to reach it. `HorizontalMenu` loses `tick30` and `tick80`, set on every call and never read. #975.

**CharGen (2026-10-25 review).** Modify Character's Wisdom row set the first-level cleric slots to 1 and added the Wisdom bonus again, which was right only at cleric level 1. In the Companion Tools' editor a higher-level cleric lost first-level slots and gained second-level ones with each key. `ClassCal.ResetClericSlots` works the slots out from the level and the Wisdom, and Exit puts the thief skills back for the Dexterity it restores. Game Settings and free training no longer mark the party unsaved in `MainMenu`. `SetHpMax` (the new hpMax held at `HpMinimum` and 1, damage kept) serves `ModifyCharacter` and CharEdit's `LowerLevel`. `CreateCharacterMenu` divides the first hit points with `ClassCal.DivideAmongClasses`. `CustomizeIcon` asks `Party.IsPartyMember`. The evil alignments are `C_ALIGN.EVIL_ALIGNMENTS` (EffHndlr's Protection from Evil too), and the ability labels are `GameDefs.abilityNames` (ChgClass and ViewChar too). Literals are named: `Types.RACE_CLASSES_LAST`, `C_LEVEL.LEVEL_LAST` and `MAX_PCS`. A multiclass starts at the oldest of its classes' ages (`ClassCal.StartingAge`, which takes the table as `ageDice`), where SSI's fixed map took the fighter's for a fighter/thief (POR-0221, #945); the human magic-user's age dice are the DMG's 24 + 2d8, where SSI had 2d4. #946.

**CharEdit (2026-10-25 review).** Lowering a level in the Levels tool brought the experience to one short of the next level's threshold, and the table's -1 for a level PoR does not reach made that -2. A next level without a threshold now leaves the experience alone. `LineOf` replaces three walks of a menu list. The scroll types are `C_ITEM.MAGE_SCROLL` and `CLERIC_SCROLL`, the template count is `RandItem.TEMPLATE_LAST`, and `SpellList`'s array is `C_SPELL.LAST` long. #947.

**ChgClass (2026-10-25 review).** `Obstacle` names what stands in the way of a new class (the rules, the hall, the fee). `Qualifies` and `ShowClassStatus` both ask it, where each wrote the three tests out. #948.

**Party (2026-10-25 review).** `NextCreature(nil)` gives the head, as `PrevCreature(nil)` gives the last, where it dereferenced nil. TParty checks both. PartyMnu and CharExt had no findings. #949.

**CharHlp: the 2026-10-25 review.** `RecalcStats` takes the weight from `RawWeight` and the Bag of Holding from `HasBagOfHolding`, the routines Money already uses to mirror it, and keeps the equipped weight in a LongInt local where a unit-level Word held it. `EnemyTeam` is `1 - Team`, so a damaged save's hostile byte above 1 gets the enemy of the team `CountTeams` counts it on (#710, #827). `SpellDuration` names its spells (`C_SPELL.CAUSE_DISEASE` and the item spells), `WeaponAdjust` looks its base item up once, `RemoveSpell` stops at its match, and `HasRangedWeapon`, `HasThrowableWeapon` and `ItemReadable` assign their conditions. `CalcSpellRange`'s comment says an item-cast special spell's range uses `ITEM_CASTER_LEVEL` while the rest of it uses `SPECIAL_CASTER_LEVEL`. A dying creature hit again in combat goes further below 0: `InflictDamage` adds the damage to the overkill it has bled to, where SSI's worked it out from the new hit alone (hit points are already 0), so a creature at 8 below hit for 2 went back to 2 below; it dies once the total passes nine, and a hit of 0 leaves it as it was (POR-0222, #954). A half-orc now has the PHB's level caps (pp. 14-15), which SSI left out: `AtLevelCap` asks `HalfOrcCap`, a cleric's 4 to Wisdom 14 and one more a point to 7 at 17, a thief's 8 to Dexterity 14 and one more a point to 11 at 17; its fighter caps (10 and up) are past PoR's fighter levels and are left out, and PoR's experience table still ends every class where it did. TCharHlp checks `EnemyTeam`, `RemoveSpell`, the dying hits and the half-orc caps. #956.

**ClassCal: the 2026-10-25 review.** `HpMinimum` and `HpMaximum` added the Constitution bonus once a class rather than on each hit die, right at level 1 only; the Companion Tools' editor clamps to them, so a level 5 fighter of Constitution 17 was held to 53 hit points instead of 65. `DiceConBonus` sums `ConHpPerDie` over every die (`HitDice`), and `ConHpTotal` uses it too; `DivideAmongClasses` takes Integers, counts no classes as one and stops at 255. `ConHpBonus` and `RollFirstLevelHp` share `RollsHitDice` and `DiceAtLevel`, so a first-level monk's bonus counts both its dice as its roll does (no monk is made at creation). `FirstLevelSlots` and `KnowClericSpells` replace the copies in `StartCasterClass` and `RecalcBaseSkills`, `ExperienceShare` asks `PrimesAtLeast`, `TurnLevel` reads the paladin level once, and the loops use `Types.CASTER_LEVEL_LAST`, the `C_SAVE` columns, `SizeOf` and `C_SPELL` names. A drain leaves a caster only what its new level gives: `RecalcBaseSkills` sets slots only above level 1, so a cleric or magic-user drained from 2 to 1 kept level 2's slots, and memorized spells past the new slots stayed. `EffHndlr.DrainLevels` now calls `FitSpellsToLevel` after `RecalcBaseSkills`, which gives a class at level 1 its level-1 slots and clears memorized or pending spells past the slots of their class and spell level, the last first; THAC0, attacks, saves, thief skills, turning and the paladin's and ranger's powers already follow the levels (POR-0223, #955). TClasCal checks the limits above level 1, the monk, the first-level slots and the drain. #957.
