**CbtMap: the native build's first fight (2026-10-25 review).** `ScrollView` read `creatureArr[i]^.enabled` before the slot's size, and the slot at `arenaCount`, one past the last creature placed, is nil in a session's first fight; under DOS the read landed in the interrupt table, but the native build died with an access violation as `CombatStart` drew the combat screen. It tests size and nil first, `CombatSlot` gives 0 for nil, and `CalcGridDeltas`, `BuildArenaArrays` and `ScrollView` loop to `Pathing.LastSlot`, so a full arena no longer writes `gridX[73]`. `BuildArenaArrays` calls `CalcGridDeltas`, `ReportCaught` and `CalcDistance` use `Pathing.SaveTargets`/`RestoreTargets`, and the arena's size and the corpse table's are `Types.ARENA_LAST_X`/`ARENA_LAST_Y` and `DOWNED_SLOTS`. SSI's code. POR-0214, #925, #928.

**CombatAi (2026-10-25 review).** `AiMoveAttack`'s runaway-turn guard set the creature Guarding and went on with the pass, which could guard it again or let it strike; the pass now ends there (POR-0215, #926). `EquipWeaponAndShield` never picks a cursed shield, as `CalcWeaponPriority` scores a cursed weapon 0; `CheckMorale` drops a second removal of bite-hold and blood drain, `SelectItemToUse` an unread `slot`; `SelectSpellToCast`'s list is a `Types.TSpellSlots` and `RouteToEnemy` scans to `Pathing.LastSlot`. #927.

**Pathing (2026-10-25 review).** `OnArena` replaces five copies of the arena bounds test, `LastSlot` the capped scan limit, and `SaveTargets`/`RestoreTargets` copy the target list and its count. `WalkLine` drops SSI's second line, which ran from the start cell's clearance to the same clearance and so never changed: the start cell's clearance is compared directly. Behaviour is kept; TPathing checks the helpers. #929.

**PostCbt (2026-10-25 review).** A won duel ran `DetermineLootExp` and `AwardExperience` once for each enabled, Okay, non-hostile creature, so a charmed monster on the duelist's side would have doubled the treasure and the experience; they run once. `LootMenu`'s Detect Magic ids are `C_SPELL.DETECT_MAGIC_CLERIC`/`DETECT_MAGIC_MU`. #930.

**Combat (2026-10-25 code review).** `MoveMenu` takes its cursor keys from `CbtActs.DirOfKey` and its step from the new `CbtActs.StepCreature` (the free attacks, the step and the cloud check), which `CombatAi.AiMoveStep` shares; `CreatureAction` clears the flanking through `CbtActs.ResetFlanking`; `CombatMenu` keeps the extended-key flag in its own variable instead of `hasSpell`. `suppressContinuePrompt`, which nothing ever set (nor in SSI's code), goes with `Combat.ResetGame`. `CombatMain` counts the teams again after `CombatStart`, whose placing can take out a monster it finds no cell for, so a side that could not be placed ends the battle at once instead of after a round. #934. e04349f.

**A mover the free attacks leave helpless stays put.** `Combat.MoveMenu` tested only for a downed mover before the step and for helplessness after it, and `CombatAi.AiMoveStep` not at all, so a creature that the free attacks of the enemies it left paralysed, held or put to sleep finished its step; under the AI, with its initiative untouched, it could then still attack. `CbtActs.StepCreature`, which both now share, ends the mover's actions without the step when it is down or helpless after the free attacks, and ends them when it is helpless after the step (a guarding enemy's blow, the cloud). SSI's code. POR-0216, #933.

**CombatSt (2026-10-25 code review).** `TerrainClass(ch, yarashDone)` replaces `TerrainClassOf`, which ran its 60-arm case for every test, once per tree and per scrub cell among them; `BuildWildernessArena` computes it once. `FillCornerNW` and `FillCornerNE` lose a for/case loop that only ran four steps in order; `PlaceAllCreatures` drops two counters nothing read; `CombatStart` no longer repeats `CombatMain`'s `ifcState`. No behaviour changes. #935. db9900d.

**CbtActs (2026-10-25 code review).** `CalcWeaponDamage` multiplied a backstab in a Byte, so a product past 255 wrapped; `WeaponDamage` works it in a LongInt and holds it to 255. `CombatAttack` takes the shots from a stack through `AmmoLeft`, which stops at 0. `SweepAttempt` stops when the sweeper goes down. `CombatCastTargetMenu` gives a one-creature spell to the creature picked rather than to `combatAddr^.other`, which the AI's pick of a non-offensive spell's target leaves on its enemy. `CombatAimMenu` starts `menuStep` at 0. Shared now: `AttackWithReadied` (the attack with the readied weapon's ammunition, for the aim menu and `CombatAi.AiMoveAttack`), `ShowRange` and `WeaponCanStrike` (the aim menu and the cursor aim); `AimTargetMenu` loses an unused parameter. TCbtActs checks `DirOfKey`, `WeaponDamage`, `AmmoLeft` and `CombatSt.TerrainClass`. None of the guards is reached with the shipped data. #936. 72aab24.

**Magic: the 2026-10-25 review.** `ResolveSpell` clears `GameVars.elementMask` before it returns for a spell with no targets (a Fireball aimed at empty squares); SSI's set the element first and left it for the next non-weapon damage that sets none of its own. It tests the spell's own `rangeFixed` against `C_SPELL.ATTACK_ROLL_RANGE`. `CastSpellNC` and `CbtActs.DirTowardTarget` share `Pathing.DirToward`, which tries the eight directions and gives `C_DIR.CENTER` for a cell off the arena where both loops would have spun for ever; `CastSpellNC`'s `ai` parameter, which only decides the announcement, is `announce`, and the combat-only item message shares `ShowCombatOnly` with `ShowCombatOnlySpell`. `AddScrollSpells` skips a slot whose spell is past the spell table (`CharHlp.ScrollSpellAt`), so a damaged scroll cannot index `spellTable`, `spellLevelNames` or `spellNames` past their ends. `UseItemSpell` tests `quickfight <> 0` instead of casting a Byte to Boolean (the #860 pattern). Literals named: `C_SPELL.SAVE_HALF`, `C_LEVEL.LEVEL_LAST`, `C_SPELL.DRAGON_BOLT_CELLS`, `Types.SPELL_SLOT_LAST`. Left for Brian: Friends cast twice keeps a Charisma gain (POR-0212, #920), a large creature rolls a Stinking Cloud save for each cell it fills (POR-0213, #921), and the spell's and the temple's cures of disease remove different effects. #922.

**Bless and Curse take one side each.** `Magic.CastOneSide` (Bless, Curse) and `HasteSlowLogic` (Haste, Slow) keep a target when `CharHlp.Team` gives the caster's side (Bless, Haste) or the other one (Curse, Slow). SSI's compared the raw `hostile` byte with the caster's or with `CharHlp.EnemyTeam`'s, so a byte above 1, which only a damaged save holds, put a creature on neither side and turned such a caster's Curse and Slow on its own side. Bless still spares allies already in melee, as 1st edition has it. The spell page's Bless and Curse say foes and allies are unaffected. SSI's code. POR-0230, #1027.

**One cure of disease.** `FxEngine.RemoveDiseases` is the one rule for the Cure Disease spell, Keoghtom's Ointment, the temple and the paladin: Diseased goes; the Strength drain goes with its hit-point drain and helplessness; rot goes with mummy rot; a hit-point drain or mummy rot found alone goes too. Helplessness on its own stays, since a Stinking Cloud's nausea is the same effect, which the temple's and the paladin's cure used to lift. It is quiet and returns whether it cured anything; `Magic.CureDiseaseOf` says "is Cured" for the spell and the ointment, and `HasDisease` looks for `C_EFFECT.DISEASES`. Magic's `RemoveDiseaseEffects` and FxEngine's `cureDiseaseEffects` are gone. TFxRemov checks the rule. #922.

**Friends leaves no lasting Charisma.** `Magic.FriendsHandler` stored the Charisma before the cast and `EffFriends` put it back, so a second Friends, which stored the first one's raised score (or replaced it), left a lasting gain, and one cast on a creature immune to it raised Charisma with no effect to lower it. The effect now holds `C_EFFECT.FRIENDS_BONUS` plus the Charisma it gave, Charisma rises only once the effect is on, and `FxEngine.FriendsEndCha` takes back that much as it ends; an effect saved before keeps restoring its stored score. SSI's code. POR-0212, #920.

**A Stinking Cloud rolls once per creature.** `StinkingCloudHandler` considered every cell of the cloud, so a creature filling two or more of them could be checked again whenever the first check left no effect on it (an immunity to the nausea). It now considers each creature once. SSI's code. POR-0213, #921.

**SpellInf: the 2026-10-25 review.** `SaveResult` gives `C_SPELL.SAVE_NONE` for an id outside the spell table, where its fallback read `spellTable` at that very id, and `SaveText` leaves the save column out for one. `TextLine` takes its chunk size from `SpellTxt.CHUNK_LINES`. TSpellInf checks the out-of-range ids. #923.

**SpellTxt: the 2026-10-25 review.** Every description's line count and width was checked against the text, and every description against its handler. Curse's said it spares foes in melee (only Bless's handler does), and Sleep's that any level-5 creature costs 10 points (only a monster; any other costs more than the pool). Both now say what the spells do. #924.

**EffHndlr: the 2026-10-25 review.** `EffPetrifyGaze` runs as the gazer's AI turn starts, before it picks a target, and on its first turn `combatAddr^.other` is nil: the save and `InflictStatusMsg` went through nil, into the interrupt vectors under DOS, a crash natively. It now gazes only when `PetrifyGazeReaches` its target: in action (`enabled`), and `InSight` (a `Pathing.WalkLine` line, as `EffCharmGaze` now uses too) within `PETRIFY_GAZE_RANGE`, 3 squares: the Monster Manual's 3" for the medusa, and the stand-in for the basilisk, which has none listed; they are the only gazers in the monster data. SSI's gazed at the last target wherever it was and whatever its state, so a basilisk could stone a dying character across the map. TEffHndl checks the range, a diagonal, a wall, a downed target and none. POR-0217, #938. `EffHealWake`, which `FxEngine.RestoreHitPoints` calls with no node, retries at strength 0 rather than reading through nil. `Protection`, `EffDragonFear` and `EffMummyFear` compare teams (`CharHlp.Team`), not raw hostile bytes. Handlers written out more than once are one routine each: `ResistElement`, `ElementImmunity`, `SleepCharmResist`, `NamedIn` and `RacialFoeBonus` (the dwarf and gnome bonuses, bound by `SizeOf`), `TickDamage`, `HoldSlot` and `Immobilize`, `AnimateBetween`, `IsMirror`, `DrainTouch`, and Mirror Image's roll and loss in `FxEngine.ImageStruck` and `LoseImage`, which `CbtActs.DoAttack` uses too. `CharDisp.RedrawPartyOutOfCombat` replaces four copies of its test; the combat sprite cells move from CbtActs's private constants to `C_SPRITE`, and `CharHlp.UNDEAD_TYPE` is public. TEffHndl tests the consolidated handlers, TProtect the team sides. #942.

**FxEngine: the 2026-10-25 review.** `EffectSource` restored the caller's target list after its range test but not `targetCount`; it restores both. Its protection-aura side test compares teams, and it takes the aura range and the creature's slot once. `RemoveEffectsHp0` and `RemoveAiEffects` bound their loops by `SizeOf`, `TriggerEffectsWithCondition` no longer breaks calls mid-argument, and the doc comments of `ToggleReadyItem`, `AdvanceTime` and `NonWeaponDamage` are on the interface and say what the code does. #943.

**Stinking Cloud does nothing to a creature that saves or resists.** `FxEngine.ConsiderStinkingCloud` gave a creature that saved effect 30, coughing (no items or spells, and a worse AC), and called the cough's or the nausea's handler whether or not the effect was added, so a creature whose magic resistance refused it ("is Unaffected") still coughed or lost its round. A save now leaves it "Unaffected" with no effect, and the nausea's handler runs only with the node the creature now has. SSI's code. POR-0218, #939.

**Magic resistance no longer stops acid, blood drain or the poison and disease ticks.** `FxEngine.EffectImmune` left the effect it checked in `GameVars.pendingEffectId`, and `EffHndlr.MagicResistance` resists any damage while that is set, so after any effect had been tried a magic-resistant creature could shrug off damage that is no magic. The check clears it as it returns; TEffHndl checks it. SSI's code. POR-0219, #940.

**A creature out of action lends no aura.** `FxEngine.EffectSource` took a protection aura (Protection from Evil and from Good 10', a paladin's) or a Prayer from any creature carrying it, a fallen paladin or a dead cleric too. Those are the carrier's own and now lapse while it is out of action (`enabled` 0); Silence 15' Radius is on its spot and stays. TProtect checks it. SSI's code. POR-0220, #941.

**Poison, disease and blood drain name themselves.** Damage of no element read "takes 3 points of damage from Magic". `C_EFFECT` has three more `elementMask` bits, `ELEM_POISON`, `ELEM_DISEASE` and `ELEM_BLOOD`, which Slow Poison's and the disease's ticks (`EffHndlr.TickDamage`) and `EffBloodDrain` set, and `FxEngine.NonWeaponDamage` prints "from poison", "from disease" and "from blood drain". No handler tests for 0, and magic resistance passes them over as before. #943.

**Money and MoneyCal: the 2026-10-25 review.** `TakeMoneyMenu` and `ViewChar.PickCoinType` built their coin-type menus by hand; `Money.CoinMenu` builds one for a creature's coins or the treasure pool's, highest first with the names padded to 8, so the pool's menu now lists jewelry first, as a character's does. `PoolHasMoney` is the money test `DetermineTreasure` and `TakeMoneyMenu` both made. `InputNumberMax` returns 0 for Return with nothing typed rather than whatever a failed `Val` leaves. `ShareMoney` loses an unread flag and a redundant branch, `PoolMoney` a copy of its loop variable and `AppraiseMenu` a `Trunc` of an integer; `TradeMoney` takes its creatures by value. `MoneyCal.ParseMoneyType` stops its leading-space loop at the string's end and returns -1 for a blank name or an unknown letter, where its result was undefined; `ItemBundle` names the ammunition kinds. #1001, #1002.

**Shop, Vault and VaultMnu: the 2026-10-25 review.** `CharHlp.LabelItems` is the walk that labels every item of a list, written out twice in Shop and once each in `PostCbt.LootTakeItemSelection` and EclVm's TREASURE. `Vault.HasRoom` counts with `MenuCore.ListCount`, now exported, instead of a copy of it. `VaultMenu` passes every extended key to `Party.SelectPrevNextChar`, as `Shop.CounterMenu` does, so the arrows step the party at the vault's counter too. #1003, #1005, #1006.

**RandItem, Sage, ClueBook, CompTool and Ending: the 2026-10-25 review.** `GenerateRandomItem` names the item kinds C_ITEM has names for. `Sage.FindNamed`'s archive parameter is `archive`, as its comment now says. ClueBook's unit comment is rewrapped. CompTool's `NoteColour` gives the note colour to `ShowSymbol`, the legend and the note under the map, so in CGA, where notes are light magenta, the legend no longer says yellow. `Ending.YarashComplete` remaps the pyramid through one local routine, dithered or whole, with its colour tables built at run time (48 bytes of typed constants gone) and Byte viewport tables; `WinGame`'s unit variables are locals. #1007, #1008, #1009, #1010, #1012.

**Temple, RandItem and Intro: the 2026-10-25 review's calls.** `Temple.CureWounds` took the fee and said "is cured." whatever `FxEngine.RestoreHitPoints` did; `NoWoundCure` now says why a cure would not heal (dead, stoned, out of reach, mummy rot, not wounded) and the temple asks "cast cure anyway" first, as its other cures do, and reports "is cured." only after a cure that healed (POR-0226, #1000). `GenerateRandomItem` rolls 1d10 for potion kind 71, where SSI's 1d11 made a nameless item on an 11 (POR-0225, #999). `Intro.Introduction` flashes title picture 2's lightning with a chance of one in `FLASH_TICKS` (290) each timer tick: SSI paced it by loop passes, which under DOSBox's default 3,000 cycles (about 2,090 passes a second, measured) is a flash about every 16 seconds, and which the native build never reached (#1011). The temple's Stone to Flesh rolls system shock, as AD&D 1st edition has the spell's recipient do: `AbilAdj.ConSystemShock` is the PHB's Constitution table, run to 25 with Deities & Demigods' values (99 at 19..24, 100 at 25), and `SurvivesSystemShock` the roll's test; a failure leaves the character dead, "DOESN'T SURVIVE THE TRANSFORMATION.", the fee spent (#1004).

**Exploring, camp and time (the 2026-10-25 code review).** AreaName, Explored and MapNotes had nothing new (their open items are #917 to #919).

**Explore.** `MapMainMenu` is one loop for 3D and the wilderness (local `Turn` and `Arrow`), and `SideScreen` runs F1, F2 and F6 for it and for camp, in place of three copies. Cast in 3D no longer ignores a character who is not Okay: `Camp.NoncombatCastMenu` says why the character cannot cast, as in the wilderness. `ResolveMovement`'s two door arms are `DoorMenu(barred)`. Knock is offered at a locked or barred door only when `FindKnockCaster` finds an Okay member with it memorized or on a scroll it can read and use, and `PartyCastsKnock` then uses that copy; v1.3 offered it at a barred door to any party with a magic-user, though only a memorized Knock worked, so the door was never walled off (POR-0229, #1015). `MoveForward` and `MovePartyWilderness` use `Types.MAP_LAST` and C_AREA's new wilderness bounds; `PickLock` tests status before rolling. #1017.

**View3D.** `WrapCoords` masks with `MAP_LAST`, so the view's far rows, two to five squares past an edge, read the right wrapped square instead of the edge one (POR-0227, #1013). `MapSquare` holds the off-map guard and wrap the three map readers repeated; `Display3DMapView`'s facing steps are ShortInts, not Words; the band color comments match `DrawBackdrop`. Tested by TView3D. #1018.

**MapView and Wilds.** `Wilds.ViewCorner` moves the 5x5 view's corner inside the map for `BuildWildernessMap` and `MapView`, which clamped it a second time and found the party icon's cell from a ladder of literals; the icon's cell is the party's less the corner. `CeilColor`, `FloorColor` and `WallColor` lose their dead arms, `skyCeilingColors` has its eight colors once, and `WILD_LAST_SITE` names the special-location table's end. #1019, #1020.

**MapTiles.** `C_TILE.ICON_LAST` names the last icon-animation slot (GameVars declares the table by it); `LoadIconAnim` loads and remaps both frames in one loop; `LoadWildIcons` loads the party's wilderness icon for Explore and EclVm. `GameDefs.colorIdentity` and `colorPinkToBlack` replace five copies of two color tables in MapTiles, Ending and Portrait. #1021.

**GameTime.** `CheckScribeDone` passes `Magic.RemoveSpellFromScroll` the queued slot's byte, and that now clears the slot holding it exactly before the last copy, so on a scroll holding a spell twice the scribed copy is the one taken (POR-0228, #1014; tested by TScribe). `healTicks`' comment says camp clears it; `CampRest` loses an unread variable. #1022.

**Camp.** `PendingLevel` bounds a pending slot's id to the spells before `CampSpellPrepTime` reads `spellTable`; `HasSlotKind` bounds a class and level for `SpellSlotsRemaining` (which indexed `slots` unbounded) and `CampScribeMenu`, and `SlotFree` folds into `SpellSlotsRemaining > 0`; `SlotsUsed` and `CampDisplayMenu` use `C_SPELL.LAST`, `CampFixMenu` `Types.MAX_PARTY`. #1023.

**File writes, archive blocks, sound entries, strings and map notes (#914 to #918).** `FileUtil.PutInPlace` erased the old file before renaming the new one in, so a failed rename, or a game ended between the two, lost POOLOPT.CFG, POOL.CFG, BESTIARY.DAT, the roster or the session vault. It now commits a set of one file through `CommitSet` (old file to its backup, copy in, backup erased), and a read-only file still fails and is kept; `FileUtil.RecoverFile` undoes an ended write, and `CfgFile.OpenText`, BeastFil's reader, `SaveLoad`'s roster reader and `Vault.ReadVault` call it first. TFileUtl injects each failure. #914. `DaxIo.ReadBlock` is a function: an entry whose block does not start inside the archive (an offset below 0, or at or past the end, compared by subtraction), or a failed seek or read, gives False and nil, and LoadDax treats it as absent; a block cut short still reads, zeroed (#122). Tested by the new TDaxIo. #915. `SfxFile.GetEntry` refuses an ADPCM entry over 32,768 bytes, whose samples SndDig counted in a Word and wrote past its buffer; `SfxFile.DecodedLength` sizes it as a LongInt (the shared parser came with #1044). #916. `StrUtil.FitLength` holds a raw string's length byte to its field: `DiskRec.CharFromDisk` and `ItemFromDisk` cut names with it (so every save, vault and DAX reader does), `SaveLoad.LoadSlot` cuts the party names, and `Stats.ReadFile` refuses a file with a name past its field. #917. `MapNotes.Note`, `SetNote` and `ClearNote` refuse a square off the map (`Types.OnMap`), `SetNote` returning False without taking a slot, and `ReadSlot` drops off-map notes and notes whose text length is past `NOTE_LEN`. #918.

**Smaller guards (the review's cross-batch leftovers).** `CharHlp.IsSpell` is the one test for a named spell id (1..`C_SPELL.LAST`); GameTime's item scan and SpellInf's page had checked only the top, so an id of 0 read `spellNames[0]`, which does not exist. The enemy-team tests in CbtActs, CbtMap, CombatAi and `CharHlp.SleepMost` compare `CharHlp.Team` with `EnemyTeam` rather than a raw hostile byte, so a damaged save's byte above 1 counts on its team. `FileUtil.Exists` takes DaxIo's empty-name guard (#962), and DaxIo's `FileExists` copy is gone.

**A folder is no file natively (#1057).** On Linux a folder opens read-only like a file, so the native `FileUtil.Exists` took a folder for a file of its name (a save, a commit's backup). `FileUtil.OpenReadOnly` now refuses a folder natively (`SysUtils.DirectoryExists`) before it opens anything, as DOS's open refuses one, so `Exists`, `ReadRecord` and every reader agree on both builds. TFileUtl checks a folder natively too, and its backup-name-held-by-a-folder case, DOS-only before, now runs on both. #1057.

## 10. Refactor phases 0 to 6 (structure, units, tests, sizes)

Once the build stopped matching SSI's executable byte for byte, binary identity could no longer serve as the safety net. `docs/refactor-plan.md` replaces it with tests in phase 0, then removes duplication and untangles the structure in measured steps. Size figures come from the build report; phase 6 appears in section 8.

### Phase 0: a safety net

**Unit tests (TestKit) and a size report.** `tests/testkit.pas` (Start, Check, CheckEqualsL, CheckEqualsS, Done; failures and a summary to TEST.LOG, exit code 1 on failure); first tests `tests/tstats.pas` (31 checks) and `tests/tareas.pas` (13). `build.sh --test` builds the game, compiles each test program with the game's settings into `obj/tests` and runs it under the same Turbo Pascal in DOSBox-X, failing the build when a test does not compile, finish or pass. Every build reports resident code and data sizes from TPC.OUT and the overlays from FIXMAIN.EXE's stubs (`build/ovlsizes.py`). CI runs `./build.sh --test`; README gained a Tests section. ad2cadc.

**Deterministic saves.** Section 6. 8048f48.

**Behaviour scenarios and `tests/regress.sh`.** Seven scenarios (boot, chargen, loadsave, combat, vault, journal, camp) that goldbox-player's `tools/regress.py` replays against two builds, comparing screen text read from the game's font (the assertion), the frame (a warning) and save files at checkpoints; each boots `START STING NORAND` so dice fall the same way. `tests/regress.sh GOLDEN [CANDIDATE] [SCENARIO...]`; golden builds go in `.golden/<commit>/`; `tests/scenarios/README.md` describes the format. a15842b. Two more scenarios, shop (buying and selling at the arms shop) and areachange (New Phlan to the training hall via NEWECL 11). a19b0ce.

**Tests: logs and boot scenario.** Each test's compiler output goes to `build/logs/tests/<name>.OUT` (8.3 names had made two tests share one log and hidden compile errors), and a test name longer than eight characters is refused; the boot scenario ignores free memory. b88a6d7.

**Tests for ClassCal and DataPat.** TClasCal checks `RecalcBaseSkills`, `AwardWisdomBonus`, `RecalcSaves` and `RecalcThiefSkills`; TDataPat checks the patch table's rules. 41160a7.

**AbilAdj.** The Strength and Dexterity tables (`TranslatedStrength`, `StrToHitAdjust`, `StrDamageAdjust`, `WeightAllowance`, `DexMissileAdjust`, `DexterityArmorAdjustment`) move from CharHlp to a new resident unit using only Types; TAbilAdj tests them. 9bda93c.

**MoneyCal.** Money's arithmetic (`CopperValue`, `CoinValueInGold`, `CoinsOf`, `CreatureGold`, `MakeChange`, `WhoPays`, `ItemBundle`, `ItemValue`, `ItemSalePrice`, `ParseMoneyType`) moves to a new overlaid unit; `MoneyCal.CreatureCopper` replaces `Stats.CoinWorth`. TMoneyCa tests it. Phase 0's unit tests marked done. 124bca6.

**SSI's file formats pinned.** TRecLay checks the sizes of `TCharRecord` (285), `TItemRec` (63) and `TEffectRec` (9) and the offset of all 113 fields, numbers read from the same program built against SSI's v1.3 source; TSaveLay checks SAVGAMx.DAT's parts and 13,137-byte total and zone 0's fields at their GBVM addresses. d7a0f6a. The chargen scenario records the .CHA files; a new `ssiformat` scenario runs with SSI's own v1.3 as the golden build and matches SSI's save files byte for byte once heap pointers, stack leftovers in party names and the (deliberately reloaded) area script are masked. efa574b.

### Phase 1: hygiene

**Every unit lists the units it calls.** 127 qualified calls named a unit not in the caller's uses clause (compiling only because Turbo Pascal accepts `Unit.Name` for any loaded unit); 27 units now list them. `tests/regress.sh` keeps the golden build's results in `GOLDEN/record.json`. e59d379.

**NPC tests.** The npc byte's bit 7 was tested 22 ways; now `CharHlp.IsNpc`, `MayTrade` and `ComesUnderPlayer`. ee892e2.

**`GDVars.stingArg`.** Eight tests of `ParamStr(1) = 'STING'` read one flag GameBoot sets. b271b08.

**`TextRend.ClearBottomLine`.** Resident, replacing MenuCore's overlaid one (which could load an overlay mid-combat) and twelve inline copies. f61f070.

**Named constants.** `NAME_LEN`, `MAX_PARTY`, `LAST_DISK`, `MAP_LAST`, `SPELL_SLOT_LAST`; `View3D.WrapCoords` for the five copies of the map-edge wrap. 9f12bb3.

**Unused locals removed.** 69 locals kept only to match SSI's stack frames (a29b1b8), and later 40 more of any name (af5776e).

**Unused globals removed.** Fifteen GDVars variables nothing used; GameInit clears the position bytes by name; data segment 694 bytes smaller. 29029f3, ace1784.

### Phase 2: shared helpers

**StrUtil.** One `NumStr`, `ZeroPad2` and `PadLeft`/`PadRight` replace six Str wrappers and seven zero-pad copies; resident, using nothing. TStrUtil, 13 checks. 423e76b.

**FileUtil.** `DeleteFile`, `OpenReadOnly`, `ReadRecord` and `WriteRecord` for the game's small files; `SaveLoad.CharFileBase` and `EraseCharFiles` (.cha, .itm, .spc, .mem, .sts). TFileUtl, 10 checks. Also fixes #635 (section 6). 55b640e.

**`CharHlp.LinkEquipped`.** Section 6 (#620). 9a3f33c.

**`RewriteSaveFile` and `SaveDiskMsg`.** Section 6. 35f21e7.

**`Shop.CounterMenu`, `Money.GoBackForIt`, interfaces name only the units they use.** The shop's and temple's counter menus are one routine (Buy is now refused for an extended key, as Heal was); the go-back prompt is one routine (fixes POR-0157, section 3); every unit's interface uses only the units its declarations name (42 units), breaking an interface-uses cycle. 4d84fd9.

**`TextRend.DisplayCentered`.** Ten hand-centred texts. e8df39a.

**`TextRend.ShowMessage` and `ViewChar.SayBelow`.** Fourteen message-window calls and six yellow panel lines; also POR-0158 (section 3). 563481a.

**Money helpers.** `Money.CreatureGold`, `WhoPays`, `PayBy` and `PayGold` replace three pay-here-else-the-pool blocks and a second coin valuation. be0ea4b.

**`CharHlp.FreeItemList` and `ClearTreasure`.** Four item-list freeing loops. af5776e.

**`FxEngine.HasDisease` and `RemoveDiseases`.** The temple's and the paladin's cures; the Cure Disease spell is unchanged. 05c00d9.

**`Party.IsPartyMember`.** Replaces `SaveLoad.InParty`, which in combat counted monsters as party members, and Stats' copy; the usual-spells owner test is the one that changes. #636. d80ea3f.

**Plan updated.** 13888a9.

### Phase 3: structure

**One typed view per shared global.** `GDVars.videoMode` as a `TVideoMode` (f7b088d); `treasureMoney` as a `TMoney` (8cace21); `dsIconAnimation` holding `PImgBuf`s, `TIconPair` gone (32c43ff); one wall-set cache slot type (8687fe1); one animation record, `TMenuAnim` and `TMFrame` gone (4c61f6f); the zones through GDVars' typed pointers, the `Session` unit and nine zone types gone, with `C_Gbvm.MONSTER_HIT_MOD` and `PARTY_HIT_MOD` (35ca447); `gameDir`, the insert-disk and intro flags as Booleans, the menu auto-answer as a `TMenuAuto` (78 bytes smaller), the effect table typed (b8cdce0); the random-item templates read by template and field (a7b29ad); one typed view of the wall graphics, `PWallSlices` (6fe34dd); the spell table split into `castTargetMenu` and `spellFns`, assigned without @ so the compiler checks signatures, and Magic's sounds through `C_Sound` names (62ec8f6). The only absolutes left in src/ are hardware addresses. Plan items marked done: 0f11ab8.

**SSI quirks.** Section 3. f0178a8, b808e70, 74825ad, d9dbbc1; plan 2ab71e9.

**EclVars.** The ECL interpreter's variable and game-memory layer (`LoadEclVars`, `GetEclVar`, `ReadGbvm`, `WriteGbvm`, `WriteGbvmString`, `CombineBytesToWord`, `SkipEclCmd`) moves out of EclMenu into its own overlaid unit; the dispatcher stays in EclVm. 6531a44; plan c03cc49.

**CharDisp.** CharHlp's 20 drawing routines move to a new overlaid unit; resident code 5,968 bytes smaller (73,616 to 67,648). 3ea574d; plan 34581d4.

### Phase 4: hidden parameters

Globals used as hidden parameters become real ones: `tempCr1` and `tempCr2` leave GDVars (`DisplayChar` takes its character, ViewChar keeps a private `tradeTarget`, and `PrintItemString` loses an unused creature parameter in 18 calls) (2e8e105); `castScroll` becomes a var result of `SpellSelection` (06ceaed); `Magic.RemoveDiseaseEffects` takes its target (e321d08); the money routines take the character who pays or is paid (d69e904); the spell-list builders take their character (d1b1cda). The plan records what stays global and why (`usingItem`, the target list). c73a1be.

### Phase 5: working sets, cycles, duplication, testability

Planning: proposed from three surveys (39ac97d), what the ECL can see through EclVars' address map (98f7cb8), approved with GBVM addresses to be named (4084078), outcome and declined items (1826ed1), where the last four combat overlay loads come from (d307eb5), issue numbers for the behaviour items (6dd9ce1).

**Step 0.** The combat, vault and areachange scenarios record the overlay load count (Ctrl-Y) as an ignored checkpoint; `tests/ovlloads.py` prints both builds' counts. aed1509.

**Step 1: overlay working sets.** Code that ordinary play calls is moved so it does not pull in a large overlay:
- the spell and effect handler tables are filled by GameInit (a combat's working set 3,168 bytes smaller; `build/ovlsizes.py --units`) (69e8668);
- `SelectPrevNextChar` moves to the resident Party unit, so stepping through the party no longer loads ViewChar (e4d1594);
- ViewChar leaves the combat turns: `ToggleReadyItem` to FxEngine, `UseItem`, `UseItemSpell` and the spell menus to Magic (15be6f9);
- Magic's item and scroll predicates (`ItemReadable`, `ScrollLegible`, `ScrollSpellAt`, `CastableScroll`, `HasScrollSpells`, `CalcSpellRange`) move to resident CharHlp (a34665b);
- the clock (`AdvanceTime`, `ValidateClock`, `TimeOutEffects`) moves to FxEngine (a462a53);
- monster loading moves from SaveLoad to EclMenu (01c67c5);
- random items get their own overlay, `RandItem` (42be4f3);
- JrnlList, compiled `{$O+}` but never listed as an overlay, is overlaid (resident code 1,792 bytes smaller) (f04e389).

**Step 2: no unit cycles.** `GetIconTileDelta` moves to Pathing (4fad886); CbtMap traces the Lightning Bolt preview through a hook (5d328ce); Ctrl-Y's memory display moves behind a hook, first into Debug (4f7f88a), then, because loading Debug changed the count it shows, into a new resident unit `MemShow` (ca400a0); `RemoveCreature` and `FreeCreature` move to SaveLoad (d8f58b6); `CheckSaveDir` and `SaveDiskMsg` move to SaveMenu (bb6178a); the vault's menu gets its own unit, `VaultMnu` (b6ea833); Guard's message moves to its callers (7f1da48); the counter menu takes its Buy or Heal command as a `TMenuAction` (92ff703).

**Step 3: duplication.** `CharHlp.PrependItemCopy` and `PrependEffectCopy` (dc63384); `AppendItemCopy` for every tail append (d305345); `Border.ClearBox` through `TextRend.ClearRect` (5dd8554); ViewChar's Trade and Drop share `PickCoinType` (134b38c); the disk number through `NumStr` (c4deab8); `AbilAdj.ConSaveBonus` for the two Constitution save handlers (2cb4430); DataArc and `QuickLoadable` open through `FileUtil.OpenReadOnly` (3004456); CbtMap's `DrawCellCreature` (a94779c); `Party.LastCreature`, `PrevCreature` and `NextCreature` (d588be9); CharGen's `PickFromList` (c1aaf22); `Explore.OpenBarrierAhead` for bash, pick and Knock (137cbfe); Camp's `SlotsUsed` and `PutPending`, and Knock through `RemoveSpell` (45b2502); EclVars' GBVM address map by name in C_Gbvm (a6e26f6), one arm for the three wall-set loads (c08e23b); `Types.ARENA_SLOTS` for 72 (bd84a2c); class-level loops and party limits by name (40f1fb1); spell ids by name (b706c18); damage elements by name, `C_Effect.ELEM_*` (3630f36); the full text window's corners by name (fc42b34); `Pathing.TargetArea` and `CbtActs.AreaHitsNonEnemy` (b1b813f); `FxEngine.HoldTarget` (35ab983); `Temple.CureWounds` confirms and heals once (8ecca3a); `TextRend.TickCount` (7ab8d31); CharGen's `DivideAmongClasses` (74d7afc); `CharDisp.OpenMessageWindow` and the combat panel by name (e649344); EclVm's encounter menu's shared arms (94cc86f); `NumStr` where a Str result was used once (PrintHpDebug loses 768 bytes of stack frame) (0ac791f); the journal text units' ranges by name (54ba386); item name parts by name, `C_Item.NAME_*` (b22b64c); `MenuCore.AppendMenuLine` (b9dca03); View3D's 3D view from three parameterised scans (f19a30b); one tile blitter, `BlitTile`, with a clip argument (resident code 1,088 bytes smaller) (d056878); `FlushView` copies the four EGA planes in one loop (dae4f06); CharGen's racial ability limits through `ClassCal.RaceAbilityMin`/`RaceAbilityMax` (2957bbc).

**Step 4: testable units.** The dice move to a resident unit, `Dice` (145 calls in 17 units; the same Random calls in the same order, so NORAND runs roll the same) (#642, 3625a6d); tests for RandItem (0b55295); `CountTargets` and `CalcDistance` move to CbtMap (a934c45); CharHlp no longer draws (`ItemLabel`, and a fault hook) (f9755ec); tests for CharHlp, and its hooks start unset (e77b594); tests for Pathing (24c629e), JrnlList (bc167cb), DataArc's names and `EclTrace.OpName` (db9ad29); the hit-point rules move to ClassCal (6b25f7e); `HpMaximum` uses its parameter's Constitution rather than `curChar`'s (every caller passed `curChar`, so nothing changed), with hit-point tests (7548bfd); EclVars reaches DaxIo and View3D through hooks (a0f553b); tests for EclVars' GBVM address map (a8682d2); the experience share moves to `ClassCal.ExperienceShare` (8b113bb); the clock's carries in a resident unit, `Clock`, with tests (db4930a); the usual-spells store moves to its own unit, `UsualSpl`, with tests (1844bbc).

**Step 5: a leaner GDVars.** Globals nothing reads and three unused constants removed (b1faa82); the script registers become explicit arms of the GBVM map, reading 0 and dropping writes (5b68996); scratch globals become locals, and CombatSt's placement tables private (d75265d); 50 globals used by one unit become private to it (e63f110); `baseItems` becomes one array of 129, its last entry the overflow (d8beeb6); the saved map position as five reads and writes (10f9b84); Magic's and FxEngine's interfaces use only Types (7591936); 24 unneeded forward declarations deleted (dff02d4).

**Step 6: the data segment and the stack.** The script strings move to the heap (data 5,116 bytes smaller) (d2a9b25); the base item table moves to the heap (2,060 smaller) (6c9966a); `spellNames`, `spellLevelNames` and `templeSpells` get string types their entries fit (928 smaller) (0c8d423); smaller stack frames on the combat paths, and a level drain no longer loads Debug when the display is off (5416c34).

### After phase 5

**C_Level.** In the owner's batch, the class-level slot indexes move from `C_Class.LEVEL_*` to a new include unit `C_Level` (CLERIC..MONK), and GDConsts' rule tables (thief skills, Dexterity adjustments, classes by race, class parts, racial ability adjustments, class minimums, racial maximum levels, age categories, alignments by class, saving throws) become typed records readable by index or by field name (`Types.TRule*`); `C_Align` gains short alignment names. 038f32e.

**CbtActs names its item types.** C_Item gains `HAND_AXE`, `CLUB`, `DAGGER`, `DART`, `JAVELIN`, `QUARREL`, `SPEAR`, `BROAD_SWORD`, `LONG_SWORD`, `SHORT_SWORD`, `SLING`, `LEATHER_ARMOR` and `ARROW`, used by CbtActs, CharHlp and FxEngine. #649. 334d3bb.

**CharHlp names its constants.** The bows, the scrolls' base slots, the spell casting classes and fixed caster levels (C_SPELL), the attack-roll range, the parts of armour class (`Types.AC_*`), `C_Level.LEVEL_LAST` and the helpless effects get names; CharHlp's movement, encumbrance, Bag of Holding and theft rules become private constants; `AtLevelCap` reads a table (section 2). f8bcdf0.

---

**Code issues from the whole-unit cleanup.** The cleanup (PR #805) listed 100 code-level issues it could not change; the bugs among them are above (#806..#822). The rest, behaviour unchanged: dead stores, unused locals and parameters (`hasMask` through every picture driver, MenuCore's never-set auto-answer and `LastLabelBefore`, the sound driver's forced-shutdown path, `noteLen` and a duplicate Tandy tune setup, whose port-write digests TSndSeq shows unchanged); bare numbers named (`C_SPELL.TABLE_LAST`, `ITEM_EFFECT_SHIFT`, `ITEM_JAVELIN_LIGHTNING`, `C_Effect.NULL_DAMAGE`/`NULL_TARGET_VETO`, `C_Item.RANDOM_ARMS_KINDS`, `MAGE_SCROLL`, `CLERIC_SCROLL`, `CbtMap.AREA_CELLS`), with `GDConsts.spellTable` and `GDVars.spellFns` sized by `TABLE_LAST`; misleading names (`ApproachOrParley`, `goldDice`, `nonSharerCount`); duplicated logic shared (`CharHlp.FindAmmo` for the AI's ammunition lookup, one starting-age lookup for multi-classes, `DrainLevels` picking its class once per level, with its `levels = 2` branch kept as SSI's and marked); `C_Gbvm`'s duplicate THAC0-modifier names removed. Turbo Pascal 6 builds with extended syntax off, so a function's unused result is still stored (or tested with `if ... then ;`).

**One helper picks a noun for a count.** `StrUtil.Pluralize(n, singular, plural)` gives the noun alone and `StrUtil.Count` the number and the noun ("1 SQUARE", "3 SQUARES"). They replace `SpellInf`'s private `Count` and the singular-or-plural branches in `BeastMnu` (creatures, times seen), `FxEngine`'s and `CbtActs.ProcessAttackResult`'s damage messages (point, points) and `CharHlp.CatchText` (enemy, ally). The drain message keeps its own "a level". TStrUtil checks both. Resident code +224 bytes, overlays -302.

**The review's cross-batch leftovers.** The 2026-10-25 review held these back so its batches would not collide. One constant, `C_MSG.CONTINUE_PROMPT`, for the Enter prompt (BldShow, EclVm, EclMenu, PostCbt twice). `MapTiles.Load24x24Set` and `Copy2DTile` drop SSI's unused last parameters. `FxEngine.EffectSource` keeps the target list with `Pathing.SaveTargets`/`RestoreTargets`. `MenuCore.ListIndex`, `FindListMember`'s inverse, replaces CharEdit's `LineOf` and ViewChar.ItemInfo's walk (#973). `CharDisp.AnimateIconBetween` and `AnimateBetween` fly an icon or a sprite between two creatures, for EffHndlr's gazes, `Magic.HoldPersonHandler` and `CbtActs.RangedAttackAnim`; Magic names its sprites with `C_SPRITE`. `Portrait.BODY_ROW_OFFSET` names the body's place below the head, for `DisplayPortraitBufs` and CharGen's picker. CombatSt and CbtActs bound the arena with `Types.ARENA_LAST_X`/`_Y`. GameInit names effect slots `READY_FLAME_TONGUE` and `READY_UNDEAD_SLAYER`, and `GameVars.scriptEntryAddrs` runs to `C_ECL.ENTRY_COUNT`. `CharHlp.TrainClasses` gives the classes a character can train, for `CanTrain` and `CharGen.TrainingMenu`. `FxEngine.UnreadyItem` puts a readied item away, for `ToggleReadyItem` and `ChgClass.SyncClassPowers`. `VidOps.CanMerge` holds MergePic's checks, which `CharGen.Merge` had repeated to decide on the colour map. Sdl3Min drops `SDL_GetAudioStreamQueued`; about thirty uses entries naming constants units, GameDefs or MenuCore that a unit never read are gone; the last lines over 120 columns are wrapped.

**Map and party bounds (#919).** `Types.OnMap` is the one map-square test (0..`MAP_LAST`), for Explored, ClueBook, Explore, CombatSt's walls and View3D (whose private `Valid3DCoords` it replaces); the bit maps, `ClueBook.EntrySquare`'s limit and `Types.TGeoPlane` run to `MAP_LAST`, and `GameTime.prepTimers` to `MAX_PARTY`. The rest of #919 came with #972. #919.

## 11. Tools, CI, tests and regression scenarios

These entries cover the machinery around the game: reproducible builds, Forgejo and GitHub automation, unit tests, and recorded play-throughs used to catch regressions.

**CI on both forges.** `build.yml` takes its image from the repository variable `CI_IMAGE` (the registry image by digest on forge.schkerke.com, a copy loaded into the runner on forgejo.brazencrows.com); registry credentials fall back to 'none'; the checkout is a plain git fetch of every branch and tag from the same forge instead of `actions/checkout`; build and reproduce skip pull requests from forks. d7abdb5. Three follow-up edits made in Forgejo's web editor remove YAML anchors (Forgejo splits the YAML when parsing jobs, so anchors do not work) and correct the syntax afterwards. 96a0746, 6f89499, c193a47.

**The release step reaches Forgejo by the event's repository URL.** `release.sh` built its API URL from `GITHUB_SERVER_URL`. forgejo-runner sets that to its own connection, which on forgejo.brazencrows.com is Forgejo's loopback address (`http://127.0.0.1:3000/`), and a job cannot reach that, so the Release step failed to connect. The step now gives `release.sh` the event's `repository.url` in `REPO_API`, as Curse and Secret do; the checkout already uses the event's `clone_url` the same way. #843.

**The manual run shows its tag field.** Forgejo's Run workflow form showed `invalid input type ""` instead of the tag, because the `workflow_dispatch` input gave no `type:` (GitHub assumes `string`; Forgejo does not). The input is now `type: string`. #845.

**A release by hand of an older tag builds, and names the tag.** The release job builds a dispatched tag's `src/` and `build/` with today's `build.sh`, which ran `build/buildid.sh`; tags from before the native build have no such file, so `obj/BUILDID.INC` was never written and the game did not compile (#848). The script is now `tools/buildid.sh`, outside the directories the job takes from the tag. The job also left HEAD at the dispatching commit, so the build ID would have named main's version and commit, marked MODIFIED; the script now describes `BUILD_REF` (HEAD unless set) and compares `src/` and `build/` with it, and the dispatched release sets `BUILD_REF` to the tag (#849).

**Build ID.** `build.sh` writes `obj/BUILDID.INC` (version and commit) from git; sources that do not include it, such as SSI's tags, build the same bytes. efccdbf.

**Unit tests.** TestKit and `build.sh --test` (ad2cadc, b88a6d7, 9945bc4); the test programs added in the range are TStats, TAreas, TStrUtil, TFileUtl, TClasCal, TDataPat, TAbilAdj, TMoneyCa, TRecLay, TSaveLay, TRandItm, TCharHlp, TPathing, TJrnl, TDataArc, TEclTrc, TEclVars, TClock, TUsualSp and TVidPic. Many later fixes add checks to them (for example TClasCal for #616 and the Wisdom totals, TCharHlp for `TheftChance` and `AtLevelCap`, TEclVars for the string value's 0, TPathing for `RouteStep`).

**Regression scenarios.** `tests/regress.sh` with goldbox-player's `tools/regress.py`: boot, chargen, loadsave, combat, vault, journal and camp (a15842b); shop and areachange (a19b0ce); ssiformat against SSI's own v1.3 (efa574b); overlay-load checkpoints (aed1509); `VIDEO=cga`, `tandy` and `vga` modes (bd7a01e, 6127810, 2a8f97c); `MEMSIZE=N` for EMS (3d10dc2); picture-memory checkpoints (e0b713b). A wilderness scenario loads save G (the owner's party in the central wilderness), takes three steps and records overlay loads, heap and picture memory, passing in all four modes at 1 and 16 MB (f9db514); wildfight walks 32 steps under NORAND to a random encounter and records the fight's start and memory (VGA has 199,312 bytes free there) (abbd4a6). Scenarios whose screens change by design record the changed screens apart (078b01f, d184cc3), and the boot scenario strips the build's version, commit, free memory and data version (ee892e2, b88a6d7, e747259). `stojanow`, `bane` and `banetemple` (#683, #682) start from save A with its area, position and quest cells poked into place (`tests/scenarios/saves/stojanow`, `bane`, `banetemple`); a build from before data version 34 shows SSI's screens there instead.

**Benchmark.** `bench/vidbench.sh` (bd7a01e).

**Overlay and size tools.** `build/ovlsizes.py` (and `--units`), `tests/ovlloads.py`.

---

**build.sh runs DOSBox-X headless.** `run_dosbox` unsets the display variables, uses SDL's dummy drivers, closes stdin, stops a stuck run with SIGKILL (a SIGTERM raised a quit dialog on the desktop) and caps each file at 2 GB, as Secret does (secret#528). #775.

**Tests start with no creature list.** `FxEngine.EffectSource` walks `GDVars.creatureList`, and since #801 does on every attack roll. TAtkRoll and TDisplac never set it, and Turbo Pascal does not clear globals, so after other programs had run in the same DOSBox-X session TAtkRoll followed a stale pointer and hung, losing every later program. Both set it to nil. #826.

**The scenarios follow today's menus, against a golden build of the port.** The golden build is now a recent commit of the port, not the pre-refactor one, so the `golden:`/`candidate:` splits for keys the old build wanted are gone (they stay in `ssiformat`, against SSI's v1.3) and every checkpoint is compared. Saving returns to the camp menu (#872), so `loadsave` and `bane` wait for it instead of a Quit question; `companion` reaches Companion Tools by its place in the Settings list (Down 16 times; Up wrapped to the combat log's lines); `journal` lets the preview pane draw before its first checkpoint and selects the second item for the entry; the save popup is waited for before it is recorded; `loadsave` records the `.MAP` with the save's files; `ssiformat` waits for the camp menu after the save and leaves out `CHARLIST.TXT`, whose lines differ from SSI's since a party member stays on the roster (#754). `tests/regress.sh` with no scenario named leaves out `ssiformat`; the README gives the commands that record a golden build's results for every mode. #898.

**`VIDEO=cga`, `tandy` and `vga` stage their config as POOL.CFG.** goldbox-player stages `--config` under its own file name, and its staging check refuses a name the game does not ship, so `VIDEO=cga tests/regress.sh ...` stopped before either build booted (POOL-CGA.CFG "is not a file por-v13 ships"), and even allowed, the game would have read the EGA POOL.CFG beside it. `regress.sh` now copies the mode's config to `obj/regress-<mode>/POOL.CFG` and passes that, as `SOUND=` already did for its rewritten config (#1162); with both set, the folder is `obj/regress-<mode>-<sound>`. Checked with `VIDEO=cga` and the advisory scenario: both builds boot, and the candidate's System page reads Video CGA. #1182.
**TDrain leaves no .chx behind (#1221).** After the native tests the folder held `TDR.CHX`, 132 bytes: the version 5 file TDrain's first `CharExt.WriteExt` wrote through FileUtil's set. TDrain wrote its version 2 file with `Assign` and `Rewrite` as `TDR.chx`, which the native build's case-sensitive folder kept as a second file, and the reads and the last save's erase found that one, leaving the first. It writes the old file through `FileUtil.WriteToSet`, as TWeapSet does, and checks that `FileUtil.Exists` finds no `.chx` after the erase; the folder is left empty of it.
**What the demo leaves in memory (#1226).** `demoabort` said the System page's free memory after the demo was what it had been; it is about 11.5 KB lower (254,448 to 242,016 after a walk ended by a key, 241,888 after a battle, EGA, 1 MB), and stays there over further runs, the largest block unchanged. A build that logged `MemAvail` and `Blit.picBytes` after each script command showed what it is, none of it a leak: pictures kept until something replaces them, Podol Plaza's wall sets (LOAD PIECES, 8,481 bytes) and, after a walk, the title portrait (3,926), and after a battle the statistics blocks of the demo's four members (`Stats.NewEntry`, 1,016 bytes each), which `Stats` keeps for whoever is in those places next, as a first fight would make them. Save A loaded after the demo has the same pictures as without it. The scenario's comment says so now.
**`trainanywhere` checks Gins's refusal without a race (#1227).** Its `wait RESTORATION|\(BC\)` could match the hall's own line, which ends in (BC), before the refusal showed, and a wait on RESTORATION alone missed the two-second flash (`TextRend.FlashBottomLine`) in one run of six. The scenario now presses Z after U: it ends the flash at once (FlashBottomLine takes the key), or, landing after it, does nothing on the hall's menu, so the `gins-drained` checkpoint is always the hall's line back with no Dual Class screen. Four runs in a row gave the same text at every checkpoint.
**The scenarios pass against the 8b5fe54 golden build, and `nodemo` runs without the demo (#1233).** Recording goldens from 8b5fe54 and running them against themselves failed seven scenarios, none of them the game. `widecombat`, `skullcrusher`, `skulldead` and `altshop` still kept `candidate:` steps for a golden build without Wide View, #1168's carting away or #1195's Alt keys, so the golden side skipped the keys its own screens wanted (in `altshop` it took the offer's checkpoint without waiting for IS IT A DEAL); the steps are everyone's now. `companion` counted seven Downs to Companion Tools for the golden build, which has it eleventh like the candidate: one count, ten. `demoabort` ignores the System page's SPEED CHECK and CYCLES lines, measured each run (29,800 against 29,900 in VGA), as `advisory` does. `nodemo` played the demo because `regress.sh` passed `--stage DEMO.DAX` to the whole run whenever a `demo*` scenario was in it, and goldbox-player's `regress.py` stages a `--stage` file for every scenario it runs: in a full run both builds had the demo, and which of them was in it at a checkpoint was a matter of timing. It also put the stage into every other scenario's record key, so a single scenario's run never reused a full run's golden result. `regress.sh` now runs the `demo*` scenarios apart from the rest, their report in `obj/regress-demo.json`. Each scenario passed twice golden against golden in EGA, and `nodemo` in CGA and VGA and `demoabort` in VGA.

## 12. Documentation

Finally, these are the changes that explain how the project is built, tested, maintained, and extended.

**`docs/refactor-plan.md` tracked.** `docs/` had been ignored as the disassembly project's notes; it now holds the repository's own, starting with the refactor plan (ground rules, constraints, phases 0 to 4). 21771b7. The plan is updated as each phase lands: 13888a9, 0f11ab8, 2ab71e9, c03cc49, ace1784, 34581d4, c73a1be (phases 1 to 4); 39ac97d, 98f7cb8, 4084078, 1826ed1, d307eb5, 6dd9ce1 (phase 5); 8f60a9f, de5c623 (phase 6, proposed from two surveys and its status). Code commits also mark their items done.

**README.** Rewritten in the owner's batch: a short description, a note that each version (v1.1, v1.2 and v1.3) reproduces its release byte for byte, an AI usage section, build requirements (Turbo Pascal 5.0 for v1.1 and v1.2, Turbo Pascal 6.0 for v1.3, DOSBox or DOSBox-X), and how the per-disk data files are aggregated into merged files at start-up (about 49 seconds at 3,000 cycles, logged to DATABLD.LOG). 038f32e. The Tests section was added in ad2cadc, and the regression README lists goldbox-player's newer steps (efa574b).

**Comments.** The comment fixes from the source review (Money, Intro, C_Keys, CharGen, Camp, GDVars) are in section 9.

**The merged archives have one model.** C_DATA's header and DataArc's family table said goldbox-re-tools' mergedax held the same values, but that model went with its branch and nothing outside this repository reads the merged archives. They now name DataBld and DataPat, with DataArc's table, as the only source. #264 (PR #687). 882a0f1.

**The journal units are hand-maintained.** C_Jrnl, JrnlList and Journal1 to Journal4 were first written by goldbox-toolkit's `tools/por-journal-gen.py` (ddcdadc), and have been edited by hand since (54ba386, f04e389). The owner's ruling: the generator is kept only as a one-time bootstrap, and no Python script is part of the build path. Each unit's header now says so; edit the units directly. JrnlView was never generated. In goldbox-toolkit the generator's doc says the same, and that its `--check` reports differences by design. #299 (PR #687). 4496b3d.
